For $u, v : V \to \C$, $h, x \in V$ and $r \in \N$,
\[
  \Delta_h^r (u v)(x) = \sum_{l=0}^r \binom{r}{l} \, \Delta_h^l u(x) \, \Delta_h^{r-l} v(x + l h) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induction on $r$, from $\Delta_h(u v)(x) = \Delta_h u(x) \, v(x + h) + u(x) \, \Delta_h v(x)$ and
Pascal's rule.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The modulus of a product]
\label{lem:product-modulus}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_higherDiff_mul_le, Rd.modulus_mul_smooth_le, ENNReal.ofReal_one_div_two_pow_pow,
  Rd.memLp_mul_of_bound, Rd.mulLp, Rd.mulLp_apply_ae, Rd.mulLp_sub, Rd.enorm_mulLp_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:smooth-differences, lem:leibniz-differences, lem:rd-differences}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $r \in \N$, $C \geq 0$ and $\psi : V \to \C$ smooth with
$\|D^l \psi\| \leq C$ for $l \leq r$. For $F \in L_p$, $\psi F \in L_p$ with
$\|\psi F\|_p \leq C \|F\|_p$, and for $t \geq 0$,
\[
  \omega_r(\psi F, t)_p \leq C \sum_{l=0}^r \binom{r}{l} \, t^l \, \omega_{r-l}(F, t)_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:leibniz-differences} and \Cref{lem:smooth-differences},
\[
  |\Delta_h^r(\psi F)(x)| \leq C \sum_l \binom{r}{l} \|h\|^l \, |\Delta_h^{r-l} F(x + l h)| ,
\]
and translations preserve $L_p$ norms.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moduli of lower order]
\label{lem:lower-order-moduli}
\lean{Rd.lqNorm_lpModulus_sub_le, Rd.multiplierConst, Rd.multiplierConst_ne_top,
  Rd.lqNorm_lpModulus_le_multiplierConst}
\leanok
\uses{thm:rd-besov, def:modulus-data, thm:modulus-coincidence}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $0 \leq l \leq r$. There is
$K_l < \infty$ with
\[
  \bigl\| \bigl( 2^{k(s-l)} \omega_{r-l}(F, 2^{-k})_p \bigr)_k \bigr\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq K_l \bigl( \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\]
for every $F \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $1 \leq l \leq s$, both sides are equivalent to the norms of the band-limited approximation
spaces $A^{s-l}_q$ and $A^s_q$ (\Cref{thm:rd-besov} with the orders $r - l$ and $r$), and
$A^s_q \subseteq A^{s-l}_q$. For $l > s$, $\omega_{r-l}(F, t)_p \leq 2^{r-l} \|F\|_p$ and
$\sum_k 2^{k(s-l)q} < \infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise multipliers]
\label{thm:multipliers}
\lean{Rd.modNorm_mulLp_le, Rd.multiplierTotal_ne_top, Rd.modNorm_mulLp_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{lem:product-modulus, lem:lower-order-moduli}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $\psi : V \to \C$ smooth with bounded
derivatives up to order $r$. There is $K < \infty$ with
\[
  \|\psi F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(\psi F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq K \bigl( \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\]
for every $F \in L_p$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:product-modulus}, $2^{ks} \omega_r(\psi F, 2^{-k})_p \leq C \sum_l \binom{r}{l} \,
2^{k(s-l)} \omega_{r-l}(F, 2^{-k})_p$; Minkowski's inequality and \Cref{lem:lower-order-moduli}
bound the $\ell_q$ norm.
\end{proof}

\section{Local extensions}

\begin{lemma}[The support principle for two sets]
\label{lem:support-principle-two}
\lean{Rd.conv_indicator_eq_of_forall, Rd.indicatorLp_convLp_indicatorLp}
\leanok
\uses{lem:conv-op, lem:indicator-lp}
Let $k \in L_1$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $U, W \subseteq V$ measurable with $x - y \in W$ whenever
$x \in U$ and $k(y) \neq 0$. Then $1_U (k * (1_W h)) = 1_U (k * h)$ for every $h \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $x \in U$, $k(y) (1_W h)(x - y) = k(y) h(x - y)$ for every $y$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bumps of every radius]
\label{lem:bumps-every-radius}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.rescale, Rd.LocalMeansBump.rescale_radius,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.scaled_eq_zero, Rd.LocalMeansBump.dual_eq_zero,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.kernel_eq_zero, Rd.centeredBumpBase, Rd.LocalMeansBump.centered,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.centered_radius, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_momentKernel_of,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_scaled_of, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_dual_of,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coneSupported_rescale}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, def:cone-supported, lem:dilate}
If $g$ is a bump for local means of radius $R$ (\Cref{def:local-means}) and $c > 0$, then $g_c$ is
one of radius $R/c$; for every $R > 0$ the normalised smooth bump around $0$ is one of radius $R$.
The kernels $m_{2^j}$, $k_j$ and $\eta_j$ of a bump of radius $R$ vanish outside the ball of radius
$L R$, and they are supported in $-K$ if $g$ is.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\int g_c = \int g$ (\Cref{lem:dilate}); the kernels are linear combinations of dilations
$g_{c'}$ with $c' \geq 1/L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Patches]
\label{lem:patches}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.patch_support, Rd.LipschitzGraph.patch_step,
  Rd.interior_patch_support, Rd.interior_patch_step, Rd.measurableSet_infDist_gt}
\leanok
\uses{lem:graph-cone, lem:bumps-every-radius, def:rd-modulus-on}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R$, $L \in \N$ and $\varepsilon, \delta, \rho > 0$.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item If $\Omega$ is a Lipschitz graph domain, $g$ is supported in $-K$ and $L R \leq \varepsilon$,
    then for $x \in \Omega \cap B(c, \rho - 2\varepsilon)$ and $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$,
    $x - y \in \Omega \cap B(c, \rho - \varepsilon)$, and the steps $h = -c'^{-1} z$, $c' \geq 1$,
    $g(z) \neq 0$, satisfy $\Omega \cap B(c, \rho - \varepsilon) \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for
    $T = \Omega \cap B(c, \rho)$.
  \item If $O \subseteq V$ and $L R \leq \delta$, then for $\operatorname{dist}(x, O^c) > 2\delta$ and
    $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$, $\operatorname{dist}(x - y, O^c) > \delta$, and the same steps satisfy
    $\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, O^c) > \delta\} \subseteq O_{Lh}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The supports of the kernels lie in the ball of radius $L R$ and, in (1), in $-K$; steps in the
cone stay in $\Omega$ (\Cref{lem:graph-cone}), and points within distance $L R$ stay in the larger
balls.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local means on a set]
\label{lem:local-means-on-sets}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.localNormOn, Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_sub_convLp_scaled_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_localMean_zero_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_indicatorLp_localMean_succ_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.localNormOn_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:moment-approx, def:local-means, lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R \leq 1/2$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, and let $W, T \subseteq V$ with $W$
measurable and $W \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for the steps $h = -c^{-1} z$, $c \geq 1$, $g(z) \neq 0$. For
$f \in L_p$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$,
\[
  \|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(W)})_j\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq (\|m\|_1 + 2 \|g\|_1) \bigl( \|f\|_p + \|(2^{js} \omega_L(f, 2^{-j}, T)_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}
  \bigr) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
As in \Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper}, with \Cref{thm:moment-approx} for the two sets $W$ and $T$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Local extensions]
\label{thm:local-extension}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.localExtTerm, Rd.LocalMeansBump.localExtension,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.summable_localExtTerm, Rd.LocalMeansBump.tendsto_sum_localExtTerm,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.modNorm_localExtension_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.indicatorLp_localExtension,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_local_extension}
\leanok
\uses{lem:support-principle-two, lem:local-means-on-sets, lem:reproducing-formula,
  lem:local-means-synthesis}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R \leq 1/2$, $L \geq 1$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < L$ and
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$. Let $U \subseteq W$ and $U \subseteq T$ be measurable with $x - y \in W$
for $x \in U$ and $\eta_j(y) \neq 0$, and with $W \subseteq T_{Lh}$ for the steps
$h = -c^{-1} z$, $c \geq 1$, $g(z) \neq 0$. If
$\|f\|_{L_p(T)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, T)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, then there is
$F \in L_p$ with $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ and $F = f$
almost everywhere on $U$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Take $F = \sum_j \eta_j * 1_W (k_j * 1_T f)$, which converges by \Cref{lem:local-means-on-sets}
and has the bound of \Cref{lem:local-means-synthesis}. On $U$, \Cref{lem:support-principle-two}
removes the cut-off $1_W$, and the reproducing formula (\Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}) gives
$1_T f = f$.
\end{proof}

The extension operator of \Cref{thm:graph-extension} is the case $U = W = T = \Omega$.

\section{Bounded Lipschitz domains}

\begin{definition}[Bounded Lipschitz domains]
\label{def:bounded-lipschitz}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.inter_ball_eq,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.measurableSet, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.isCompact_closure,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.isCompact_frontier}
\leanok
\uses{def:lipschitz-graph}
A bounded Lipschitz domain is a bounded open set $\Omega \subseteq V$ with finitely many open balls
$B_j = B(c_j, \rho_j)$ with $c_j \in \partial\Omega$, $\partial \Omega \subseteq \bigcup_j B_j$, and
Lipschitz graph domains $\Omega_j$ with $B_j \cap \Omega = B_j \cap \Omega_j$.
\end{definition}

This is the definition of Stein and Triebel, as in \cite[Remark 3.2]{SchneiderVybiral}; the
direction $e_j$ of each graph domain replaces the rotations.

\begin{lemma}[Shrinking and the inner margin]
\label{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_shrink, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_interior_margin}
\leanok
\uses{def:bounded-lipschitz}
There is $\varepsilon > 0$ with $\partial\Omega \subseteq \bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$.
If $\Omega \neq V$, there is $\delta > 0$ with $\operatorname{dist}(x, \Omega^c) > 2\delta$ for every
$x \in \Omega$ outside these balls.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Both by compactness: $\partial\Omega$ is covered by the increasing open sets
$\bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 1/(n+1))$, and $\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c)$ attains a positive
minimum on the compact set $\overline{\Omega} \setminus \bigcup_j B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)
\subseteq \Omega$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Resolutions of unity]
\label{lem:resolution-of-unity}
\lean{Rd.exists_smooth_partition, Rd.exists_iteratedFDeriv_bound}
\leanok
For a closed set $S \subseteq V$ and a finite open cover $(U_i)$ of $S$ there are smooth
$\psi_i \geq 0$ with $\operatorname{supp} \psi_i \subseteq U_i$ and $\sum_i \psi_i = 1$ on $S$.
Smooth functions with compact support have bounded derivatives of every order.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Mathlib's smooth partitions of unity on manifolds, for the model space $V$; the derivatives are
continuous with compact support.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pasting local extensions]
\label{lem:pasting}
\lean{Rd.lpModulus_finsetSum_le, Rd.modNorm_finsetSum_lt_top, Rd.exists_pasting}
\leanok
\uses{lem:resolution-of-unity, thm:multipliers}
Let $O \subseteq V$ be measurable, $(U_i)$ a finite cover of $\overline O$ by bounded open sets, and
$F_i \in L_p$ with $\|F_i\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F_i, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ and
$F_i = f$ almost everywhere on $O \cap U_i$, where $s < r$. Then $f$ agrees on $O$ with some $F$ with
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$F = \sum_i \psi_i F_i$ for a resolution of unity subordinate to $(U_i)$
(\Cref{lem:resolution-of-unity}): each $\psi_i F_i$ has a finite norm by \Cref{thm:multipliers},
and $\psi_i F_i = \psi_i f$ on $O$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Restrictions and smaller sets]
\label{lem:restriction-moduli}
\lean{Rd.modNormOn_le_modNorm_of_indicatorLp, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.modNormOn_mono_set}
\leanok
\uses{lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $O \subseteq V$ be measurable. If $F = f$ almost everywhere on $O$, then
$\|f\|_{L_p(O)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, O)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq
\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$, and the left side decreases when $O$ is
replaced by a measurable subset.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The patches of a bounded Lipschitz domain]
\label{lem:bounded-patches}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_local_extension_boundary,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_local_extension_interior}
\leanok
\uses{thm:local-extension, lem:patches, lem:bumps-every-radius, lem:restriction-moduli}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $\varepsilon, \delta > 0$, and
$f \in L_p$ with $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
For every $j$, $f$ agrees on $\Omega \cap B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$, and on
$\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c) > 2\delta\}$, with functions with finite Besov quasi-norms
on $V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:local-extension} with the patches of \Cref{lem:patches}: for the boundary balls the cone
bump of $\Omega_j$ rescaled to radius $\leq \varepsilon / r$, inside a centred bump of radius
$\leq \delta / r$; the moduli on the smaller sets are bounded by those on $\Omega$
(\Cref{lem:restriction-moduli}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Besov spaces on bounded Lipschitz domains by intrinsic differences]
\label{thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_besov_extension_of_modulusOn,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_besov_extension_iff_modulusOn,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_memApprox_extension_iff}
\leanok
\uses{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry, lem:bounded-patches, lem:pasting, lem:restriction-moduli,
  thm:rd-besov}
Let $\Omega$ be a bounded Lipschitz domain, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$,
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $f \in L_p$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F \in L_p$ with $F = f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$ and
    $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$;
  \item there is $F$ in the approximation space $A^s_q$ of the band-limited scheme with $F = f$
    almost everywhere on $\Omega$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
(2) gives (1) by \Cref{lem:restriction-moduli}. For the converse, if $\Omega = V$ take $F = f$.
Otherwise \Cref{lem:bounded-lipschitz-geometry} gives $\varepsilon$ and $\delta$, the balls
$B(c_j, \rho_j - 2\varepsilon)$ and the set $\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot, \Omega^c) > 2\delta\}$
cover $\overline\Omega$, \Cref{lem:bounded-patches} gives the local extensions, and
\Cref{lem:pasting} pastes them. (2) and (3) agree by \Cref{thm:rd-besov}.
\end{proof}

\section{Atoms}

Atoms are functions of small support with size bounds at a dyadic scale. As in Schneider and
Vyb\'{\i}ral~\cite{SchneiderVybiral}, they need no moment conditions and no smoothness beyond the
order of the differences that measure them.

\begin{definition}[Dyadic cells]
\label{def:dyadic-cells}
\lean{Rd.gridBasis, Rd.cellIndex, Rd.cell, Rd.cellCenter, Rd.mem_cell_cellIndex,
  Rd.repr_cellCenter, Rd.measurable_cellIndex, Rd.measurableSet_cell}
\leanok
Let $(b_i)$ be an orthonormal basis of $V$. For $j \in \N$ and $m \in \Z^n$ the dyadic cell of
level $j$ and index $m$ is
$P_{j,m} = \{x \in V : \lfloor 2^j \langle b_i, x \rangle \rfloor = m_i \text{ for all } i\}$,
with centre $c_{j,m} = \sum_i (m_i + 1/2) 2^{-j} b_i$. Every $x$ lies in the cell of index
$(\lfloor 2^j \langle b_i, x \rangle \rfloor)_i$, and the cells are measurable.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cells and centres]
\label{lem:dyadic-cells}
\lean{Rd.abs_repr_le_norm, Rd.abs_repr_sub_cellCenter_le, Rd.norm_sub_cellCenter_le,
  Rd.card_le_of_norm_cellCenter_sub_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells}
For $x \in P_{j,m}$ every coordinate of $x - c_{j,m}$ is at most $2^{-j}/2$ in modulus, and
$\|x - c_{j,m}\| \leq n 2^{-j}$. For $\rho \geq 0$ at most $(2\rho + 2)^n$ centres of level $j$ lie
in a closed ball of radius $\rho 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The coordinates of the centres in such a ball lie in intervals of length $2 \rho 2^{-j}$, and such
an interval contains at most $2\rho + 2$ points of $(\Z + 1/2) 2^{-j}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded overlap]
\label{lem:bounded-overlap}
\lean{Rd.exists_finset_of_card_le, Rd.enorm_tsum_rpow_le_of_overlap, Rd.eLpNorm_rpow_le_of_overlap}
\leanok
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$ and let $g_m$, $m$ in a countable set, be measurable functions such that at
every point at most $N$ of them are nonzero. If $F = \sum_m g_m$ almost everywhere, then
$\|F\|_p^p \leq N^{p-1} \sum_m \|g_m\|_p^p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At every point the sum has at most $N$ nonzero terms, and
$|\sum_{m \in T} a_m|^p \leq |T|^{p-1} \sum_{m \in T} |a_m|^p$ by H\"older's inequality. Integrate
and exchange the sum with the integral.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Regular atoms and the coefficient quasi-norm]
\label{def:regular-atoms}
\lean{Rd.IsRegularAtomOn, Rd.IsRegularAtom, Rd.levelNorm, Rd.levelNorm_eq_tsum, Rd.levelNorm_mono,
  Rd.coeffNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $\sigma \geq 0$, $r \in \N$, $d, C \geq 0$ and $\Omega \subseteq V$. A
regular atom on $\Omega$ of level $j$, exponent $\sigma$ and order $r$ with centre $c$ is a
measurable $a : V \to \C$ with $a = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$,
\begin{gather*}
  \|a\|_{L_p(\Omega)} \leq C 2^{-jn/p}, \
  \|\Delta_h^r a\|_{L_p(\Omega_{rh})} \leq C 2^{-jn/p} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^\sigma) \quad (h \in V).
\end{gather*}
A regular atom is a regular atom on $V$.
For coefficients $\lambda = (\lambda_{j,m})$ let
$\|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} = (\sum_m |\lambda_{j,m}|^p)^{1/p}$ and
$\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p})_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

The Lip atoms of \cite[Definition 2.1]{SchneiderVybiral}, with $|a| \leq 1$ and
$|a(x) - a(y)| \leq 2^j |x - y|$, are regular atoms with $\sigma = r = 1$, and their smooth atoms of
\cite[Definition 1.3]{SchneiderVybiral} are regular atoms with $\sigma = r = K$
(\Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms}).

\begin{lemma}[Level sums]
\label{lem:level-sums}
\lean{Rd.higherDiff_tsum_apply, Rd.le_of_rpow_le_overlap, Rd.one_sub_one_div_toReal_nonneg,
  Rd.summable_levelSum, Rd.eLpNorm_levelSum_le, Rd.card_le_of_higherDiff_ne_zero,
  Rd.modulus_levelSum_le, Rd.eLpNorm_indicator_levelSum_le, Rd.modulusOn_levelSum_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:bounded-overlap, def:regular-atoms, lem:rd-modulus, lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, and let $a_m$ be regular atoms of level $j$ with centres $c_m$ and
coefficients $\lambda_m$, such that at most $N$ of the centres $c_m$ with $\lambda_m \neq 0$ lie in a
ball of radius $(d + r) 2^{-j}$. Then $f = \sum_m \lambda_m a_m$ converges at every point, and
\[
  \|f\|_p \leq N^{1-1/p} C 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p}, \qquad
  \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p \leq 2^r N^{1-1/p} C 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p} c_{kj},
\]
with $c_{kj} = 2^{-(k-j)\sigma}$ for $j \leq k$ and $c_{kj} = 1$ for $j > k$. For regular atoms
on a measurable set $\Omega$ the same bounds hold for $\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)}$ and
$\omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At every point at most $N$ of the terms are nonzero, and \Cref{lem:bounded-overlap} applies. For
$j \leq k$ and $\|h\| \leq 2^{-k}$ the differences $\Delta_h^r a_m$ vanish outside
$B(c_m, (d + r) 2^{-j})$, and \Cref{lem:bounded-overlap} applies to
$\Delta_h^r f = \sum_m \lambda_m \Delta_h^r a_m$ with $(2^j \|h\|)^\sigma \leq 2^{-(k-j)\sigma}$; for
$j > k$, $\omega_r(f, t)_p \leq 2^r \|f\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic synthesis]
\label{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}
\lean{Rd.atomSynthConst, Rd.atomSynthConst_ne_top, ENNReal.enorm_le_of_tendsto_sum,
  Rd.modNorm_le_coeffNorm, Rd.eLpNorm_indicator_le_tsum, Rd.lpModulusOn_le_tsum,
  Rd.modNormOn_le_coeffNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:level-sums, lem:almost-diagonal, lem:rd-modulus-on}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $0 < s < \sigma$. Let $a_{j,m}$ be regular atoms
of level $j$ such that the centres of the atoms with $\lambda_{j,m} \neq 0$ satisfy the overlap
condition of \Cref{lem:level-sums} at every level, and let $F = \sum_j f_j$ in $L_p$ with
$f_j = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere. Then
\[
  \|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq K \|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}},
\]
with $K = N^{1-1/p} C \bigl((1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} + 2^r ((1 - 2^{-(\sigma - s)})^{-1} +
(1 - 2^{-s})^{-1})\bigr)$. For regular atoms on a measurable set $\Omega$,
\[
  \|F\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq
  K \|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 2.6 and Corollary 2.8, direction $\Leftarrow$]{SchneiderVybiral} for
$p \geq 1$. The norm and the moduli are subadditive and bounded on $L_p$, so they are bounded by the
sums over the levels. With \Cref{lem:level-sums} and $b_j = 2^{-jn/p} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p}$,
the almost diagonal lemma (\Cref{lem:almost-diagonal}) with exponent $\sigma$ sums the levels,
since $2^{js} b_j = 2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Synthesis of one level]
\label{lem:single-level}
\lean{Rd.lqNorm_ite_eq, Rd.isRegularAtomOn_zero, Rd.modNorm_level_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}
For regular atoms of level $j$ with the overlap of \Cref{lem:level-sums}, $F = \sum_m \lambda_m a_m$
in $L_p$ and $0 < s < \sigma$,
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq K 2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda\|_{\ell_p}$,
with the constant $K$ of \Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis} with the coefficients and atoms of the other levels equal to $0$; the
zero function is a regular atom, and a sequence with one nonzero term $x$ has $\ell_q$-norm $x$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Atoms from pointwise bounds]
\label{lem:pointwise-atoms}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_le_of_bound_of_support, Rd.volume_closedBall_div_two_pow,
  Rd.rpow_mul_dyadicWeight_neg, Rd.isRegularAtomOn_of_pointwise, Rd.isRegularAtom_of_pointwise,
  Rd.enorm_higherDiff_le_of_iteratedFDeriv}
\leanok
\uses{def:regular-atoms, lem:smooth-differences}
Let $a$ be measurable with $a = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$, $|a| \leq A$ and
$|\Delta_h^r a| \leq A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^\sigma)$. Then $a$ is a regular atom with the constant
$A ((r + 1) d^n |B_1|)^{1/p}$; on a measurable set $\Omega$ the bounds are needed only on $\Omega$
and on $\Omega_{rh}$. A smooth $a$ with $\|D^l a\| \leq A 2^{jl}$ for $l \leq r$ has
$|\Delta_h^r a| \leq 2^r A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^r)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
A function with $|g| \leq B$ that vanishes outside $S$ has $\|g\|_p \leq B |S|^{1/p}$. The
difference $\Delta_h^r a$ vanishes outside $r + 1$ translates of the ball, and
$|\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})| = d^n |B_1| 2^{-jn}$. For smooth $a$, \Cref{lem:smooth-differences}
gives $|\Delta_h^r a| \leq A (2^j \|h\|)^r$, and $|\Delta_h^r a| \leq 2^r \sup |a|$.
\end{proof}

\section{Atomic decompositions}

\begin{lemma}[The cells partition the space]
\label{lem:cells-partition}
\lean{Rd.cell_disjoint, Rd.iUnion_cell, Rd.lintegral_eq_tsum_cell, Rd.eLpNorm_rpow_eq_tsum_cell,
  Rd.card_le_of_norm_sub_cellCenter_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells, lem:dyadic-cells}
The cells of one level are disjoint and cover $V$. Hence $\int F = \sum_m \int_{P_{j,m}} F$ for
$F \geq 0$ and $\|g\|_p^p = \sum_m \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p^p$. At most
$\lceil (2\rho + 2)^n \rceil$ centres of level $j$ lie in a ball of radius $\rho 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Countable additivity of the integral, and \Cref{lem:dyadic-cells}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise bounds of convolutions]
\label{lem:conv-pointwise}
\lean{Rd.lintegral_closedBall_le, Rd.lintegral_kernel_mul_le, Rd.enorm_conv_le_of_bound,
  Rd.integrable_translate_mul_of_locallyIntegrable, Rd.conv_add_apply, Rd.higherDiff_conv_apply,
  Rd.enorm_higherDiff_le_of_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $g \in L_p$, and $|K| \leq A$ with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho)$.
Then $\int_{\overline{B}(x, \rho)} |g| \leq |\overline{B}(x, \rho)|^{1 - 1/p} \|g\|_p$ and
\[
  |(K * g)(x)| \leq A |\overline{B}(0, \rho)|^{1-1/p} \|g\|_p \quad (x \in V).
\]
For a continuous $K$ with compact support the integrals $\int K(y + v) g(x - y) \, dy$ converge
absolutely, they equal $(K * g)(x + v)$, and $\Delta_h^r (K * g) = (\Delta_h^r K) * g$ at every point.
If $|f| \leq B$, then $|\Delta_h^r f| \leq 2^r B$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
H\"older's inequality on the ball, and the translation invariance of the Lebesgue measure; the
differences are finite sums of translates.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Cell atoms]
\label{def:cell-atoms}
\lean{Rd.cellCoef, Rd.cellAtom, Rd.enorm_cellCoef, Rd.cellCoef_mul_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells}
For $1 \leq p < \infty$, a kernel $K$ and $g \in L_p$, the coefficients of the cells of level $j$
are $\lambda_{j,m} = 2^{jn/p} \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p$, and the cell atoms are
$a_{j,m} = \lambda_{j,m}^{-1} K * (1_{P_{j,m}} g)$, with $a_{j,m} = 0$ if $\lambda_{j,m} = 0$.
Then $\lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m} = K * (1_{P_{j,m}} g)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cell decomposition of a convolution]
\label{lem:cell-decomposition}
\lean{Rd.conv_eq_tsum_cellAtom, Rd.continuous_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{def:cell-atoms, lem:cells-partition, lem:conv-pointwise}
Let $K$ be continuous with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho)$, and $g \in L_p$. Then
$(K * g)(x) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}(x)$ at every $x \in V$, with finitely many nonzero terms,
and the cell atoms are continuous.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Only the finitely many cells that meet $\overline{B}(x, \rho)$ contribute, and on this ball their
indicators add up to $1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cell atoms are atoms]
\label{lem:cell-atoms-regular}
\lean{Rd.cellAtom_eq_zero, Rd.volume_closedBall_div_two_pow_rpow,
  Rd.enorm_inv_cellCoef_mul_conv_le, Rd.enorm_cellAtom_le, Rd.enorm_higherDiff_cellAtom_le,
  Rd.cellAtomConst, Rd.cellAtomConst_ne_top, Rd.isRegularAtom_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cell-decomposition, lem:conv-pointwise, lem:pointwise-atoms}
Let $K$ be continuous with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho 2^{-j})$ and
$|\Delta_h^l K| \leq A 2^{jn} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for $l \in \{0, r\}$. Then the cell atoms of
level $j$ vanish outside $\overline{B}(c_{j,m}, (\rho + n) 2^{-j})$,
\begin{gather*}
  |a_{j,m}| \leq A (\rho^n |B_1|)^{1 - 1/p}, \\
  |\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}| \leq 2^r A ((\rho + r)^n |B_1|)^{1 - 1/p} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^r),
\end{gather*}
so they are regular atoms of order and exponent $r$ with radius factor $\rho + n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:conv-pointwise} with $\lambda_{j,m} = 2^{jn/p} \|1_{P_{j,m}} g\|_p$: the factors
$2^{jn}$, $|\overline{B}(0, \rho 2^{-j})|^{1-1/p} = (\rho^n |B_1|)^{1-1/p} 2^{-jn(1-1/p)}$ and
$2^{-jn/p}$ cancel. For $\|h\| \leq 2^{-j}$ the kernel $\Delta_h^r K$ of $\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}$
vanishes outside $\overline{B}(0, (\rho + r) 2^{-j})$; for larger $h$,
$|\Delta_h^r a_{j,m}| \leq 2^r \sup |a_{j,m}|$. \Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The coefficients of the cells]
\label{lem:cell-coefficients}
\lean{Rd.levelNorm_cellCoef, Rd.coeffNorm_cellCoef}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cells-partition, def:cell-atoms}
$\|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} = 2^{jn/p} \|g\|_p$. Hence the cell coefficients of $g_j \in L_p$
have $\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{js} \|g_j\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cells-partition}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bounds of the dual kernels]
\label{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
\lean{ENNReal.ofReal_two_pow_pow, Rd.LocalMeansBump.scaled_eq_zero_of_lt,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.dual_eq_zero_of_lt, Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport_dual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.continuous_dual, Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_higherDiff_dual_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:smooth-differences, lem:resolution-of-unity, lem:rd-differences}
For a bump $g$ of radius $R$ and $L, r \in \N$, the kernels $m_{2^j}$ and $\eta_j$ are continuous
and vanish outside $\overline{B}(0, L R 2^{-j})$ and $\overline{B}(0, 2 L R 2^{-j})$, and there is
$A < \infty$ with $|\Delta_h^l \eta_j| \leq A 2^{jn} \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for all $l \leq r$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The moment kernel is smooth with compact support, so its derivatives are bounded
(\Cref{lem:resolution-of-unity}). \Cref{lem:smooth-differences} bounds its differences, and
$\Delta_h^l (\Phi_c) = (\Delta_{ch}^l \Phi)_c$ for the dilations (\Cref{lem:rd-differences}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The cell atoms of the dual kernels]
\label{lem:cell-atoms-dual}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_cellAtom_pointwise_bound}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cell-atoms-regular, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
For every order $r$ there is $A < \infty$ such that the cell atoms of $\eta_j * g$, $g \in L_p$,
satisfy $|\Delta_h^l a_{j,m}| \leq A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for all $l \leq r$, $j$ and $m$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular} with the bounds of \Cref{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}, for each $l \leq r$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic decomposition]
\label{thm:atomic-decomposition}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.atomic_decomposition}
\leanok
\uses{lem:reproducing-formula, lem:cell-decomposition, lem:cell-atoms-regular,
  lem:cell-coefficients, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $L, r \in \N$. There is $C < \infty$ such
that for every $F \in L_p$ the terms of the reproducing formula $F = \sum_j \eta_j * (k_j * F)$
(\Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}) are $\eta_j * (k_j * F) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost
everywhere, with the cell atoms of $k_j * F$; these are regular atoms of order and exponent $r$
with radius factor $2LR + n$ and constant $C$, and
$\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{js} \|k_j * F\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$ for all $s$ and $q$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:cell-decomposition} for the kernels $\eta_j$, whose bounds
(\Cref{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}) make the cell atoms regular (\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular}), and
\Cref{lem:cell-coefficients} for the coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic characterisation of Besov spaces]
\label{thm:atomic-characterisation}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.besov_iff_atomic}
\leanok
\uses{thm:atomic-decomposition, thm:rd-atomic-synthesis, thm:local-means, lem:cells-partition}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $L \geq 1$, $0 < s < L$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $F \in L_p$. Then
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and only if
$F = \sum_j f_j$ in $L_p$ with $f_j = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere, for regular
atoms of order and exponent $L$ whose centres satisfy the overlap condition of
\Cref{lem:level-sums}, and $\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 2.6]{SchneiderVybiral} for $p \geq 1$. If the norm is finite, so is the local
means quasi-norm (\Cref{thm:local-means}), and \Cref{thm:atomic-decomposition} with $r = L$ gives
the decomposition, with the overlap of \Cref{lem:cells-partition}. The converse is
\Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis} with $\sigma = r = L$.
\end{proof}

\section{Smooth atoms}

On a convex set $\Omega$ with nonempty interior, derivatives within $\Omega$ bound the differences
whose steps stay in $\Omega$. Smooth atoms on $\Omega$ are therefore regular atoms on $\Omega$, and
the cell atoms of the atomic decomposition are smooth atoms of every order. Smooth atoms keep their
form under smooth changes of variables, which is how Besov spaces move between charts.

\begin{lemma}[Differences through derivatives within a convex set]
\label{lem:convex-differences}
\lean{Rd.fwdDiff_iter_comp_ofReal, Rd.fwdDiff_iter_taylorWithinEval_eq_zero,
  Rd.norm_fwdDiff_iter_le_of_iteratedDerivWithin, Rd.norm_iteratedFDeriv_line_le,
  Rd.add_smul_mem_of_mem_stepSet, Rd.norm_higherDiff_le_of_iteratedFDerivWithin}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on, lem:rd-differences}
Let $\Omega \subseteq V$ be convex with nonempty interior, $r \geq 1$, and let $a$ be $r$ times
continuously differentiable within $\Omega$ with $\|D^i a(y)\| \leq C$ for $i \leq r$ and
$y \in \Omega$, the derivatives taken within $\Omega$. Then for $x \in \Omega_{rh}$
\[
  |\Delta_h^r a(x)| \leq 2^r r^{r+1} C \|h\|^r .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By convexity the segment $[x, x + rh]$ lies in $\Omega$. The function $g(t) = a(x + th)$ on
$[0, r]$ has $|g^{(r)}| \leq r!\, C \|h\|^r$ by the chain rule within $\Omega$ (Mathlib's
\texttt{norm\_iteratedFDerivWithin\_comp\_le}). The $r$-th difference of step $1$ annihilates the
Taylor polynomial of $g$ of degree $r - 1$ at $0$, and Taylor's theorem (Mathlib's
\texttt{taylor\_mean\_remainder\_bound}) bounds the remainder on $[0, r]$ by
$\sup |g^{(r)}|\, r^r / (r-1)!$. The binomial coefficients add up to $2^r$, and
$2^r r^r r! / (r - 1)! = 2^r r^{r+1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Smooth atoms]
\label{def:smooth-atoms}
\lean{Rd.IsSmoothAtom, Rd.isSmoothAtom_zero, Rd.IsSmoothAtom.of_univ}
\leanok
\uses{def:regular-atoms}
Let $\Omega \subseteq V$, $K \in \N$ and $d, A \geq 0$. A smooth atom on $\Omega$ of level $j$
and order $K$ with centre $c$ is a measurable $a : V \to \C$ with $a = 0$ outside
$\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$ that is $K$ times continuously differentiable within $\Omega$ with
\[
  \|D^l a(y)\| \leq A 2^{jl} \qquad (l \leq K,\ y \in \Omega).
\]
The function $0$ is a smooth atom, and a smooth atom on $V$ is a smooth atom on every set of
unique differentiability, where the derivatives within the set are the derivatives.
\end{definition}

These are the $K$-atoms of \cite[Definition 1.3]{SchneiderVybiral} on a set, without moment
conditions.

\begin{lemma}[Smooth atoms are regular atoms]
\label{lem:smooth-atoms-regular}
\lean{Rd.IsSmoothAtom.enorm_higherDiff_le, Rd.isRegularAtomOn_of_isSmoothAtom}
\leanok
\uses{def:smooth-atoms, lem:convex-differences, lem:pointwise-atoms}
Let $\Omega$ be measurable and convex with nonempty interior, and let $a$ be a smooth atom on
$\Omega$ of order $K$ and level $j$. For $1 \leq r \leq K$ and $x \in \Omega_{rh}$,
$|\Delta_h^r a(x)| \leq 2^r r^{r+1} A \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^r)$, and $a$ is a regular atom on
$\Omega$ of order and exponent $r$ with the constant
$2^r r^{r+1} A ((r + 1) d^n |B_1|)^{1/p}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:convex-differences} with $C = A 2^{jr}$ gives the bound $(2^j \|h\|)^r$, and
$|\Delta_h^r a| \leq 2^r A$ on $\Omega_{rh}$ since all its points lie in $\Omega$.
\Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms} on $\Omega$ concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Derivatives of convolutions]
\label{lem:conv-derivatives}
\lean{Rd.conv_eq_convolution_mul_real, Rd.contDiff_conv, Rd.fderiv_conv_apply,
  Rd.iteratedFDeriv_conv_apply, Rd.norm_iteratedFDeriv_const_mul_conv_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv}
Let $K$ be smooth with compact support and $g$ locally integrable. Then $K * g$ is smooth and
\[
  D^l (K * g)(x)(v_1, \dots, v_l) = \bigl((D^l K)(\cdot)(v_1, \dots, v_l) * g\bigr)(x) .
\]
Hence $\|D^l (c\, K * g)(x)\| \leq B$ as soon as
$|c\, ((D^l K)(\cdot)(v_1, \dots, v_l) * g)(x)| \leq B \prod_i \|v_i\|$ for all $v_i$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Mathlib's \texttt{HasCompactSupport.hasFDerivAt\_convolution\_left} differentiates under the
integral; induction on $l$ applies it to the directional derivatives of $K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Derivatives of the dual kernels]
\label{lem:dual-kernel-derivatives}
\lean{Rd.contDiff_dilate, Rd.ofReal_two_pow_mul, Rd.norm_iteratedFDeriv_dilate_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.contDiff_scaled, Rd.LocalMeansBump.contDiff_dual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_iteratedFDeriv_dual_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
Dilations of smooth functions are smooth, with
$\|D^l (\Phi_c)(x)\| \leq |c|^{n+l} \sup_y \|D^l \Phi(y)\|$. For a bump $g$ and $L, K \in \N$ the
kernels $m_{2^j}$ and $\eta_j$ are smooth, and there is $A$ with
$\|D^l \eta_j\| \leq A 2^{j(n+l)}$ for all $l \leq K$ and $j$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\Phi_c(x) = c^n \Phi(cx)$, and the chain rule for the linear map $x \mapsto cx$. The moment kernel
is smooth with compact support, so its derivatives are bounded, and $\eta_j$ is a sum of two
dilations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Cell atoms are smooth atoms]
\label{lem:cell-atoms-smooth}
\lean{Rd.norm_iteratedFDeriv_cellAtom_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_isSmoothAtom_cellAtom}
\leanok
\uses{lem:conv-derivatives, lem:dual-kernel-derivatives, lem:cell-atoms-regular}
Let $K$ be smooth with $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, \rho 2^{-j})$ and
$\|D^l K\| \leq A 2^{j(n+l)}$. Then the cell atoms of $K * g$, $g \in L_p$, have
$\|D^l a_{j,m}\| \leq A 2^{jl} (\rho^n |B_1|)^{1-1/p}$. For a bump of radius $R$ and every order
$K$ there is $A$ such that every cell atom of $\eta_j * g$ is a smooth atom on $V$ of order $K$
with radius factor $2LR + n$ and constant $A$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:conv-derivatives} moves the derivatives onto the kernel, and the bound of
\Cref{lem:conv-pointwise} for the kernel $(D^l K)(\cdot)(v_1, \dots, v_l)$ cancels the factor
$2^{jn}$ against the coefficient as in \Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Atomic decomposition into smooth atoms]
\label{thm:smooth-atomic-decomposition}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.atomic_decomposition_smooth, Rd.stdBumpBase, Rd.LocalMeansBump.std}
\leanok
\uses{thm:atomic-decomposition, lem:cell-atoms-smooth}
Let $g$ be a bump of radius $R$, $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $L, K \in \N$. There is $A$ such that for
every $F \in L_p$ the terms of the reproducing formula are
$\eta_j * (k_j * F) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere, with cell atoms that are
smooth atoms on $V$ of order $K$, radius factor $2LR + n$ and constant $A$, and
$\|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \|(2^{js} \|k_j * F\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$. The normalised smooth bump of
radius $1$ around $0$ is a bump.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:atomic-decomposition} for the decomposition and the coefficients, and
\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-smooth} for the atoms.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Smooth changes of variables with a cut-off]
\label{def:cutoff-diffeo}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.carrier,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.isCompact_carrier, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.carrier_subset,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_eq_zero_of_notMem, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_eq_zero_of_forall_far,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.uniqueDiffOn_source, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.uniqueDiffOn_target,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.continuousOn_apply}
\leanok
Let $\Omega \subseteq V$. A smooth change of variables with a cut-off on $\Omega$ consists of open
sets $U, U'$, a bijection $\Phi : \Omega \cap U \to \Omega \cap U'$ that is smooth within
$\Omega \cap U$ with inverse $\Psi$ smooth within $\Omega \cap U'$, a compact
$K_0 \subseteq \Omega \cap U'$, and a function $\chi$ that is smooth within $\Omega \cap U'$ and
vanishes on $\Omega \setminus K_0$. It acts on functions by
\[
  T f = 1_{\Omega \cap U}\, (\chi f) \circ \Phi .
\]
$T f$ vanishes outside the compact set $\Psi(K_0) \subseteq \Omega \cap U$, and $T a = 0$ if $a$
vanishes on a ball that contains no point of $K_0$, and $T f$ is continuous on $\Omega$ for
continuous $f$. If $\Omega$ is convex with nonempty interior, $\Omega \cap U$ and $\Omega \cap U'$
are sets of unique differentiability.
\end{definition}

The pieces of a function in two overlapping charts of a manifold are related in this way; for
$\Omega = V$ and $\Phi = \mathrm{id}$, $T$ is a pointwise multiplier.

\begin{lemma}[Bounds of the change of variables]
\label{lem:cutoff-diffeo-bounds}
\lean{Rd.locallyLipschitzOn_inter, Rd.exists_lipschitzOnWith_of_contDiffOn_inter,
  Rd.iteratedFDerivWithin_eq_zero_of_eqOn, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_lipschitzOnWith_Psi,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_lipschitzOnWith_Phi, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_iteratedFDerivWithin_chi_bound,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_iteratedFDerivWithin_Phi_bound}
\leanok
\uses{def:cutoff-diffeo}
A map of class $C^1$ within a relatively open part $\Omega \cap U$ of a convex set is Lipschitz on
the compact subsets of $\Omega \cap U$. Hence $\Psi$ is Lipschitz on $K_0$ and $\Phi$ on
$\Psi(K_0)$. For every $K$ the derivatives of $\chi$ of order at most $K$ are bounded on
$\Omega \cap U'$, and those of $\Phi$ on $\Psi(K_0)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The mean value inequality on the convex sets $\Omega \cap B$ for small balls $B$ gives local
Lipschitz bounds within $\Omega$; on a compact set finitely many of them suffice. The derivatives
are continuous within the sets, hence bounded on the compact sets $K_0$ and $\Psi(K_0)$; the
derivatives of $\chi$ vanish on $\Omega \cap U' \setminus K_0$, where $\chi$ vanishes near
every point.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Overlap of transported centres]
\label{lem:transported-centres}
\lean{Rd.card_le_of_lipschitz_centres}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cells-partition}
Let $\Phi$ be $L$-Lipschitz on $B$, and let $z_m \in B$ with
$\|\Phi(z_m) - c_{j,m}\| \leq d 2^{-j}$ for the centres $c_{j,m}$ of level $j$. Then at most
$\lceil (2(2L\rho + d) + 2)^n \rceil$ of the points $z_m$ lie in a ball of radius $\rho 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $z_{m_0}$ and $z_m$ lie in such a ball, then
$\|\Phi(z_{m_0}) - c_{j,m}\| \leq (2L\rho + d) 2^{-j}$, and \Cref{lem:cells-partition} counts the
centres in the ball around $\Phi(z_{m_0})$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Smooth atoms under changes of variables]
\label{thm:smooth-atoms-change-of-variables}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.norm_iteratedFDerivWithin_mul_atom_le, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_eq_zero_of_far,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.isSmoothAtom_apply}
\leanok
\uses{def:smooth-atoms, def:cutoff-diffeo, lem:cutoff-diffeo-bounds}
Let $\Omega$ be measurable and convex with nonempty interior, $T$ a smooth change of variables
with a cut-off on $\Omega$, and $K \in \N$. There are $L, A' \geq 0$ such that for every smooth
atom $a$ on $V$ of order $K$, level $j$, radius factor $d$, constant $A$ and centre $c$, and every
$y_0 \in K_0$ with $\|y_0 - c\| \leq d 2^{-j}$, $T a$ is a smooth atom on $\Omega$ of order $K$
and level $j$ with centre $\Psi(y_0)$, radius factor $2Ld$ and constant $A' A$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $T a(x) \neq 0$, then $\Phi(x) \in K_0$ and $\|\Phi(x) - c\| \leq d 2^{-j}$, so
$\|x - \Psi(y_0)\| \leq L \|\Phi(x) - y_0\| \leq 2 L d 2^{-j}$ with a Lipschitz constant $L$ of
$\Psi$ on $K_0$. By Leibniz's rule within $\Omega \cap U'$,
$\|D^i (\chi a)\| \leq 2^i M A 2^{ji}$ (Mathlib's \texttt{norm\_iteratedFDerivWithin\_mul\_le}),
and by the chain rule within sets (Mathlib's \texttt{norm\_iteratedFDerivWithin\_comp\_le}),
$\|D^l (T a)(x)\| \leq l!\, 2^l M A D^l 2^{jl}$ on $\Psi(K_0)$, with the bounds $M$ of $\chi$ and
$D \geq 1$ of $\Phi$ from \Cref{lem:cutoff-diffeo-bounds}. Outside the compact $\Psi(K_0)$ the
function $T a$ vanishes near every point of $\Omega$, so its derivatives within $\Omega$ vanish.
Hence $A' = K!\, 2^K M D^K$.
\end{proof}

\section{Invariance under changes of variables}

A smooth change of variables with a cut-off maps the Besov spaces of $V$ into the intrinsic Besov
spaces of a convex set, and, on sets with a bounded extension, the intrinsic spaces into
themselves. The proof transports smooth atoms (\Cref{thm:smooth-atoms-change-of-variables}).

\begin{lemma}[The change of variables on $L_p$]
\label{lem:cutoff-diffeo-lp}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_add, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_smul, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_tsum,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.apply_ae_eq, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.aestronglyMeasurable_apply,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.lintegral_apply_eq, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_det_bound,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.eLpNorm_apply_le, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.memLp_apply, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.applyLp,
  Rd.CutoffDiffeo.applyLp_ae}
\leanok
\uses{def:cutoff-diffeo}
Let $\Omega$ be measurable and convex with nonempty interior, and $T$ a smooth change of variables
with a cut-off on $\Omega$. Then $T$ is linear, $T g_1 = T g_2$ almost everywhere if $g_1 = g_2$
almost everywhere on $\Omega$, and for $e > 0$
\[
  \int |T g|^e = \int_{K_0} |\det D\Psi(y)|\, |\chi(y)|^e |g(y)|^e \, dy .
\]
Hence $T$ is a bounded linear operator on $L_p$, $1 \leq p < \infty$. If for every $m$ either
$\lambda'_m = \lambda_m$ or $T a_m = 0$, then $T(\sum_m \lambda_m a_m) = \sum_m \lambda'_m T a_m$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $T g_1(x) \neq T g_2(x)$, then $x = \Psi(y)$ with $y \in K_0$ and $g_1(y) \neq g_2(y)$; the
differentiable map $\Psi$ maps the null set of these $y$ to a null set (Mathlib's
\texttt{addHaar\_image\_eq\_zero\_of\_differentiableOn\_of\_addHaar\_eq\_zero}). $T g$ vanishes
outside $\Psi(K_0)$, and the change of variables $x = \Psi(y)$ on the compact $K_0$ (Mathlib's
\texttt{lintegral\_image\_eq\_lintegral\_abs\_det\_fderiv\_mul}) gives the integral; the Jacobian
and $\chi$ are bounded on $K_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Transport of smooth atoms]
\label{lem:transported-atoms}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.exists_transported_atoms}
\leanok
\uses{thm:smooth-atoms-change-of-variables, lem:smooth-atoms-regular, lem:transported-centres}
Let $\Omega$ be measurable and convex with nonempty interior, $r \geq 1$, and $d, A \geq 0$. There
are $d'$, $C' < \infty$ and $N$ such that for smooth atoms $a_{j,m}$ on $V$ of order $r$, radius
factor $d$ and constant $A$ centred at the cell centres $c_{j,m}$, and all coefficients
$\lambda_{j,m}$, the functions $T a_{j,m}$ are regular atoms on $\Omega$ of order and exponent
$r$ with radius factor $d'$ and constant $C'$, with coefficients $\lambda'_{j,m}$ such that
$|\lambda'_{j,m}| \leq |\lambda_{j,m}|$, $\lambda'_{j,m} = \lambda_{j,m}$ unless $T a_{j,m} = 0$,
and at most $N$ centres with $\lambda'_{j,m} \neq 0$ lie in a ball of radius $(d' + r) 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If a point $y \in K_0$ lies within $d 2^{-j}$ of $c_{j,m}$, the new centre is $\Psi(y)$ and
$\lambda'_{j,m} = \lambda_{j,m}$ (\Cref{thm:smooth-atoms-change-of-variables},
\Cref{lem:smooth-atoms-regular}); otherwise $T a_{j,m} = 0$ and $\lambda'_{j,m} = 0$. Since
$\Phi(\Psi(y)) = y$ and $\Phi$ is Lipschitz on $\Psi(K_0)$, \Cref{lem:transported-centres} counts
the centres.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Invariance under changes of variables]
\label{thm:diffeo-invariance}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.modNormOn_applyLp_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:cutoff-diffeo-lp, lem:transported-atoms, thm:smooth-atomic-decomposition,
  thm:rd-atomic-synthesis, thm:local-means}
Let $\Omega$ be measurable and convex with nonempty interior, $T$ a smooth change of variables
with a cut-off on $\Omega$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. There is
$C < \infty$ such that for all $F \in L_p(V)$
\[
  \|T F\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(T F, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq
  C \bigl(\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}\bigr) .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Decompose $F$ into smooth cell atoms of order $r$ (\Cref{thm:smooth-atomic-decomposition}) for the
standard bump. $T$ is continuous on $L_p$, so $T F = \sum_j T f_j$ in $L_p$, and
$T f_j = \sum_m \lambda'_{j,m} T a_{j,m}$ almost everywhere by \Cref{lem:cutoff-diffeo-lp}. The
transported atoms (\Cref{lem:transported-atoms}) are regular atoms on $\Omega$ of exponent
$r > s$, and the synthesis on $\Omega$ (\Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}) bounds the left side by
$K \|\lambda'\|_{b^s_{p,q}} \leq K \|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}}$, which is the local means quasi-norm of
$F$, bounded by the right side (\Cref{thm:local-means}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The intrinsic quasi-norm]
\label{lem:modnorm-on}
\lean{Rd.modNormOn, Rd.modNormOn_zero, Rd.modNormOn_add_le, Rd.modNormOn_const_smul,
  Rd.modNormOn_finsetSum_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on, lem:rd-modulus-on}
On a measurable set $\Omega$, $N_\Omega(G) = \|G\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(G, 2^{-k},
\Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$ vanishes at $0$, is homogeneous and, for $q \geq 1$, subadditive, also
for finite sums.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The modulus on $\Omega$ is homogeneous and subadditive (\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}), and so are the
$L_p$ and $\ell_q$ norms.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Besov domains]
\label{def:besov-domain}
\lean{Rd.BesovDomain, Rd.modNormOn_univ, Rd.BesovDomain.univ, Rd.BesovDomain.measurableSet}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on}
A Besov domain is a closed convex set $\Omega \subseteq V$ with nonempty interior such that for
$1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ there is $C < \infty$: every
$f \in L_p(V)$ with
$N_\Omega(f) = \|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$
agrees on $\Omega$ with some $F \in L_p(V)$ with
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq C N_\Omega(f)$. The space $V$ is a
Besov domain, with $F = f$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Invariance on Besov domains]
\label{thm:besov-domain-invariance}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.applyLp_eq_of_indicatorLp_eq, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.modNormOn_applyLp_le_modNormOn,
  Rd.BesovDomain.modNormOn_order_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:besov-domain, thm:diffeo-invariance, thm:rd-besov}
Let $\Omega$ be a Besov domain, $T$ a smooth change of variables with a cut-off on $\Omega$,
$1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. There is $C < \infty$ with
$N_\Omega(T f) \leq C N_\Omega(f)$ for all $f$ with $N_\Omega(f) < \infty$. For $s < r'$ there is
$C < \infty$ with $N_\Omega(f; r') \leq C N_\Omega(f; r)$, where $N_\Omega(f; r)$ uses the
differences of order $r$; so the order is a free parameter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$T f$ depends only on $f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$ (\Cref{lem:cutoff-diffeo-lp}). Extend $f$
to $F$ with $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \leq C N_\Omega(f)$; then
$T f = T F$, and \Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance} applies. For the order, change it on $V$
(\Cref{thm:rd-besov}) and restrict, $N_\Omega(f; r') \leq
\|F\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_{r'}(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$ (\Cref{lem:rd-modulus-on}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Null sets do not matter]
\label{lem:null-set-domains}
\lean{Rd.stepSet_ae_eq, Rd.modulusOn_congr_set, Rd.lpModulusOn_congr_set, Rd.indicatorLp_congr_set}
\leanok
\uses{def:rd-modulus-on}
If $U$ and $U'$ agree up to a null set, so do their step sets $U_{rh}$ and $U'_{rh}$, and
$\omega_r(f, t, U)_p = \omega_r(f, t, U')_p$ and $1_U f = 1_{U'} f$ in $L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The step sets are intersections of translates, and translations preserve the Lebesgue measure.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The half-space is a Besov domain]
\label{thm:half-space-besov-domain}
\lean{Rd.closedHalfSpace, Rd.isClosed_closedHalfSpace, Rd.convex_closedHalfSpace,
  Rd.mem_interior_closedHalfSpace, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flat, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flat_domain,
  Rd.closedHalfSpace_ae_eq, Rd.BesovDomain.closedHalfSpace,
  Rd.BesovDomain.range_euclideanHalfSpace, Rd.BesovDomain.range_of_boundaryless}
\leanok
\uses{def:besov-domain, lem:null-set-domains, thm:graph-besov}
For a unit vector $e$ the closed half-space $\{x : \langle x, e \rangle \geq 0\}$ is a Besov
domain. Hence the models of manifolds are Besov domains: the range $\{y : y_0 \geq 0\}$ of the
model with corners of the Euclidean half-space, and the range $V$ of a model without boundary.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The closed half-space differs from the Lipschitz graph domain $\{x : \langle x, e \rangle > 0\}$
with graph function $0$ by the null set $e^\perp$ (\Cref{lem:null-set-domains}), and Rychkov's
extension is bounded on the latter (\Cref{thm:graph-besov}).
\end{proof}

\section{Traces on Lipschitz graph domains}

Let $\Omega = \{x : \phi(x) < \langle x, e \rangle\}$ be a Lipschitz graph domain
(\Cref{def:lipschitz-graph}) with Lipschitz constant $M$. Its boundary is the graph of $\phi$ over
the hyperplane $H = e^\perp$, of dimension $n - 1$ with its Lebesgue measure, and the trace is a
function on $H$.

\begin{definition}[The graph map]
\label{def:graph-map}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.hyperplane, Rd.LipschitzGraph.e_ne_zero, Rd.LipschitzGraph.inner_e_self,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.inner_coe_e, Rd.LipschitzGraph.sub_inner_smul_mem,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.finrank_hyperplane_add_one, Rd.LipschitzGraph.finrank_hyperplane_eq,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.proj, Rd.LipschitzGraph.coe_proj, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_proj_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.proj_coe, Rd.LipschitzGraph.proj_e, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.proj_graphMap, Rd.LipschitzGraph.inner_graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.phi_graphMap, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_graphMap_sub_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_graphMap, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_sub_proj_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphMap_add_mem}
\leanok
\uses{def:lipschitz-graph, lem:graph-cone}
Let $P x = x - \langle x, e \rangle e$ be the orthogonal projection onto $H$ and
$\gamma(x') = x' + \phi(x') e$ for $x' \in H$. Then $P \gamma(x') = x'$,
$\langle \gamma(x'), e \rangle = \phi(x') = \phi(\gamma(x'))$,
$\|\gamma(x') - \gamma(y')\| \leq (1 + M) \|x' - y'\|$, $\|x' - Pc\| \leq \|\gamma(x') - c\|$, and
$\gamma(x') + K \subseteq \Omega$ for the cone $K$ of \Cref{lem:graph-cone}.
\end{definition}

The last property holds since a step $y \in K$ raises $\langle \cdot, e \rangle$ by more than
$M \|Py\|$ and $\phi$ by at most $M \|Py\|$.

\begin{lemma}[Traces of atoms]
\label{lem:atom-traces}
\lean{Rd.min_one_mul_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.isRegularAtom_comp_graphMap}
\leanok
\uses{def:graph-map, lem:pointwise-atoms}
Let $a$ be continuous with $a = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(c, d 2^{-j})$, $|a| \leq A$ and
$|a(x + h) - a(x)| \leq A \min(1, 2^j \|h\|)$. Then $a \circ \gamma$ is a regular atom on $H$ of
level $j$, order and exponent $1$, centre $Pc$, radius factor $d$ and constant
$(1 + M) A (2 d^{n-1} |B_1|)^{1/p}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
As in the proof of \cite[Theorem 4.2]{SchneiderVybiral}: $\|\gamma(x' + h') - \gamma(x')\| \leq
(1 + M) \|h'\|$, and $\min(1, K t) \leq K \min(1, t)$ for $K \geq 1$; \Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms} on
$H$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Traces of the terms]
\label{def:trace-terms}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceFun, Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceCoef,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_traceFun, Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceFun_eq_tsum,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_traceCoef_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.levelNorm_traceCoef_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:graph-map, lem:cell-decomposition, lem:cell-coefficients}
For a bump, $L \in \N$ and $h \in L_p$, the trace of the term $\eta_j * h$ is the continuous
function $x' \mapsto (\eta_j * h)(\gamma(x'))$ on $H$. With the coefficients $\lambda'_{j,m}$ equal
to $\lambda_{j,m}$ for the cells whose atoms meet the graph and $0$ otherwise,
$(\eta_j * h)(\gamma(x')) = \sum_m \lambda'_{j,m} a_{j,m}(\gamma(x'))$ and
$\|\lambda'_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} \leq 2^{jn/p} \|h\|_p$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The trace atoms]
\label{lem:trace-atoms}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_graphMap_sub_cellCenter_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_trace_atoms}
\leanok
\uses{def:trace-terms, lem:cell-atoms-regular, lem:dual-kernel-bounds, lem:atom-traces,
  lem:cells-partition}
There are $d \geq 0$, $C < \infty$ and $N$ such that for every level $j$ and $h \in L_p$ the
restrictions $a_{j,m} \circ \gamma$ of the cell atoms of $\eta_j * h$ are regular atoms on $H$ of
order and exponent $1$ with centres $P c_{j,m}$, and the centres $P c_{j,m}$ with
$\lambda'_{j,m} \neq 0$ have overlap at most $N$ on balls of radius $(d + 1) 2^{-j}$ in $H$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The cell atoms satisfy the pointwise bounds of \Cref{lem:atom-traces} by
\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-regular} and \Cref{lem:dual-kernel-bounds}. If $\lambda'_{j,m} \neq 0$, the
atom meets the graph at some $\gamma(y')$ with $\|\gamma(y') - c_{j,m}\| \leq d 2^{-j}$; if moreover
$\|x' - P c_{j,m}\| \leq (d + 1) 2^{-j}$, then
$\|\gamma(x') - c_{j,m}\| \leq (d + (1 + M)(2d + 1)) 2^{-j}$, and \Cref{lem:cells-partition} counts
these centres.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The traces of the terms in $L_p(H)$]
\label{lem:trace-terms-lp}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.eLpNorm_traceFun_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.memLp_traceFun,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceTerm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceTerm_ae}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-atoms, lem:level-sums}
There is $K < \infty$ with $\|(\eta_j * h) \circ \gamma\|_{L_p(H)} \leq K 2^{j/p} \|h\|_p$ for all
$j$ and $h \in L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:level-sums} on $H$, of dimension $n - 1$, with \Cref{lem:trace-atoms}:
$2^{-j(n-1)/p} \|\lambda'_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} \leq 2^{-j(n-1)/p} 2^{jn/p} \|h\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace of a series]
\label{thm:trace-series}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceSeries, Rd.LipschitzGraph.summable_traceTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.tendsto_sum_traceTerm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_traceSeries_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-terms-lp, lem:trace-atoms, thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. There is $K < \infty$ such that for
$h_j \in L_p$ with $X = \|(2^{j(s + 1/p)} \|h_j\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ the series
$\operatorname{Tr} = \sum_j (\eta_j * h_j) \circ \gamma$ converges absolutely in $L_p(H)$ and
\[
  \|\operatorname{Tr}\|_{L_p(H)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(\operatorname{Tr}, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq K X.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The terms have $\|(\eta_j * h_j) \circ \gamma\|_{L_p(H)} \leq K 2^{j/p} \|h_j\|_p \leq K X 2^{-js}$
(\Cref{lem:trace-terms-lp}). \Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis} on $H$ with the atoms of
\Cref{lem:trace-atoms} and $\sigma = r = 1 > s$ bounds the left side by the coefficient
quasi-norm on $H$, and since $s - (n - 1)/p = (s + 1/p) - n/p$,
$2^{j(s - (n-1)/p)} \|\lambda'_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p} \leq 2^{j(s + 1/p)} \|h_j\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace theorem on Lipschitz graph domains]
\label{thm:trace-graph}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace, Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_trace_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-series, lem:intrinsic-upper}
The trace of $f \in L_p$ is
$\operatorname{Tr} f = \sum_j (\eta_j * 1_\Omega (k_j * f)) \circ \gamma$, with the kernels of the
cone bump. Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \in \N$. There is
$K < \infty$ such that
\begin{multline*}
  \|\operatorname{Tr} f\|_{L_p(H)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(\operatorname{Tr} f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \\ \leq K \bigl( \|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} +
    \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr)
\end{multline*}
whenever the right side is finite.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 4.2]{SchneiderVybiral} for $p \geq 1$. \Cref{thm:trace-series} with
$h_j = 1_\Omega (k_j * f)$, and \Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper} with smoothness $s + 1/p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Linearity and continuity of the trace]
\label{lem:trace-linear}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceTerm_sub, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_sub,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_trace_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.tendsto_trace_of_modNorm_sub_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_trace_le_modNormOn}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-graph, lem:trace-terms-lp, lem:local-means-upper, lem:intrinsic-upper}
The trace is linear on the functions with a finite intrinsic norm of smoothness $s + 1/p$, $s > 0$,
and there is $K < \infty$ with
$\|\operatorname{Tr} f\|_{L_p(H)} \leq K (\|f\|_p + \sum_k 2^{k/p} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)$ and
$\|\operatorname{Tr} f\|_{L_p(H)} \leq
K (\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \sum_k 2^{k/p} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)$.
If $f$ and the $f_J$ have finite intrinsic norms of smoothness $s + 1/p$ and
$\|f - f_J\|_p + \sum_k 2^{k/p} \omega_L(f - f_J, 2^{-k})_p \to 0$, then
$\operatorname{Tr} f_J \to \operatorname{Tr} f$ in $L_p(H)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The terms are linear in $h_j$, and the series converge. The bound:
$\|\operatorname{Tr} f\|_{L_p(H)} \leq \sum_j K 2^{j/p} \|k_j * f\|_p$
(\Cref{lem:trace-terms-lp}), and the local means are bounded by the moduli
(\Cref{lem:local-means-upper}); with the cut-offs $1_\Omega (k_j * f)$ kept, by the moduli on
$\Omega$ (\Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper}). The convergence follows from the linearity and the bound for
$f - f_J$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Approximate identities at points of continuity]
\label{lem:approx-identity-points}
\lean{Rd.conv_dilate_near}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dilate}
Let $\Phi$ be integrable with $\int \Phi = 1$ and $\Phi = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, R)$, and let
$F_J(z) \to z_0$ as $J \to \infty$ and $z \to y$. Then $(\Phi_{2^J} * F_J)(z) \to z_0$ as
$J \to \infty$ and $z \to y$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$(\Phi_{2^J} * F_J)(z) - z_0 = \int \Phi_{2^J}(w) (F_J(z - w) - z_0) \, dw$, only
$\|w\| \leq R 2^{-J}$ enters, and $\|\Phi_{2^J}\|_1 = \|\Phi\|_1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The reproducing formula at every point]
\label{lem:reproducing-pointwise}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport_scaled, Rd.LocalMeansBump.continuous_scaled,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.hasCompactSupport_kernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.continuous_kernel,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_conv_dual_localMean_apply}
\leanok
\uses{lem:reproducing-formula, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
For $f \in L_p$ and every $y \in V$,
$\sum_{j \leq J} (\eta_j * (k_j * f))(y) = (m_{2^J} * (m_{2^J} * f))(y)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Both sides are continuous and agree almost everywhere by \Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The trace as a limit of mollified restrictions]
\label{lem:trace-mollified}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.sum_traceFun_eq_conv, Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_subseq_tendsto_trace}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-series, lem:reproducing-pointwise, lem:support-principle-two, def:graph-map}
Let $s > 0$ and let $f \in L_p$ have a finite intrinsic norm of smoothness $s + 1/p$. The partial
sums of the trace at $x' \in H$ are $(m_{2^J} * (m_{2^J} * f))(\gamma(x'))$, and a subsequence of
them converges to $\operatorname{Tr} f(x')$ for almost every $x' \in H$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At $\gamma(x')$ the cut-off does not matter, since the kernels are supported in $-K$ and
$\gamma(x') + K \subseteq \Omega$ (\Cref{def:graph-map}, \Cref{lem:support-principle-two}), so
\Cref{lem:reproducing-pointwise} gives the partial sums. They converge to $\operatorname{Tr} f$ in
$L_p(H)$ (\Cref{thm:trace-series}), and a subsequence converges almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace of continuous functions]
\label{thm:trace-continuous}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.sum_traceFun_eq, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_ae_eq_comp_graphMap}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-mollified, lem:approx-identity-points}
Let $L \geq 1$, $s > 0$, and let $f \in L_p$ have a finite norm as in \Cref{thm:trace-graph} and a
continuous representative $G$. Then $\operatorname{Tr} f = G \circ \gamma$ almost everywhere on $H$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The partial sums of the trace at $x'$ are $(m_{2^J} * (m_{2^J} * G))(\gamma(x'))$, and a subsequence
converges to $\operatorname{Tr} f$ almost everywhere (\Cref{lem:trace-mollified}). By
\Cref{lem:approx-identity-points}, applied twice, the whole sequence converges to $G(\gamma(x'))$
for every $x'$.
\end{proof}

\section{The trace space of a Lipschitz graph domain}

To extend functions from $H$ into $V$ with smoothness above $1$, the graph is smoothed at each
scale: the extended atoms need second differences of order $(2^j \|h\|)^2$, which a merely
Lipschitz graph does not give.

\begin{lemma}[Convolution with Lipschitz functions]
\label{lem:conv-lipschitz}
\lean{Rd.higherDiff_one_apply, Rd.higherDiff_two_apply, Rd.integral_higherDiff_eq_zero,
  Rd.norm_conv_sub_conv_le,
  Rd.norm_conv_sub_self_le, Rd.norm_higherDiff_two_conv_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.integral_norm_scaled, Rd.LocalMeansBump.integral_norm_higherDiff_scaled_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:conv-pointwise, lem:dual-kernel-bounds}
Let $u$ satisfy $|u(x) - u(y)| \leq M \|x - y\|$, and let $K$ be integrable, continuous, with
compact support and $K = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(0, R)$. Then
$|(K * u)(x) - (K * u)(y)| \leq M \|K\|_1 \|x - y\|$; if $\int K = 1$, then
$|(K * u)(x) - u(x)| \leq M R \|K\|_1$; and
$|\Delta_h^2 (K * u)(x)| \leq M (R + 2\|h\|) \|\Delta_h^2 K\|_1$. For the dilated moment kernels,
$\|\Delta_v^L m_{2^j}\|_1 \leq (2^j \|v\|)^L \|D^L m\|_1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Differences integrate to $0$, and $\Delta_h^2 (K * u) = (\Delta_h^2 K) * u$
(\Cref{lem:conv-pointwise}), so
$\Delta_h^2 (K * u)(x) = \int \Delta_h^2 K(z) (u(x - z) - u(x)) \, dz$ with
$\|z\| \leq R + 2\|h\|$ on the support of $\Delta_h^2 K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The mollified graph]
\label{def:mollified-graph}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphFun, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_graphFun_sub_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_graphFun, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBump,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBump_radius, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBumpNorm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBumpNorm_nonneg, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBumpDeriv,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBumpDeriv_nonneg, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphBump_scaled_eq_zero,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.integrable_graphBump_mul, Rd.LipschitzGraph.smoothGraph,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_smoothGraph}
\leanok
\uses{def:graph-map, def:moment-kernel}
Let $\rho$ be the moment kernel of order $2$ of a bump of radius $1$ on $H$. The mollified graph at
scale $2^{-j}$ is $\phi_j = \operatorname{Re} (\rho_{2^j} * \phi)$ on $H$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Bounds of the mollified graph]
\label{lem:mollified-graph}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.abs_smoothGraph_sub_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.abs_smoothGraph_sub_phi_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.abs_second_smoothGraph_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:mollified-graph, lem:conv-lipschitz}
$\phi_j$ is Lipschitz with constant $M \|\rho\|_1$, $|\phi_j - \phi| \leq 2 M \|\rho\|_1 2^{-j}$, and
$|\phi_j(x' + 2h') - 2\phi_j(x' + h') + \phi_j(x')| \leq 4 M \|D^2 \rho\|_1 2^j \|h'\|^2$ for
$\|h'\| \leq 2^{-j}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:conv-lipschitz} with $R = 2 \cdot 2^{-j}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The vertical cut-off]
\label{def:vertical-cutoff}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoffLevel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.cutoffLevel_pos,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.profileBump, Rd.LipschitzGraph.profile, Rd.LipschitzGraph.contDiff_profile,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.hasCompactSupport_profile, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_profile_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.profile_eq_one, Rd.LipschitzGraph.profile_eq_zero,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_profile_bound, Rd.LipschitzGraph.profileConst,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_iteratedFDeriv_profile_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.profileConst_nonneg,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCoord, Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCutoff, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_vertCutoff_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCutoff_graphMap, Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCutoff_eq_zero,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_vertCutoff}
\leanok
\uses{lem:mollified-graph}
Let $c = 2 M \|\rho\|_1 + 1$ and let $\chi$ be a smooth bump on $\R$ with $0 \leq \chi \leq 1$,
$\chi = 1$ on $[-c, c]$ and $\chi = 0$ outside $(-(c + 1), c + 1)$. The vertical cut-off is
$v_j(x) = \chi(\psi_j(x))$ with $\psi_j(x) = 2^j (\langle x, e \rangle - \phi_j(Px))$. Then
$v_j(\gamma(x')) = 1$, since $|\psi_j(\gamma(x'))| = 2^j |\phi(x') - \phi_j(x')| < c$, and
$v_j(x) = 0$ if $|\langle x, e \rangle - \phi_j(Px)| \geq (c + 1) 2^{-j}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Differences of the vertical cut-off]
\label{lem:vertical-cutoff}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_profile_sub_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_profile_second_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.abs_vertCoord_sub_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.abs_second_vertCoord_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCutoffConst, Rd.LipschitzGraph.four_le_vertCutoffConst,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_vertCutoff_add_sub_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_vertCutoff_second_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.norm_higherDiff_vertCutoff_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_higherDiff_vertCutoff_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:vertical-cutoff, lem:mollified-graph, lem:smooth-differences}
There is $B < \infty$ with $|\Delta_h^m v_j| \leq B \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^m)$ for $m \leq 2$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\psi_j$ is Lipschitz with constant $2^j (1 + M \|\rho\|_1)$ and has second differences at most
$4 M \|D^2 \rho\|_1 (2^j \|h\|)^2$ for $\|h\| \leq 2^{-j}$ (\Cref{lem:mollified-graph}); the linear
part $\langle x, e \rangle$ has none. With $\psi_l = \psi_j(x + lh)$ and $t = \psi_1 - \psi_0$,
\[
  \Delta_h^2 v_j(x) = [\chi(\psi_2) - \chi(2\psi_1 - \psi_0)] + \Delta_t^2 \chi(\psi_0),
\]
and \Cref{lem:smooth-differences} bounds both terms by the derivatives of $\chi$. For
$\|h\| > 2^{-j}$, $|\Delta_h^m v_j| \leq 4$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Extended atoms]
\label{lem:extended-atoms}
\lean{Rd.min_one_rpow_mul_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extendAtom, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extendAtom_graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_extendAtom, Rd.LipschitzGraph.higherDiff_comp_proj,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_extendAtom_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.enorm_higherDiff_two_extendAtom_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.extendRadius, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extendCenter,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.proj_extendCenter, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extendAtom_eq_zero,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.isRegularAtom_extendAtom}
\leanok
\uses{lem:vertical-cutoff, lem:leibniz-differences, lem:pointwise-atoms}
Let $a$ be continuous on $H$, $a = 0$ outside $\overline{B}(c', d 2^{-j})$, and
$|\Delta_h^l a| \leq A_0 \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^l)$ for $l \leq 2$. Then $A(x) = a(Px) v_j(x)$ has
$A \circ \gamma = a$, vanishes outside
$\overline{B}(c' + \phi_j(c') e, (d (1 + M \|\rho\|_1) + c + 1) 2^{-j})$, and
$|\Delta_h^2 A| \leq 4 A_0 B \min(1, (2^j \|h\|)^2)$; so $A$ is a regular atom of order and exponent
$2$ on $V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By Leibniz's rule for differences (\Cref{lem:leibniz-differences}),
$\Delta_h^2 A(x) = \sum_{l \leq 2} \binom{2}{l} \Delta_{Ph}^l a(Px) \, \Delta_h^{2 - l} v_j(x + lh)$,
and $\min(1, t^l) \min(1, t^{2-l}) \leq \min(1, t^2)$. \Cref{lem:pointwise-atoms} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The extension from the boundary]
\label{thm:graph-trace-extension}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.extLevelH, Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_extLevelH,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.extRadiusH, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extRadiusH_nonneg,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_extension_atoms, Rd.LipschitzGraph.extLevel,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.extLevel_eq_tsum, Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_extLevel,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.eLpNorm_extLevel_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.memLp_extLevel,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceExtTerm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.traceExtTerm_ae,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphExtension, Rd.LipschitzGraph.summable_traceExtTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_graphExtension_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:extended-atoms, thm:atomic-decomposition, lem:cell-atoms-dual, thm:rd-atomic-synthesis,
  thm:local-means}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. For $g \in L_p(H)$ let
$g_j = \eta_j * (k_j * g)$ be the terms of the reproducing formula on $H$, with moment order $1$,
and $F_j(x) = g_j(Px) v_j(x)$. There is $K < \infty$ such that $E g = \sum_j F_j$ satisfies
\begin{multline*}
  \|E g\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_2(E g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \\ \leq K \bigl( \|g\|_{L_p(H)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \bigr).
\end{multline*}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 4.10]{SchneiderVybiral} for $p \geq 1$. $F_j = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} A_{j,m}$
with the extensions of the cell atoms of $g_j$ (\Cref{lem:extended-atoms}), whose centres project to
the cell centres of $H$ and so have bounded overlap. The atomic synthesis on $V$
(\Cref{thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}) with exponent $2 > s + 1/p$ bounds the left side by the coefficient
quasi-norm with smoothness $s + 1/p$ on $V$, which is the one with smoothness $s$ on $H$ since
$(s + 1/p) - n/p = s - (n-1)/p$, that is, the local means quasi-norm of $g$
(\Cref{lem:cell-coefficients}, \Cref{thm:local-means}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Moduli of different orders]
\label{lem:moduli-orders}
\lean{Rd.lpModulus_add_order_le, Rd.modNorm_order_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_lt_top_of_modNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:rd-modulus, lem:intrinsic-upper}
$\omega_{r + l}(f, t)_p \leq 2^l \omega_r(f, t)_p$. Hence a finite norm with moduli of order $2$ on
$V$ gives a finite intrinsic norm of every order $L \geq 2$ on $\Omega$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\Delta_h^{r + l} f = \Delta_h^l \Delta_h^r f$ and $\|\Delta_h^l g\|_p \leq 2^l \|g\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace of the extension]
\label{thm:trace-extension}
\lean{Rd.modNorm_one_tsum_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_modNorm_traceExtTerm_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_sum_traceExtTerm, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_graphExtension}
\leanok
\uses{thm:graph-trace-extension, thm:trace-continuous, lem:trace-linear, lem:moduli-orders,
  lem:reproducing-formula}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $L \geq 2$, and $g \in L_p(H)$ with
$\|g\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. Then
$\operatorname{Tr}(E g) = g$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The partial sums $\sum_{j \leq J} F_j$ are continuous, so their traces are
$\sum_{j \leq J} F_j \circ \gamma = \sum_{j \leq J} g_j$ (\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}), which converge
to $g$ (\Cref{lem:reproducing-formula}). They converge to $E g$ in the norm
$\|\cdot\|_p + \sum_k 2^{k/p} \omega_L(\cdot, 2^{-k})_p$, since each level has norm at most a constant
times $\|k_j * g\|_p$ by \Cref{lem:single-level} and \Cref{lem:moduli-orders}, and
$\sum_j \|k_j * g\|_p < \infty$; there the trace is linear and continuous (\Cref{lem:trace-linear}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace space of a Lipschitz graph domain]
\label{thm:graph-trace-space}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_trace_eq_iff}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-graph, thm:trace-extension, lem:moduli-orders}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \geq 2$. A function
$g \in L_p(H)$ is the trace of some $f$ with
$\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and
only if $\|g\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 4.11]{SchneiderVybiral} for one Lipschitz graph domain and $p \geq 1$. The
trace is bounded (\Cref{thm:trace-graph}), and $E g$ has the trace $g$
(\Cref{thm:trace-extension}) and a finite intrinsic norm (\Cref{thm:graph-trace-extension},
\Cref{lem:moduli-orders}).
\end{proof}

\section{Traces of every order on the half-space}

For the half-space the boundary is flat, and the restrictions and extensions of smooth atoms stay
smooth. This removes the restriction $s < 1$ of the Lipschitz case. Let $e$ be a unit vector,
$\Omega = \{x : \langle x, e \rangle > 0\}$ the Lipschitz graph domain with graph function $0$,
and $H = e^\perp$.

\begin{lemma}[Restrictions of smooth atoms to the hyperplane]
\label{lem:smooth-atoms-hyperplane}
\lean{Rd.norm_iteratedFDeriv_comp_clm_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flat_graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.isSmoothAtom_comp_graphMap_flat}
\leanok
\uses{def:smooth-atoms, def:graph-map}
The graph map of $\Omega$ is the inclusion of $H$. A smooth atom on $V$ of order $K$ with centre
$c$ restricts to a smooth atom on $H$ of order $K$ with centre $Pc$, the same radius factor and the
same constant.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\|D^i (f \circ L)(x)\| \leq \|D^i f(Lx)\| \|L\|^i$ for a continuous linear map $L$, and the
inclusion has norm at most $1$. For $x' \in H$, $\|x' - Pc\| \leq \|x' - c\|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace theorem of every order]
\label{thm:trace-every-order}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_trace_atoms_flat, Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_traceSeries_le_flat,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_trace_le_flat}
\leanok
\uses{lem:smooth-atoms-hyperplane, lem:cell-atoms-smooth, lem:smooth-atoms-regular,
  thm:trace-graph, thm:rd-atomic-synthesis}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \in \N$. There is
$K < \infty$ such that
\begin{multline*}
  \|\operatorname{Tr} f\|_{L_p(H)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(\operatorname{Tr} f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
  \\ \leq K \bigl(\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} +
    \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}\bigr)
\end{multline*}
for all $f \in L_p(V)$ for which the right side is finite.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
As \Cref{thm:trace-graph}: the restrictions of the cell atoms are smooth atoms of order $r$ on $H$
(\Cref{lem:cell-atoms-smooth}, \Cref{lem:smooth-atoms-hyperplane}), hence regular atoms of order
and exponent $r$ (\Cref{lem:smooth-atoms-regular}). The centres of the cells meeting $H$ lie within
$3d 2^{-j} + r 2^{-j}$ of the point, and the synthesis on $H$ with exponent $r > s$ concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Extended atoms of every order]
\label{lem:extended-atoms-every-order}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.smoothGraph_flat, Rd.LipschitzGraph.vertCutoff_flat,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_isSmoothAtom_extendAtom_flat}
\leanok
\uses{def:vertical-cutoff, lem:extended-atoms, def:smooth-atoms}
For $\Omega$ the vertical cut-off is $v_j(x) = \chi(2^j \langle x, e \rangle)$. For every order $K$
there is $B \geq 0$ such that the extension $A(x) = a(Px) \chi(2^j \langle x, e \rangle)$ of a
smooth atom $a$ on $H$ of order $K$, level $j$ and constant $A$ is a smooth atom on $V$ of order
$K$ with constant $2^K B A$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The mollified graph of the graph function $0$ vanishes. Leibniz's rule bounds
$\|D^l A\| \leq \sum_i \binom{l}{i} A 2^{ji} B 2^{j(l-i)} = 2^l A B 2^{jl}$, since
$\|D^i (a \circ P)\| \leq A 2^{ji}$ and the derivatives of $x \mapsto \chi(2^j \langle x, e \rangle)$
of order $i$ are at most $2^{ji} \sup |\chi^{(i)}|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The extension of every order]
\label{def:flat-extension}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatLevelH, Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_flatLevelH,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatLevel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatLevel_eq_tsum,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_flatLevel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatTerm_ae, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatExtension}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, lem:cell-decomposition}
For $g \in L_p(H)$ and a moment order $L_H$ let $g_j = \eta_j * (k_j * g)$ be the terms of the
reproducing formula on $H$. The extension is $E g = \sum_j F_j$ with the continuous levels
$F_j(x) = g_j(Px) \chi(2^j \langle x, e \rangle) = \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} A_{j,m}(x)$, the
extensions of the cell atoms of $g_j$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The extension of every order is bounded]
\label{thm:flat-extension-bounded}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_flat_extension_atoms, Rd.LipschitzGraph.eLpNorm_flatLevel_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.memLp_flatLevel, Rd.LipschitzGraph.summable_flatTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.coeffNorm_flat_eq, Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_flatExtension_le_localMeansNorm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.modNorm_flatExtension_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:flat-extension, lem:extended-atoms-every-order, lem:cell-atoms-smooth,
  thm:rd-atomic-synthesis, thm:local-means}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$ and $s + 1/p < r$. There is $K < \infty$ with
\begin{multline*}
  \|E g\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_r(E g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} \\ \leq
  K \bigl(\|g\|_{L_p(H)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_{L_H}(g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}\bigr) .
\end{multline*}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The extended atoms are smooth of order $r$ (\Cref{lem:extended-atoms-every-order}), hence regular
atoms of order and exponent $r$ on $V$; they project to the cell centres of $H$, so they have
bounded overlap. The coefficient quasi-norm with smoothness $s + 1/p$ in dimension $n$ is the local
means quasi-norm of $g$ with smoothness $s$, since $(s + 1/p) - n/p = s - (n-1)/p$, and
\Cref{thm:local-means} bounds it.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace of the extension of every order]
\label{thm:trace-flat-extension}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_modNorm_flatTerm_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_sum_flatTerm,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_flatExtension}
\leanok
\uses{thm:flat-extension-bounded, thm:trace-continuous, thm:trace-extension}
Let $L \geq 2$, $L_H \geq 1$, $s > 0$ with $s + 1/p < L$, and $g \in L_p(H)$ with
$\|(2^{js} \|k_j * g\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. Then $\operatorname{Tr}(E g) = g$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
As \Cref{thm:trace-extension}: the partial sums $\sum_{j \leq J} F_j$ are continuous with traces
$\sum_{j \leq J} g_j \to g$ (\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}), and they converge to $E g$ in the norm of
$\Besov{1/p}{p}{1}$ with order $L$, where the trace is continuous.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace space of the half-space]
\label{thm:half-space-trace-space}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.exists_trace_eq_iff_flat}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-every-order, thm:flat-extension-bounded, thm:trace-flat-extension}
