Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $L \geq 2$ with $s + 1/p < L$, and
$r > s$. Then $g \in L_p(H)$ is the trace of some $f$ with
$\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{k(s+1/p)} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and
only if $\|g\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(g, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. So
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(H)$ is the trace space of $\Besov{s + 1/p}{p}{q}$ of the half-space for every
$s > 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:trace-every-order} bounds the trace. Conversely, the extension with moment order
$L_H = r$ has finite norm (\Cref{thm:flat-extension-bounded}) and trace $g$
(\Cref{thm:trace-flat-extension}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Traces of functions continuous on the closed half-space]
\label{lem:trace-continuous-half-space}
\lean{Rd.foldHalfSpace, Rd.continuous_foldHalfSpace, Rd.foldHalfSpace_of_nonneg,
  Rd.inner_foldHalfSpace, Rd.foldHalfSpace_mem, Rd.norm_foldHalfSpace,
  Rd.trace_ae_eq_of_continuousOn}
\leanok
\uses{thm:trace-continuous, lem:trace-locality}
Let $f \in L_p(V)$ have a finite intrinsic norm of smoothness $s + 1/p$, $s > 0$, on $\Omega$, and
let $G$ be continuous on the closed half-space $\{x : \langle x, e \rangle \geq 0\}$, vanish there
outside a compact set, and agree with $f$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$. Then
$\operatorname{Tr} f = G$ almost everywhere on $H$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The folding $F(x) = x + (|\langle x, e \rangle| - \langle x, e \rangle) e$ is continuous, preserves
the norm, maps $V$ into the closed half-space and fixes it. So $G \circ F$ is continuous with
compact support and agrees with $f$ on $\Omega$. The trace depends only on $1_\Omega f$
(\Cref{lem:trace-locality}), and the trace of a continuous function is its restriction
(\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}).
\end{proof}

\section{Traces in charts}

Three properties make the trace a function on the boundary, independent of the graph domain that
describes the boundary near a point: locality, approximation by continuous functions, and the
change of chart. In this section $\Omega$, $\Omega_1$ and $\Omega_2$ are Lipschitz graph domains,
with hyperplanes $H$, $H_1$, $H_2$, graph maps $\gamma$, $\gamma_1$, $\gamma_2$ and projections $P$,
$P_1$, $P_2$.

\begin{lemma}[Locality of the trace]
\label{lem:trace-locality}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.conv_scaled_convLp_eq_zero,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_ae_eq_zero_of_ae_eq_zero, Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_sub_le,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_ae_eq_of_ae_eq, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_congr}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-mollified, lem:trace-linear, lem:support-principle-two}
Let $s > 0$, let $f_1, f_2 \in L_p$ have finite intrinsic norms of smoothness $s + 1/p$, and let
$U \subseteq V$ be open with $f_1 = f_2$ almost everywhere on $\Omega \cap U$. Then
$\operatorname{Tr} f_1 = \operatorname{Tr} f_2$ almost everywhere on $\gamma^{-1}(U)$. For
$U = V$ this holds for all $f_1, f_2 \in L_p$: the trace depends only on $1_\Omega f$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By linearity (\Cref{lem:trace-linear}) it suffices to treat $f = f_1 - f_2$, whose intrinsic norm is
at most the sum of the two. The mollified restriction $(m_{2^J} * (m_{2^J} * f))(\gamma(x'))$ only
sees $f$ on $\Omega \cap B(\gamma(x'), 2 L R 2^{-J})$, since the kernels are supported in the cone
(\Cref{lem:support-principle-two}); so it vanishes for large $J$ if $\gamma(x') \in U$, and a
subsequence converges to $\operatorname{Tr} f(x')$ almost everywhere (\Cref{lem:trace-mollified}).
For $U = V$, the trace is built from the cut-offs $1_\Omega (k_j * f)$, which depend only on
$1_\Omega f$ (\Cref{lem:support-principle-two}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Approximation by the partial sums of the reproducing formula]
\label{lem:partial-sums-approx}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.partialSum, Rd.LocalMeansBump.partialSum_ae,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.continuous_partialSum_rep, Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_range_ite_lt,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.tendsto_modNorm_sub_partialSum, Rd.LocalMeansBump.modNorm_partialSum_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{lem:reproducing-formula, lem:local-means-synthesis, lem:intrinsic-upper}
The partial sums $G_J = \sum_{j \leq J} \eta_j * (k_j * f)$ are continuous and, for $0 < \sigma < L$,
have finite norms $\|G_J\|_p + \|(2^{k\sigma} \omega_L(G_J, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$. If
$\|f\|_p + \|(2^{k\sigma} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ and $0 < \sigma' < \sigma$,
$\sigma' < L$, then
\[
  \|f - G_J\|_p + \sum_k 2^{k\sigma'} \omega_L(f - G_J, 2^{-k})_p \to 0 .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The tail $f - G_J = \sum_{j > J} \eta_j * (k_j * f)$ is bounded by the synthesis estimate
(\Cref{lem:local-means-synthesis}) by
$\sum_{j > J} 2^{j\sigma'} \|k_j * f\|_p \leq
\sum_{j > J} 2^{-j(\sigma - \sigma')} \|(2^{i\sigma} \|k_i * f\|_p)_i\|_{\ell_q}$, and the local
means quasi-norm is finite (\Cref{lem:intrinsic-upper}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The traces of the partial sums]
\label{lem:trace-partial-sums}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_lt_top_of_modNorm',
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.tendsto_trace_partialSum}
\leanok
\uses{lem:partial-sums-approx, lem:trace-linear, lem:restriction-moduli}
Let $s > 0$, $s + 1/p < L$ and
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. Then $F$ and the $G_J$
have finite intrinsic norms on every Lipschitz graph domain, and
$\operatorname{Tr} G_J \to \operatorname{Tr} F$ in $L_p(H)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The trace is linear and bounded by $\|\cdot\|_p + \sum_k 2^{k/p} \omega_L(\cdot, 2^{-k})_p$
(\Cref{lem:trace-linear}), and \Cref{lem:partial-sums-approx} with $\sigma' = 1/p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Lipschitz images of null sets]
\label{lem:lipschitz-null}
\lean{Rd.volume_image_eq_zero_of_lipschitzWith}
\leanok
A Lipschitz map between finite-dimensional inner product spaces of the same dimension maps null
sets to null sets.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
In dimension $k$ the Hausdorff measure $\mu_H^k$ is a Haar measure, so it has the null sets of
Lebesgue measure, and a $K$-Lipschitz map $f$ has $\mu_H^k(f(N)) \leq K^k \mu_H^k(N)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Change of chart]
\label{thm:change-of-chart}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.lipschitzWith_proj_graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.finrank_hyperplane_eq_finrank, Rd.LipschitzGraph.ae_proj_graphMap,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_ae_eq_trace_comp}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-partial-sums, thm:trace-continuous, lem:lipschitz-null}
Let $s > 0$, $s + 1/p < L$ and
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. For almost every
$x' \in H_1$ such that $\gamma_1(x')$ lies on the graph of $\Omega_2$, that is
$\gamma_2(P_2 \gamma_1(x')) = \gamma_1(x')$,
\[
  \operatorname{Tr}_1 F(x') = \operatorname{Tr}_2 F(P_2 \gamma_1(x')) .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The traces of the continuous $G_J$ are $G_J \circ \gamma_1$ and $G_J \circ \gamma_2$
(\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}), and they converge to $\operatorname{Tr}_1 F$ and $\operatorname{Tr}_2 F$
(\Cref{lem:trace-partial-sums}); a common subsequence converges almost everywhere on $H_1$ and on
$H_2$. The map $P_1 \circ \gamma_2$ is Lipschitz with constant $1 + M_2$, and $H_1$, $H_2$ both have
dimension $n - 1$, so it maps the exceptional null set of $H_2$ to a null set of $H_1$
(\Cref{lem:lipschitz-null}). Outside of it, $G_J(\gamma_1(x')) = G_J(\gamma_2(P_2 \gamma_1(x')))$
converges to both values.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Traces of products with smooth functions]
\label{lem:trace-products}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_mulLp_ae}
\leanok
\uses{thm:change-of-chart, thm:multipliers, thm:trace-continuous, lem:trace-partial-sums}
Let $s > 0$, $s + 1/p < L$, $F$ as in \Cref{thm:change-of-chart}, and $\psi$ smooth with bounded
derivatives up to order $L$. Then $\operatorname{Tr}(\psi F) = (\psi \circ \gamma) \operatorname{Tr} F$
almost everywhere on $H$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\operatorname{Tr}(\psi G_J) = (\psi G_J) \circ \gamma$ and $\operatorname{Tr} G_J = G_J \circ \gamma$
(\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}); $\psi G_J \to \psi F$ in the norm with smoothness $1/p$ by
\Cref{thm:multipliers} and \Cref{lem:partial-sums-approx}, and subsequences converge almost
everywhere.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Traces of finite sums]
\label{lem:trace-sums}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_zero, Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_finsetSum,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_add_le, Rd.LipschitzGraph.intrinsicNorm_finsetSum_lt_top,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.trace_finsetSum_of_intrinsicNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:trace-linear, lem:pasting}
For $s > 0$ and summands $F_i$ with finite norms
$\|F_i\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F_i, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$, or with finite intrinsic
norms of smoothness $s + 1/p$,
$\operatorname{Tr} \sum_i F_i = \sum_i \operatorname{Tr} F_i$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induction on the number of summands with \Cref{lem:trace-linear}; finite sums keep the norms finite
(\Cref{lem:pasting}), and the intrinsic norms are subadditive.
\end{proof}

\section{Traces on bounded Lipschitz domains}

In this section $\Omega$ is a bounded Lipschitz domain with boundary $\Gamma$, boundary balls
$B_j$ and graph domains $\Omega_j$ (\Cref{def:bounded-lipschitz}), with hyperplanes $H_j$, graph
maps $\gamma_j$, projections $P_j$ and traces $\operatorname{Tr}_j$.

\begin{lemma}[The boundary in the charts]
\label{lem:boundary-charts}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.phi_proj, Rd.LipschitzGraph.graphMap_proj_eq_iff,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.mem_frontier_domain_iff, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.mem_frontier_iff,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.graphMap_mem_frontier, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.graphMap_proj_graphMap}
\leanok
\uses{def:bounded-lipschitz, def:graph-map}
The boundary of a Lipschitz graph domain is its graph
$\{y : \langle y, e \rangle = \phi(y)\} = \{y : \gamma(Py) = y\}$. For $y \in B_j$,
$y \in \Gamma$ if and only if $\gamma_j(P_j y) = y$. Hence a boundary point $\gamma_j(x')$ in
$B_j \cap B_k$ satisfies $\gamma_k(P_k \gamma_j(x')) = \gamma_j(x')$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
A point of the graph is the limit of the points $y + te \in \Omega$, $t > 0$, since
$\phi(y + te) = \phi(y)$, and it is not in the open set $\Omega$. The boundary is local in open sets,
and $\Omega \cap B_j = \Omega_j \cap B_j$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Resolutions of unity on the boundary]
\label{def:boundary-partition}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.toComplex,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.contDiff_toComplex,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.mem_ball_of_ne_zero,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.toComplex_of_eq_zero,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.sum_toComplex_eq_one,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.hasCompactSupport_toComplex,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.nonempty_boundaryPartition}
\leanok
\uses{lem:resolution-of-unity}
A resolution of unity on the boundary consists of smooth $\psi_j$ with
$\operatorname{supp} \psi_j \subseteq B_j$ and $\sum_j \psi_j = 1$ on $\Gamma$. It exists by
\Cref{lem:resolution-of-unity}, and the $\psi_j$ have compact support.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cut-offs]
\label{lem:boundary-cutoffs}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.BoundaryPartition.exists_cutoff}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-partition}
For each $j$ there is a smooth $\zeta_j$ with compact support, $\zeta_j \neq 0$ only in $B_j$, and
$\zeta_j = 1$ where $\psi_j \neq 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\operatorname{supp} \psi_j$ is closed in $B_j = B(c_j, \rho_j)$, so it lies in some $B(c_j, r)$ with
$r < \rho_j$; take a bump function equal to $1$ on $\overline{B}(c_j, r)$ with support $B_j$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Almost everywhere on the boundary]
\label{def:ae-boundary}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.aeBoundary, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.eventually_aeBoundary_iff,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.eventually_aeBoundary_of_chart}
\leanok
\uses{lem:boundary-charts, thm:change-of-chart}
A property holds almost everywhere on $\Gamma$ if for every $j$ it holds at $\gamma_j(x')$ for almost
every $x' \in H_j$ with $\gamma_j(x') \in B_j$. A property of $\gamma_k(x')$ for almost every
$x' \in H_k$ with $\gamma_k(x') \in B_k$ holds almost everywhere on $\Gamma \cap B_k$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Besov spaces on the boundary]
\label{def:boundary-besov}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.MemBoundaryBesov, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.MemBoundaryBesov.congr}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-partition, def:ae-boundary}
A function $g$ belongs to $B^s_{p,q}(\Gamma)$ if for every $j$ the chart piece
$(\psi_j g) \circ \gamma_j$ agrees almost everywhere on $H_j$ with some $G_j \in L_p(H_j)$ with
$\|G_j\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_1(G_j, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. This only depends on $g$
almost everywhere on $\Gamma$.
\end{definition}

This is \cite[Definition 3.3]{SchneiderVybiral} with Lebesgue measure on the hyperplanes and first
differences, which describe $B^s_{p,q}(H_j)$ for $0 < s < 1$.

\begin{definition}[The boundary trace]
\label{def:boundary-trace}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.boundaryTrace}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-partition, thm:trace-graph}
The boundary trace of $F \in L_p(V)$ is
$\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F(y) = \sum_k \psi_k(y) \operatorname{Tr}_k F(P_k y)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The boundary trace in the charts]
\label{thm:boundary-trace-charts}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.boundaryTrace_graphMap,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.boundaryTrace_eventuallyEq,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.boundaryTrace_eventuallyEq_of_continuous}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-trace, thm:change-of-chart, lem:boundary-charts, def:ae-boundary,
  thm:trace-continuous}
Let $s > 0$, $s + 1/p < L$ and
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. Then
$\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F(\gamma_j(x')) = \operatorname{Tr}_j F(x')$ for almost every $x' \in H_j$
with $\gamma_j(x') \in B_j$. Hence $\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F$ does not depend on the resolution of
unity up to null sets of $\Gamma$, and $\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F = G$ almost everywhere on $\Gamma$
if $F$ has a continuous representative $G$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If $\psi_k(\gamma_j(x')) \neq 0$, then $\gamma_j(x') \in B_k$ lies on the graph of $\Omega_k$
(\Cref{lem:boundary-charts}), and
$\operatorname{Tr}_k F(P_k \gamma_j(x')) = \operatorname{Tr}_j F(x')$ for almost every such $x'$
(\Cref{thm:change-of-chart}); and $\sum_k \psi_k = 1$ on $\Gamma$. For continuous $F$,
\Cref{thm:trace-continuous}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Locality of the boundary trace]
\label{lem:boundary-trace-locality}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.boundaryTrace_congr}
\leanok
\uses{thm:boundary-trace-charts, lem:trace-locality}
For $F_1$, $F_2$ as in \Cref{thm:boundary-trace-charts} with $F_1 = F_2$ almost everywhere on
$\Omega$, $\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F_1 = \operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F_2$ almost everywhere on $\Gamma$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
In the chart $j$ both are the traces on $\Omega_j$ (\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-charts}), and
$F_1 = F_2$ on $\Omega_j \cap B_j = \Omega \cap B_j$, so \Cref{lem:trace-locality} with $U = B_j$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The boundary trace is bounded]
\label{thm:boundary-trace-bounded}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.trace_mulLp_ae_boundaryTrace,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.memBoundaryBesov_boundaryTrace}
\leanok
\uses{thm:boundary-trace-charts, lem:trace-products, thm:trace-graph, thm:multipliers,
  def:boundary-besov}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $s + 1/p < L$ and
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$. Then
$\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F \in B^s_{p,q}(\Gamma)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The chart piece $(\psi_j \operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F) \circ \gamma_j$ is
$(\psi_j \circ \gamma_j) \operatorname{Tr}_j F = \operatorname{Tr}_j (\psi_j F)$ almost everywhere
(\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-charts}, \Cref{lem:trace-products}), and $\psi_j F$ has a finite norm
(\Cref{thm:multipliers}), so \Cref{thm:trace-graph} applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The extension from the boundary]
\label{thm:boundary-trace-extension}
\lean{Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_boundaryTrace_eq}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-besov, lem:boundary-cutoffs, thm:graph-trace-extension, thm:trace-extension,
  lem:moduli-orders, lem:trace-products, thm:change-of-chart, lem:trace-sums,
  thm:boundary-trace-charts}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \geq 2$. Every
$g \in B^s_{p,q}(\Gamma)$ is $\operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F$ almost everywhere on $\Gamma$ for some $F$
with $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Let $G_j$ be the chart pieces of $g$, $E_j$ the extensions of the graph domains
(\Cref{thm:graph-trace-extension}, \Cref{lem:moduli-orders}) and $\zeta_j$ the cut-offs of
\Cref{lem:boundary-cutoffs}, and $F = \sum_j \zeta_j E_j G_j$. In the chart $k$, at almost every
$x'$ with $y = \gamma_k(x') \in B_k$,
$\operatorname{Tr}_k(\zeta_j E_j G_j)(x') = \zeta_j(y) \operatorname{Tr}_k(E_j G_j)(x')$
(\Cref{lem:trace-products}). If $\zeta_j(y) \neq 0$, then $y \in B_j$, and by the change of chart
(\Cref{thm:change-of-chart}) and \Cref{thm:trace-extension} this is
$\zeta_j(y) G_j(P_j y) = \zeta_j(y) \psi_j(y) g(y) = \psi_j(y) g(y)$; otherwise $\psi_j(y) = 0$.
Summing (\Cref{lem:trace-sums}), $\operatorname{Tr}_k F(x') = g(y)$, and
\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-charts} concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace space of a bounded Lipschitz domain]
\label{thm:boundary-trace-space}
\lean{Rd.add_one_div_toReal_lt, Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.exists_boundaryTrace_eq_iff,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.memBoundaryBesov_iff_of_boundaryPartition,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.IsBoundaryTrace, Rd.ae_eq_of_indicatorLp_eq,
  Rd.BoundedLipschitzDomain.memBoundaryBesov_iff_exists_isBoundaryTrace}
\leanok
\uses{thm:boundary-trace-bounded, thm:boundary-trace-extension, lem:boundary-trace-locality,
  thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov, lem:restriction-moduli}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < 1$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \geq 2$. Then
$g \in B^s_{p,q}(\Gamma)$ if and only if $g = \operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F$ almost everywhere on $\Gamma$
for some $F$ with $\|F\|_p + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(F, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, if and
only if $g$ is the boundary trace of some $f$ with
$\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{k(s + 1/p)} \omega_L(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, that
is, $g = \operatorname{Tr}_\Gamma F$ almost everywhere on $\Gamma$ for every such $F$ that agrees with
$f$ on $\Omega$. In particular $B^s_{p,q}(\Gamma)$ does not depend on the resolution of unity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
This is \cite[Theorem 4.11]{SchneiderVybiral} for $p \geq 1$. The first equivalence is
\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-bounded} and \Cref{thm:boundary-trace-extension}. For the second, the
functions with a finite intrinsic norm on $\Omega$ are the restrictions of such $F$
(\Cref{thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov}), and all of them have the same boundary trace
(\Cref{lem:boundary-trace-locality}). Both resolutions of unity give the space of boundary traces,
and the boundary traces agree almost everywhere on $\Gamma$ (\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-charts}).
\end{proof}

\chapter{Triebel--Lizorkin spaces}
\label{ch:triebel-lizorkin}

Besov quasi-norms take the $L_p$ norm of every Littlewood--Paley block and then the $\ell_q$ norm
over the levels. Triebel--Lizorkin quasi-norms take the two norms in the other order: the $\ell_q$
norm over the levels at every point, and then the $L_p$ norm. This chapter starts with the
comparison of the two mixed norms on an arbitrary measure space.

\section{Mixed norms}

Throughout this section $(X, \mu)$ is a measure space, $I$ a countable set, and
$g = (g_i)_{i \in I}$ a family of measurable functions $g_i : X \to [0, \infty]$. The two mixed
norms of $g$ are
\[
  \|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)} = \Bigl\| x \mapsto \|(g_i(x))_{i \in I}\|_{\ell_q} \Bigr\|_{L_p},
  \qquad
  \|g\|_{\ell_q(L_p)} = \bigl\| (\|g_i\|_{L_p})_{i \in I} \bigr\|_{\ell_q},
\]
with the $\ell_q$ norm of \Cref{def:lq-norm}. All functions take values in $[0, \infty]$, so
every integral, series and norm has a value and no integrability conditions appear.

\begin{lemma}[{Homogeneity, powers and measurability in $[0, \infty]$}]
\label{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}
\lean{MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_const_mul_ennreal, MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_rpow_eq_lintegral,
  MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_fun_rpow, ENNReal.lqNorm_fun_rpow, MeasureTheory.aemeasurable_lqNorm_comp}
\leanok
\uses{def:lq-norm}
For every measurable $h : X \to [0, \infty]$, every $c \in [0, \infty]$, every
$p \in [0, \infty]$ and every $r > 0$,
\[
  \|c h\|_p = c \, \|h\|_p, \qquad \|h^r\|_p = \|h\|_{pr}^r,
\]
and $\|h\|_p^p = \int h^p \, d\mu$ if $0 < p < \infty$.

For every family $a$ in $[0, \infty]$ indexed by $I$, every $u \in [0, \infty]$ and every
$r > 0$, $\|(a_i^r)_i\|_{\ell_u} = \|a\|_{\ell_{ur}}^r$.

For every $q \in [0, \infty]$ the function $x \mapsto \|(g_i(x))_i\|_{\ell_q}$ is measurable.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $0 < p < \infty$ the norm is $(\int h^p)^{1/p}$, and constants leave the integral; for
$p = \infty$ the essential supremum is homogeneous. The power rule is Mathlib's
\texttt{eLpNorm\_enorm\_rpow}, and for the counting measure on $I$ it is the statement on
$\ell_u$ norms. The pointwise $\ell_q$ norm is a countable supremum for $q = \infty$ and the
$1/q$-th power of a series of measurable functions for $0 < q < \infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Monotone convergence and Minkowski's inequality for series in $L_p$]
\label{lem:lp-series-minkowski}
\lean{MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_iSup_of_directed, MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_tsum_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}
For every countable directed family $(F_b)_b$ of measurable functions $F_b : X \to [0, \infty]$
and every $p \in [0, \infty]$,
$\|\sup_b F_b\|_p = \sup_b \|F_b\|_p$. For every $1 \leq p \leq \infty$,
\[
  \Bigl\| \sum_{i \in I} g_i \Bigr\|_p \leq \sum_{i \in I} \|g_i\|_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $0 < p < \infty$, $\|\sup_b F_b\|_p^p = \int \sup_b F_b^p = \sup_b \int F_b^p$ by the
monotone convergence theorem for directed families; for $p = \infty$, almost everywhere
$F_b \leq \|F_b\|_\infty$ for all $b$ at once, since there are countably many. The series is the
supremum of its finite partial sums, a directed family, and Minkowski's inequality bounds the
norm of each partial sum by $\sum_{i \in I} \|g_i\|_p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Minkowski's integral inequality for $\ell_u$-valued functions]
\label{lem:minkowski-integral-lq}
\lean{ENNReal.rpow_eq_mul_rpow_sub_one, ENNReal.one_lt_toReal, ENNReal.le_rpow_sum_rpow,
  MeasureTheory.sum_rpow_lintegral_le_mul, MeasureTheory.rpow_sum_rpow_lintegral_le,
  MeasureTheory.lqNorm_lintegral_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:lq-norm}
For every $1 \leq u \leq \infty$,
\[
  \Bigl\| \Bigl( \int_X g_i \, d\mu \Bigr)_{i \in I} \Bigr\|_{\ell_u}
  \leq \int_X \|(g_i(x))_{i \in I}\|_{\ell_u} \, d\mu(x) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $u = 1$ both sides are equal by Tonelli's theorem, and for $u = \infty$ the supremum of the
integrals is at most the integral of the supremum. Let $1 < u < \infty$ with conjugate exponent
$u'$, let $S \subseteq I$ be finite, $a_i = \int g_i$ and $T = \sum_{i \in S} a_i^u$. Then
\[
  T = \sum_{i \in S} a_i \, a_i^{u-1}
  = \int \sum_{i \in S} g_i(x) \, a_i^{u-1} \, d\mu(x)
  \leq \int \Bigl( \sum_{i \in S} g_i^u \Bigr)^{1/u} d\mu \cdot T^{1/u'}
\]
by H\"older's inequality at every point, since $(u - 1) u' = u$. If $0 < T < \infty$, dividing by
$T^{1/u'}$ gives $T^{1/u} \leq \int (\sum_{i \in S} g_i^u)^{1/u}$; if $T = \infty$, some $a_i$ is
infinite and so is the right side, because $g_i \leq (\sum_{j \in S} g_j^u)^{1/u}$. The right side
is at most $\int \|(g_i)_i\|_{\ell_u}$, and the supremum over $S$ gives the claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Comparison of mixed norms]
\label{thm:mixed-norms}
\lean{MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_eq_lqNorm_eLpNorm,
  MeasureTheory.lqNorm_eLpNorm_le_eLpNorm_lqNorm, MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_le_lqNorm_eLpNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative, lem:lp-series-minkowski, lem:minkowski-integral-lq,
  lem:lq-basic}
For every $0 < p < \infty$,
$\|g\|_{L_p(\ell_p)} = \|g\|_{\ell_p(L_p)}$. For all $0 < p \leq q \leq \infty$,
$\|g\|_{\ell_q(L_p)} \leq \|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)}$. For all $0 < q \leq p < \infty$,
$\|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)} \leq \|g\|_{\ell_q(L_p)}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The $p$-th powers of both sides of the identity are $\sum_i \int g_i^p$, by Tonelli's theorem
for series. For $p \leq q < \infty$ put $U = q/p \geq 1$; by \Cref{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}
\[
  \|g\|_{\ell_q(L_p)}^p = \Bigl\| \Bigl( \int g_i^p \Bigr)_i \Bigr\|_{\ell_U}
  \leq \int \|(g_i^p)_i\|_{\ell_U} = \|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)}^p
\]
by \Cref{lem:minkowski-integral-lq}; for $q = \infty$ each $g_i$ is at most the pointwise
supremum. For $q \leq p$ put $U = p/q \geq 1$; then
\[
  \|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)}^q = \Bigl\| \sum_i g_i^q \Bigr\|_U \leq \sum_i \|g_i^q\|_U
  = \sum_i \|g_i\|_p^q = \|g\|_{\ell_q(L_p)}^q
\]
by \Cref{lem:lp-series-minkowski}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The mixed-norm sandwich]
\label{cor:mixed-norm-sandwich}
\lean{MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_le_lqNorm_min, MeasureTheory.lqNorm_max_le_eLpNorm_lqNorm,
  MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_const_mul_enorm, MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_weighted_eq,
  MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_weighted_le_min, MeasureTheory.lqNorm_max_weighted_le,
  MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_dyadicWeight_eq, MeasureTheory.eLpNorm_lqNorm_dyadicWeight_le_min,
  MeasureTheory.lqNorm_max_dyadicWeight_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:mixed-norms, lem:lp-norm-nonnegative, lem:lq-basic}
For all $1 \leq p < \infty$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$,
\[
  \|g\|_{\ell_{\max(p,q)}(L_p)} \leq \|g\|_{L_p(\ell_q)} \leq \|g\|_{\ell_{\min(p,q)}(L_p)} .
\]
For every normed group $E$, all measurable functions $u_j : X \to E$ and all weights
$w_j \in [0, \infty]$, indexed by $j \in I$, the family $g_j = w_j \|u_j(\cdot)\|_E$ satisfies
$\|g_j\|_p = w_j \|u_j\|_p$ for every $p$, the two inequalities above, and
$\|g\|_{L_p(\ell_p)} = \|g\|_{\ell_p(L_p)}$ for $0 < p < \infty$. This holds in particular for
$I = \N$ and the weights $w_j = 2^{js}$ with $s \in \R$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $p \leq q$ the lower bound is \Cref{thm:mixed-norms}, and the upper bound follows from the
pointwise inequality $\|\cdot\|_{\ell_q} \leq \|\cdot\|_{\ell_p}$ of \Cref{lem:lq-basic} and the
identity $L_p(\ell_p) = \ell_p(L_p)$. For $q \leq p$ the roles are exchanged. The weighted family
is the case $g_j = w_j \|u_j(\cdot)\|_E$, by homogeneity (\Cref{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}).
\end{proof}

With $u_j$ the Littlewood--Paley blocks of a function $f$ and $w_j = 2^{js}$, the corollary
places the Triebel--Lizorkin quasi-norm between the Besov quasi-norms with $\ell_{\max(p,q)}$
and $\ell_{\min(p,q)}$, with equality for $q = p$.

\begin{lemma}[Limits of pointwise bounds]
\label{lem:limits-of-pointwise-bounds}
\lean{MeasureTheory.ae_le_of_le_add_of_tendsto_eLpNorm}
\leanok
For every $0 < p \leq \infty$, all measurable $u, v : X \to [0, \infty]$ and every sequence of
functions $w_J : X \to [0, \infty]$ with $u \leq v + w_J$ almost everywhere for every $J$ and
$\|w_J\|_p \to 0$, we have $u \leq v$ almost everywhere.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The truncated difference $(u - v)_+$ is measurable and at most $w_J$ almost everywhere, so
$\|(u - v)_+\|_p \leq \|w_J\|_p$ for every $J$. Hence $\|(u - v)_+\|_p = 0$, and $(u - v)_+ = 0$
almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

This is how pointwise bounds pass to limits in $L_p$ for functions with values in
$[0, \infty]$; Mathlib derives convergence in measure from convergence in $L_p$ for normed
groups.

\section{The spaces $F^s_{p,q}$ and the Besov sandwich}

As in \Cref{ch:rd}, $V$ is a finite-dimensional real inner product space with Lebesgue measure
and $L_p = L_p(V)$ for $1 \leq p < \infty$. The Littlewood--Paley kernels $\psi_j$ are those of
\Cref{def:lp-kernels}, and the Besov space $\Besov{s}{p}{q}$ is the approximation space $A^s_q$
of the band-limited scheme in $L_p$ (\Cref{thm:lp-band}).

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise bound of a convolution]
\label{lem:conv-pointwise-bound}
\lean{Rd.enorm_conv_le_of_enorm_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:conv, lem:weighted-jensen}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, every measurable $K : V \to \C$ and every $B \in [0, \infty]$ with
$|K(y)| \leq B$ for all $y \in V$, every measurable $f : V \to \C$ and every $x \in V$,
\[
  |(K * f)(x)| \leq \|K\|_1^{1 - 1/p} \, B^{1/p} \, \|f\|_p .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By the weighted Jensen inequality (\Cref{lem:weighted-jensen}) with the weight $|K|$,
\[
  \Bigl( \int |K(y)| \, |f(x - y)| \, dy \Bigr)^p
  \leq \|K\|_1^{p-1} \int |K(y)| \, |f(x - y)|^p \, dy ,
\]
and the last integral is at most $B \|f\|_p^p$ by the translation invariance of Lebesgue
measure. Take the $p$-th root.
\end{proof}

The bound covers $p = 1$, where it reads $|(K * f)(x)| \leq B \|f\|_1$.

\begin{definition}[Triebel--Lizorkin spaces]
\label{def:triebel-lizorkin}
\lean{Rd.blockFun, Rd.lpBlock_ae_eq_blockFun, Rd.aestronglyMeasurable_blockFun,
  Rd.enorm_lpBlock_eq_eLpNorm_blockFun, Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm, Rd.MemTriebelLizorkin,
  Rd.aemeasurable_lqNorm_blockFun, Rd.littlewoodPaleyNorm_eq_lqNorm_eLpNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-blocks, def:conv, def:lq-norm}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. For $f \in L_p$ and $j \in \N$ the
pointwise block is $\psi_j * f$, the convolution integral at every point of $V$; it represents
the Littlewood--Paley block $\Delta_j f \in L_p$. The Triebel--Lizorkin quasi-norm of $f$ is
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} = \Bigl\| x \mapsto \bigl\| (2^{js} |(\psi_j * f)(x)|)_{j \in \N}
  \bigr\|_{\ell_q} \Bigr\|_{L_p},
\]
and $F^s_{p,q}$ consists of the $f \in L_p$ with $\|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} < \infty$.
\end{definition}

The Littlewood--Paley quasi-norm of the Besov spaces (\Cref{def:lp-blocks}) takes the two norms
in the other order, $\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q} = \|(2^{js} \|\psi_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$; in the
notation of the mixed norms, $\|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}}$ is an $L_p(\ell_q)$ norm and
$\|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,q}$ an $\ell_q(L_p)$ norm of the same family.

\begin{lemma}[The pointwise blocks]
\label{lem:tl-pointwise-blocks}
\lean{Rd.iSup_enorm_psi_lt_top, Rd.eLpNorm_psi_mul_iSup_psi_ne_top, Rd.enorm_blockFun_le,
  Rd.blockFun_add_ae, Rd.blockFun_smul}
\leanok
\uses{def:triebel-lizorkin, lem:conv-pointwise-bound, lem:lp-kernels-basic, lem:conv-assoc}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, all $f, g \in L_p$, every $c \in \C$, every $j \in \N$ and every
$x \in V$,
\[
  |(\psi_j * f)(x)| \leq \|\psi_j\|_1^{1 - 1/p} \bigl( \sup |\psi_j| \bigr)^{1/p} \|f\|_p
\]
with $\|\psi_j\|_1^{1 - 1/p} ( \sup |\psi_j| )^{1/p} < \infty$, and
$(\psi_j * (c f))(x) = c \, (\psi_j * f)(x)$. For almost every $x \in V$,
$(\psi_j * (f + g))(x) = (\psi_j * f)(x) + (\psi_j * g)(x)$ for all $j \in \N$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The bound is \Cref{lem:conv-pointwise-bound}; its constant is finite since $\psi_j$ is
integrable and bounded (\Cref{lem:lp-kernels-basic}). Homogeneity holds for the integrals, and
additivity holds almost everywhere for each $j$ by \Cref{lem:conv-assoc}, hence for all $j$ at
once outside a countable union of null sets.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Elementary properties]
\label{prop:tl-elementary}
\lean{Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_add_le, Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_smul, Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_zero,
  Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_mono_exponent, Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_mono_smoothness,
  Rd.MemTriebelLizorkin.mono_exponent, Rd.MemTriebelLizorkin.mono_smoothness,
  Rd.MemTriebelLizorkin.add, Rd.MemTriebelLizorkin.smul, Rd.triebelLizorkinSpace,
  Rd.mem_triebelLizorkinSpace}
\leanok
\uses{def:triebel-lizorkin, lem:tl-pointwise-blocks, lem:lq-basic, lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, all $s \leq t$ in $\R$, all $1 \leq q \leq r \leq \infty$, all
$f, g \in L_p$ and every $c \in \C$,
\[
  \|f + g\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} + \|g\|_{F^s_{p,q}}, \qquad
  \|c f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} = |c| \, \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}},
\]
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,r}} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}}, \qquad \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|_{F^t_{p,q}} .
\]
In particular $F^s_{p,q}$ is a linear subspace of $L_p$, $F^s_{p,q} \subseteq F^s_{p,r}$ and
$F^t_{p,q} \subseteq F^s_{p,q}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At almost every point the blocks of $f + g$ are the sums of the blocks of $f$ and $g$
(\Cref{lem:tl-pointwise-blocks}), so the triangle inequality in $\ell_q$
(\Cref{lem:lq-basic}) and then in $L_p$ gives the first claim. The blocks of $c f$ are $c$ times
those of $f$, and $|c|$ leaves the $\ell_q$ and the $L_p$ norm
(\Cref{lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}). The monotonicity follows from
$\|\cdot\|_{\ell_r} \leq \|\cdot\|_{\ell_q}$ and $2^{js} \leq 2^{jt}$ at every point.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$F^s_{p,p} = \Besov{s}{p}{p}$]
\label{thm:tl-diagonal}
\lean{Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_eq_littlewoodPaleyNorm, Rd.memTriebelLizorkin_iff_memApprox,
  Rd.memTriebelLizorkin_iff_modulus}
\leanok
\uses{def:triebel-lizorkin, cor:mixed-norm-sandwich, thm:lp-band, thm:rd-besov}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, every $s \in \R$ and every $f \in L_p$,
$\|f\|_{F^s_{p,p}} = \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,p}$. If moreover $s > 0$, then $f \in F^s_{p,p}$ if
and only if $f \in \Besov{s}{p}{p}$, and for every $r \in \N$ with $s < r$, $f \in F^s_{p,p}$ if
and only if $\|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_p} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The $L_p$ norm of the pointwise block $\psi_j * f$ is $\|\Delta_j f\|_p$, so the identity is
$\|g\|_{L_p(\ell_p)} = \|g\|_{\ell_p(L_p)}$ for $g_j = 2^{js} |\psi_j * f|$
(\Cref{cor:mixed-norm-sandwich}). The two characterisations are \Cref{thm:lp-band} and
\Cref{thm:rd-besov} with $q = p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The Besov sandwich]
\label{thm:tl-besov-sandwich}
\lean{Rd.littlewoodPaleyNorm_max_le_triebelLizorkinNorm,
  Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_le_littlewoodPaleyNorm_min, Rd.approxNormDyadic_le_triebelLizorkinNorm,
  Rd.triebelLizorkinNorm_le_approxNormDyadic, Rd.memApprox_of_memTriebelLizorkin,
  Rd.memTriebelLizorkin_of_memApprox}
\leanok
\uses{def:triebel-lizorkin, cor:mixed-norm-sandwich, thm:lp-band, def:dyadic-norm}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, every $s \in \R$, every $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and every
$f \in L_p$,
\[
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,\max(p,q)} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,\min(p,q)} .
\]
If $s \geq 0$, then
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq 8 \cdot 8^s \, \|\Phi\|_1 \, \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_{\min(p,q)}}
\]
with the band-limited scheme. If $s > 0$, then
\[
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_{\max(p,q)}} \leq 2 (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} \, \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}}
\]
and
\[
  \Besov{s}{p}{\min(p,q)} \subseteq F^s_{p,q} \subseteq \Besov{s}{p}{\max(p,q)} .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The first two inequalities are \Cref{cor:mixed-norm-sandwich} for the family
$g_j = 2^{js} |\psi_j * f|$, whose $L_p$ norms are $2^{js} \|\Delta_j f\|_p$. The bounds by the
approximation norms follow with \Cref{thm:lp-band} at the exponents $\max(p,q)$ and
$\min(p,q)$, and so do the inclusions.
\end{proof}

For $q = 2$ the theorem places $F^s_{p,2}$ between $\Besov{s}{p}{\min(p,2)}$ and
$\Besov{s}{p}{\max(p,2)}$: for $p \geq 2$ between $\Besov{s}{p}{2}$ and $\Besov{s}{p}{p}$, for
$p \leq 2$ between $\Besov{s}{p}{p}$ and $\Besov{s}{p}{2}$.

\section{Triebel--Lizorkin spaces on the torus}

The Littlewood--Paley blocks on the torus are those of \Cref{def:torus-lp-blocks}, and
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\T^d)$ is the approximation space of the trigonometric polynomials in
$L_p(\T^d)$, $1 \leq p < \infty$.

\begin{definition}[Triebel--Lizorkin spaces on the torus]
\label{def:torus-triebel-lizorkin}
\lean{Torus.blockFun, Torus.lpBlock_ae_eq_blockFun, Torus.aestronglyMeasurable_blockFun,
  Torus.enorm_lpBlock_eq_eLpNorm_blockFun, Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm, Torus.MemTriebelLizorkin,
  Torus.aemeasurable_lqNorm_blockFun, Torus.littlewoodPaleyNorm_eq_lqNorm_eLpNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:torus-lp-blocks, def:torus-econv, def:lq-norm}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. For $f \in L_p(\T^d)$ and
$j \in \N$ the pointwise block is $(\psi_j * f)(t) = \int_{\R^d} \psi_j(y) f(t - \pi y) \, dy$
at every $t \in \T^d$; it represents the block $\Delta_j f \in L_p(\T^d)$. The
Triebel--Lizorkin quasi-norm of $f$ is
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} = \Bigl\| t \mapsto \bigl\| (2^{js} |(\psi_j * f)(t)|)_{j \in \N}
  \bigr\|_{\ell_q} \Bigr\|_{L_p(\T^d)},
\]
and $F^s_{p,q}(\T^d)$ consists of the $f \in L_p(\T^d)$ with $\|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} < \infty$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Elementary properties on the torus]
\label{prop:torus-tl-elementary}
\lean{Torus.blockFun_add_ae, Torus.blockFun_smul_ae, Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_add_le,
  Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_smul, Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_zero,
  Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_mono_exponent, Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_mono_smoothness,
  Torus.MemTriebelLizorkin.mono_exponent, Torus.MemTriebelLizorkin.mono_smoothness,
  Torus.MemTriebelLizorkin.add, Torus.MemTriebelLizorkin.smul, Torus.triebelLizorkinSpace,
  Torus.mem_triebelLizorkinSpace}
\leanok
\uses{def:torus-triebel-lizorkin, lem:torus-econv-young, lem:lq-basic, lem:lp-norm-nonnegative}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, all $s \leq t$ in $\R$, all $1 \leq q \leq r \leq \infty$, all
$f, g \in L_p(\T^d)$ and every $c \in \C$, for almost every $x \in \T^d$ and all $j \in \N$,
\[
  (\psi_j * (f + g))(x) = (\psi_j * f)(x) + (\psi_j * g)(x), \qquad
  (\psi_j * (c f))(x) = c \, (\psi_j * f)(x),
\]
and
\[
  \|f + g\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} + \|g\|_{F^s_{p,q}}, \qquad
  \|c f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} = |c| \, \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}},
\]
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,r}} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}}, \qquad \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|_{F^t_{p,q}} .
\]
In particular $F^s_{p,q}(\T^d)$ is a linear subspace of $L_p(\T^d)$,
$F^s_{p,q}(\T^d) \subseteq F^s_{p,r}(\T^d)$ and $F^t_{p,q}(\T^d) \subseteq F^s_{p,q}(\T^d)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The pointwise block depends on $f$ only through its class, and the convolution integral is
linear in $f$. Since $L_p(\T^d) \subseteq L_1(\T^d)$, the integrals converge absolutely at almost
every point (\Cref{lem:torus-econv-young}), which gives the identities for the pointwise blocks,
for all $j$ at once. The rest is the proof of \Cref{prop:tl-elementary}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$F^s_{p,p}(\T^d) = \Besov{s}{p}{p}(\T^d)$ and the Besov sandwich on the torus]
\label{thm:torus-tl-besov-sandwich}
\lean{Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_eq_littlewoodPaleyNorm, Torus.memTriebelLizorkin_iff_memBesov,
  Torus.memTriebelLizorkin_iff_modulus, Torus.littlewoodPaleyNorm_max_le_triebelLizorkinNorm,
  Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_le_littlewoodPaleyNorm_min,
  Torus.approxNormDyadic_le_triebelLizorkinNorm, Torus.triebelLizorkinNorm_le_approxNormDyadic,
  Torus.memBesov_of_memTriebelLizorkin, Torus.memTriebelLizorkin_of_memBesov}
\leanok
\uses{def:torus-triebel-lizorkin, cor:mixed-norm-sandwich, thm:torus-lp-besov, def:dyadic-norm,
  thm:torus-lp-moduli}
For every $1 \leq p < \infty$, every $s \in \R$, every $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and every
$f \in L_p(\T^d)$,
\[
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,\max(p,q)} \leq \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,\min(p,q)} ,
  \qquad \|f\|_{F^s_{p,p}} = \|f\|^{\mathrm{LP}}_{s,p} .
\]
Let $A$ be the scheme of trigonometric polynomials in $L_p(\T^d)$. If $s \geq 0$, then
\[
  \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} \leq 2 (\#d + 1) \, 2^{(\#d + 1) s} \, \|\Phi\|_1 \,
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_{\min(p,q)}} .
\]
If $s > 0$, then
\[
  \|f\|^{\mathrm{dy}}_{A^s_{\max(p,q)}} \leq 2 \cdot 2^s (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1} \, \|f\|_{F^s_{p,q}} ,
\]
$f \in F^s_{p,p}(\T^d)$ if and only if $f \in \Besov{s}{p}{p}(\T^d)$, for every $r \in \N$ with
$s < r$, $f \in F^s_{p,p}(\T^d)$ if and only if
$\|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_p} < \infty$, and
\[
  \Besov{s}{p}{\min(p,q)}(\T^d) \subseteq F^s_{p,q}(\T^d) \subseteq \Besov{s}{p}{\max(p,q)}(\T^d) .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The $L_p$ norm of the pointwise block $\psi_j * f$ is $\|\Delta_j f\|_p$, so the bounds are
\Cref{cor:mixed-norm-sandwich} for the family $g_j = 2^{js} |\psi_j * f|$ on $\T^d$. The
characterisation of $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\T^d)$ by Littlewood--Paley blocks
(\Cref{thm:torus-lp-besov}) at the exponents $p$, $\max(p,q)$ and $\min(p,q)$ gives the
bounds by the approximation norms and the statements on spaces; the description by moduli of
smoothness is \Cref{thm:torus-lp-moduli} with $q = p$.
\end{proof}

As on $\R^n$, for $q = 2$ the theorem places $F^s_{p,2}(\T^d)$ between
$\Besov{s}{p}{\min(p,2)}(\T^d)$ and $\Besov{s}{p}{\max(p,2)}(\T^d)$.

\section{Dyadic cubes and the Lebesgue differentiation theorem}

The Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition stops at dyadic cubes of every size, so the cells of
\Cref{def:dyadic-cells} are extended to all integer levels. Throughout, $V$ is a
finite-dimensional real inner product space of dimension $n$ with Lebesgue measure $|\cdot|$,
$(b_i)_{i=1}^n$ is the orthonormal basis of \Cref{def:dyadic-cells}, $x_i = \langle b_i, x \rangle$
are the coordinates of $x \in V$, and for a measure $\mu$ and a set $Q$,
$F_Q = \mu(Q)^{-1} \int_Q F \, d\mu$ is the average of $F$ over $Q$ (with $\mu = |\cdot|$ on $V$;
as in Mathlib, $F_Q = 0$ when $\mu(Q)$ is $0$ or $\infty$).

\begin{definition}[Dyadic cubes]
\label{def:dyadic-cubes}
\lean{Rd.zcellIndex, Rd.zcell, Rd.zcellCenter, Rd.zcellIndex_natCast, Rd.zcell_natCast,
  Rd.zcellCenter_natCast, Rd.mem_zcell_zcellIndex, Rd.repr_zcellCenter, Rd.measurable_zcellIndex,
  Rd.measurableSet_zcell, Rd.cubeAt, Rd.mem_cubeAt}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cells}
For $k \in \Z$ and $m \in \Z^n$ the dyadic cube of level $k$ and index $m$ is
$Q_{k,m} = \{x \in V : \lfloor 2^k x_i \rfloor = m_i \text{ for all } i\}$, with centre
$c_{k,m} = \sum_i (m_i + 1/2) 2^{-k} b_i$. Every $x$ lies in the cube $Q_k(x)$ of level $k$ with
index $(\lfloor 2^k x_i \rfloor)_i$. For every $k \in \Z$ and every $m \in \Z^n$ the cube
$Q_{k,m}$ is measurable, and for every $k \in \N$ it is the cell $P_{k,m}$, with the same centre.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Size, partition and volume of cubes]
\label{lem:dyadic-cubes}
\lean{Rd.repr_sub_zcellCenter, Rd.abs_repr_sub_zcellCenter_le, Rd.norm_sub_zcellCenter_le,
  Rd.zcell_subset_closedBall, Rd.ball_subset_zcell, Rd.zcell_disjoint, Rd.iUnion_zcell,
  Rd.zcell_eq_preimage, Rd.volume_zcell, Rd.volume_zcell_pos, Rd.volume_zcell_lt_top,
  Rd.volume_real_zcell}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cells}
For every $k \in \Z$, every $m \in \Z^n$ and every $x \in V$, the $i$-th coordinate of
$x - c_{k,m}$ is $(2^k x_i - m_i - 1/2) 2^{-k}$. For every $k \in \Z$, every $m \in \Z^n$ and
every $x \in Q_{k,m}$, every coordinate of $x - c_{k,m}$ is at most $2^{-k}/2$ in modulus and
$\|x - c_{k,m}\| \leq n 2^{-k}$. For every $k \in \Z$ and every $m \in \Z^n$,
\[
  Q_{k,m} = \{x \in V : m_i 2^{-k} \leq x_i < (m_i + 1) 2^{-k} \text{ for all } i\},
\]
$B(c_{k,m}, 2^{-k}/2) \subseteq Q_{k,m} \subseteq \bar B(c_{k,m}, n 2^{-k})$ and
$|Q_{k,m}| = 2^{-kn}$. For every $k \in \Z$ the cubes of level $k$ are pairwise disjoint and cover
$V$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The bounds are those of \Cref{lem:dyadic-cells} with $2^{-k}$ in place of $2^{-j}$. A point of
the ball has coordinates within less than $2^{-k}/2$ of $(m_i + 1/2) 2^{-k}$, so
$\lfloor 2^k x_i \rfloor = m_i$. The description as a set of inequalities is
$\lfloor t \rfloor = m_i \iff m_i \leq t < m_i + 1$; in the coordinates of the basis the cube is
therefore the box $\prod_i [m_i 2^{-k}, (m_i + 1) 2^{-k})$, and the coordinate map $V \to \R^n$
preserves the volume (Mathlib's \texttt{OrthonormalBasis.measurePreserving\_repr}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Nesting of cubes]
\label{lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}
\lean{Rd.parent, Rd.zcell_succ_subset_parent, Rd.volume_zcell_parent, Rd.volume_zcell_sub,
  Rd.ancestor,
  Rd.ancestor_add, Rd.zcell_subset_ancestor, Rd.zcellIndex_sub_ancestor,
  Rd.zcell_subset_or_disjoint}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes}
For $m \in \Z^n$ let $\hat m = (\lfloor m_i / 2 \rfloor)_i$, and let $m^{(l)}$ be the result of
$l$ applications of $m \mapsto \hat m$. For every $k \in \Z$, every $l \in \N$ and every
$m \in \Z^n$,
\[
  Q_{k+1,m} \subseteq Q_{k,\hat m}, \qquad |Q_{k,\hat m}| = 2^n |Q_{k+1,m}|, \qquad
  Q_{k+l,m} \subseteq Q_{k,m^{(l)}}.
\]
For every $k \in \Z$, every $l \in \N$ and all $m, m' \in \Z^n$,
$|Q_{k-l,m'}| = 2^{ln} |Q_{k,m}|$. For every $k \in \Z$, every $l \in \N$ and every $x \in V$,
the index of $Q_{k-l}(x)$ is $p^{(l)}$, where $p = (\lfloor 2^k x_i \rfloor)_i$ is the index of
$Q_k(x)$. For all integers
$k \leq k'$ and all $m, m' \in \Z^n$, either $Q_{k',m'} \subseteq Q_{k,m}$ or
$Q_{k',m'} \cap Q_{k,m} = \emptyset$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For real $t$, $\lfloor 2^k t \rfloor = \lfloor 2^{k+1} t / 2 \rfloor
= \lfloor \lfloor 2^{k+1} t \rfloor / 2 \rfloor$, which gives the first inclusion; the volumes
are those of \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes}, and the third inclusion follows by induction on $l$. For
$k \leq k'$ the cube $Q_{k',m'}$ lies in the cube of level $k$ with index $m'^{(k'-k)}$, which is
$Q_{k,m}$ or disjoint from it.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Averages over a subset]
\label{lem:average-subset}
\lean{Rd.abs_setAverage_sub_le, Rd.setLAverage_eq_ofReal_setAverage}
\leanok
For every measure space $(X, \mu)$, all sets $Q \subseteq B \subseteq X$ with $\mu(Q) > 0$ and
$\mu(B) < \infty$, every $F : X \to \R$ integrable on $B$ and every $c \in \R$,
\[
  |F_Q - c| \leq \frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(Q)} \, (|F - c|)_B .
\]
For every measure space $(X, \mu)$, every set $Q \subseteq X$ and every $G : X \to [0, \infty]$,
almost everywhere measurable on $Q$ with $\int_Q G \, d\mu < \infty$, the average $G_Q$ equals the
average over $Q$ of the real function equal to $G$ where $G < \infty$ and to $0$ elsewhere.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$F_Q - c = \mu(Q)^{-1} \int_Q (F - c) \, d\mu$, and
$\int_Q |F - c| \, d\mu \leq \int_B |F - c| \, d\mu$. In the second statement $G$ is finite almost
everywhere on $Q$, so both functions have the same integral over $Q$ (Mathlib's
\texttt{toReal\_setLAverage}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lebesgue differentiation along dyadic cubes]
\label{thm:lebesgue-differentiation-cubes}
\lean{Rd.cubeAt_subset_closedBall, Rd.volume_real_closedBall_div_cubeAt,
  Rd.ae_tendsto_average_cubeAt, Rd.ae_le_of_forall_laverage_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes, lem:average-subset}
For every locally integrable $F : V \to \R$ and almost every $x \in V$,
\[
  F_{Q_k(x)} \to F(x) \qquad (k \to \infty, \ k \in \N) .
\]
For every almost everywhere measurable $G : V \to [0, \infty]$ with $\int G < \infty$ and every
$t \in [0, \infty]$, almost every $x \in V$ with $G_{Q_k(x)} \leq t$ for all $k \in \N$
satisfies $G(x) \leq t$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Let $w_k$ be the centre of $Q_k(x)$ and $\delta_k = (n + 1) 2^{-k}$. By \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes},
$Q_k(x)$ lies in the closed ball $\bar B_k = \bar B(w_k, \delta_k)$, which contains $x$, and
$|\bar B_k| = (n + 1)^n |B(0,1)| \, |Q_k(x)|$. By \Cref{lem:average-subset},
\[
  |F_{Q_k(x)} - F(x)| \leq (n + 1)^n |B(0,1)| \, (|F - F(x)|)_{\bar B_k} .
\]
Lebesgue measure is doubling, and Mathlib's Lebesgue differentiation theorem for doubling
measures (\texttt{IsUnifLocDoublingMeasure.\allowbreak ae\_tendsto\_average\_norm\_sub}) shows
that the right side tends to $0$ at almost every $x$, since the radii $\delta_k$ tend to $0$.
For $G$ apply this to the integrable real function $F$ equal to $G$ where $G < \infty$ and to $0$
elsewhere; $G$ is finite almost everywhere, so the averages of $F$ over the cubes are those of $G$
(\Cref{lem:average-subset}), and the bound $t$ passes to the limit.
\end{proof}

\section{The Hardy--Littlewood maximal function}

The maximal function is the tool that turns pointwise bounds of convolutions into $L_p$ bounds
when the $\ell_q$ norm over the levels is taken before the $L_p$ norm. As in the previous section,
$V$ is a finite-dimensional real inner product space of dimension $n$ with Lebesgue measure
$|\cdot|$, and $B(x, r)$ and $\bar B(x, r)$ are the open and the closed ball. All functions in
this section take values in $[0, \infty]$, so every integral has a value; for a function that
is not measurable, $\int$ is the lower Lebesgue integral, as in Mathlib.

\begin{definition}[Hardy--Littlewood maximal function]
\label{def:maximal-function}
\lean{Rd.maximal, Rd.volume_ball_ne_zero, Rd.volume_ball_ne_top, Rd.le_maximal,
  Rd.setLIntegral_ball_le_mul_maximal, Rd.maximal_le_of_forall}
\leanok
For $g : V \to [0, \infty]$ and $x \in V$,
\[
  M g(x) = \sup_{r > 0} \frac{1}{|B(0, r)|} \int_{B(x, r)} g(y) \, dy \in [0, \infty] .
\]
For every $r > 0$, $0 < |B(0, r)| < \infty$. For every $g$, every $x \in V$ and every $r > 0$,
$\int_{B(x,r)} g \leq |B(0,r)| \, M g(x)$, and for every $c \in [0, \infty]$ with
$\int_{B(x,r)} g \leq |B(0,r)| \, c$ for all $r > 0$, $M g(x) \leq c$.
\end{definition}

The normalisation by $|B(0, r)| = |B(x, r)|$ keeps the factor independent of $x$.

\begin{lemma}[Elementary properties of the maximal function]
\label{lem:maximal-function-basic}
\lean{Rd.maximal_mono, Rd.maximal_add_le, Rd.maximal_const_mul, Rd.maximal_le_of_ae_le,
  Rd.maximal_const, Rd.maximal_congr_ae, Rd.maximal_indicator_le, Rd.maximal_iSup_of_monotone,
  Rd.maximal_eq_self_of_subsingleton, Rd.maximal_le_eLpNorm_top,
  Rd.maximal_le_maximal_indicator_add}
\leanok
\uses{def:maximal-function}
For all $g, h : V \to [0, \infty]$, every $x \in V$, every $c \in [0, \infty]$, every
$s \in [0, \infty]$ and every set $S \subseteq V$:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item if $g \leq h$, then $M g \leq M h$; in particular $M(\mathbf 1_S g) \leq M g$;
  \item if $g$ is almost everywhere measurable, then $M(g + h)(x) \leq M g(x) + M h(x)$;
  \item if $c < \infty$, then $M(c g) = c \, M g$; and $M c = c$ for the constant function $c$;
  \item if $g \leq s$ almost everywhere, then $M g \leq s$; in particular $M g \leq \|g\|_\infty$;
  \item if $g = h$ almost everywhere, then $M g = M h$;
  \item if $g$ is measurable, then $M g(x) \leq M(\mathbf 1_{\{g > s\}} g)(x) + s$;
  \item if $n = 0$, then $M g = g$.
\end{enumerate}
For every increasing sequence $(g_N)_{N \in \N}$ of almost everywhere measurable functions
$g_N : V \to [0, \infty]$, $M(\sup_N g_N) = \sup_N M g_N$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Each property holds for the integrals over every ball $B(x, r)$ and passes to the supremum over
$r$: monotonicity and additivity of the integral (additivity needs one measurable summand),
homogeneity, the integral of a constant, and the monotone convergence theorem on each ball,
followed by an exchange of the two suprema. The cut at height $s$ uses
$g \leq \mathbf 1_{\{g > s\}} g + s$. For $n = 0$ every ball of positive radius is the whole
one-point space.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Measurability of the maximal function]
\label{lem:maximal-function-measurable}
\lean{Rd.lowerSemicontinuous_setLIntegral_ball, Rd.lowerSemicontinuous_maximal,
  Rd.measurable_maximal}
\leanok
\uses{def:maximal-function}
For every $g : V \to [0, \infty]$ and every $r \in \R$, the function
$x \mapsto \int_{B(x,r)} g$ is lower semicontinuous. For every $g : V \to [0, \infty]$, $M g$ is
lower semicontinuous, hence measurable.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The ball $B(x_0, r)$ is the increasing union of the balls $B(x_0, r - 1/(m+1))$, so
$\int_{B(x_0, r)} g > a$ implies $\int_{B(x_0, r - 1/(m+1))} g > a$ for some $m$, and this ball
lies in $B(x, r)$ for every $x$ with $\|x - x_0\| < 1/(m+1)$. A supremum of lower semicontinuous
functions is lower semicontinuous, and lower semicontinuous functions are Borel measurable.
\end{proof}

The lemma holds for every $g$; the measurability comes from the open balls.

\begin{lemma}[Convolutions with kernels under radial majorants]
\label{lem:maximal-function-kernels}
\lean{Rd.lintegral_mul_le_maximal_of_ball, Rd.dyadicShell, Rd.measurableSet_dyadicShell,
  Rd.iUnion_dyadicShell, Rd.kernel_le_of_mem_dyadicShell, Rd.volume_ball_two_pow_mul,
  Rd.tsum_two_inv_pow_mul_two_pow, Rd.lintegral_mul_le_maximal_of_decay, Rd.decayMaximalConst,
  Rd.decayMaximalConst_ne_top, Rd.inv_one_add_two_pow_mul_pow_le,
  Rd.lintegral_mul_le_maximal_of_one_add_norm}
\leanok
\uses{def:maximal-function}
For every integer $N > n$ let $c_{n,N} = 2^n \bigl(1 - 2^{-(N - n)}\bigr)^{-1}$ and
$C_{n,N} = |B(0, 1)| \, c_{n,N}$. For every $K : V \to [0, \infty]$, every
$g : V \to [0, \infty]$, every $x \in V$, every $A \in [0, \infty)$ and every $\rho > 0$:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item if $K \leq A \mathbf 1_{B(0, \rho)}$, then
    $\int K(y) g(x - y) \, dy \leq A \, |B(0, \rho)| \, M g(x)$;
  \item if $N > n$ is an integer, $K(y) \leq A$ for $\|y\| \leq \rho$ and
    $K(y) \leq A (\rho / \|y\|)^N$ for $\|y\| > \rho$, then
    $\int K(y) g(x - y) \, dy \leq c_{n,N} \, A \, |B(0, \rho)| \, M g(x)$;
  \item if $N > n$ and $j \geq 0$ are integers and
    $K(y) \leq A \, 2^{jn} (1 + 2^j \|y\|)^{-N}$ for all $y$, then
    $\int K(y) g(x - y) \, dy \leq C_{n,N} \, A \, M g(x)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
(1) The integrand is at most $A \mathbf 1_{B(x, \rho)}(x - y) \, g(x - y)$; translation
invariance gives $A \int_{B(x, \rho)} g \leq A \, |B(0, \rho)| \, M g(x)$. (2) The shells
$S_0 = B(0, 2\rho)$ and $S_i = \{2^i \rho \leq \|y\| < 2^{i+1} \rho\}$, $i \geq 1$, cover $V$,
and $K \leq A 2^{-iN} \mathbf 1_{B(0, 2^{i+1} \rho)}$ on $S_i$. By (1) and
$|B(0, 2^{i+1} \rho)| = 2^{(i+1) n} |B(0, \rho)|$, the integral over $S_i$ is at most
$A \, |B(0, \rho)| \, 2^n 2^{-i(N - n)} M g(x)$, and the geometric series sums to the bound.
(3) is (2) with $\rho = 2^{-j}$: $(1 + 2^j \|y\|)^{-N} \leq 1$, and
$(1 + 2^j \|y\|)^{-N} \leq (2^{-j} / \|y\|)^N$, and $2^{jn} |B(0, 2^{-j})| = |B(0, 1)|$.
\end{proof}

Only the integral of $g$ over subsets of $V$ enters, so the lemma holds for all $K$ and $g$, and
$C_{n,N}$ is the same for every level $j$.

\begin{theorem}[Weighted weak type $(1,1)$]
\label{thm:maximal-weak-type}
\lean{Rd.setLIntegral_closedBall_le_maximal, Rd.mul_setLIntegral_closedBall_le, Rd.bigBallSet,
  Rd.bigBallSet_mono, Rd.setOf_lt_maximal_subset, Rd.mul_setLIntegral_bigBallSet_le,
  Rd.setLIntegral_maximal_gt_le, Rd.volume_maximal_gt_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:maximal-function, lem:maximal-function-basic}
For all $g, w : V \to [0, \infty]$ and every $t \in (0, \infty]$,
\[
  \int_{\{M g > t\}} w(x) \, dx \leq \frac{5^n}{t} \int g(x) \, M w(x) \, dx,
  \qquad
  |\{M g > t\}| \leq \frac{5^n}{t} \int g(x) \, dx .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The second inequality is the first for $w = 1$, since $M 1 = 1$. For $t = \infty$ the level set
is empty. For $t < \infty$ and $R > 0$ let $E_R$ be the set of $x$ with a radius
$0 < r \leq R$ such that $t \, |B(0, r)| < \int_{B(x, r)} g$. If $M g(x) > t$, some radius
$r$ satisfies this, so $\{M g > t\}$ is the increasing union of the sets $E_N$, $N \in \N$, and
it suffices to show $t \int_{E_R} w \leq 5^n \int g \, M w$ for every $R$.

Choose such a radius $r_x \leq R$ for every $x \in E_R$. Vitali's covering lemma (Mathlib's
\texttt{Vitali.\allowbreak exists\_disjoint\_subfamily\_\allowbreak
covering\_enlargement\_closedBall}) gives a
set $U \subseteq E_R$ such that the balls $\bar B(b, r_b)$, $b \in U$, are pairwise disjoint and
the balls $\bar B(b, 4 r_b)$ cover $E_R$; $U$ is countable, since the disjoint open balls
$B(b, r_b)$ are nonempty. For $b \in U$ and $y \in B(b, r_b)$,
$\bar B(b, 4 r_b) \subseteq B(y, 5 r_b)$, so
$W_b = \int_{\bar B(b, 4 r_b)} w \leq 5^n |B(0, r_b)| \, M w(y)$. Hence
\[
  t \, |B(0, r_b)| \, W_b \leq W_b \int_{B(b, r_b)} g
  \leq 5^n |B(0, r_b)| \int_{B(b, r_b)} g \, M w ,
\]
and after division by $|B(0, r_b)|$ the sum over $b \in U$ gives
$t \int_{E_R} w \leq \sum_b t \, W_b \leq 5^n \int g \, M w$, as the balls $B(b, r_b)$ are
disjoint.
\end{proof}

The theorem holds for all $g$ and $w$: the radii are bounded by $R$ on each $E_R$, and the
exhaustion in $R$ takes the place of a bound of the radii by $\int g$.

\begin{lemma}[{Layer cake formula for functions with values in $[0, \infty]$}]
\label{lem:layer-cake}
\lean{ENNReal.lt_toReal_min_natCast_iff, MeasureTheory.lintegral_min_natCast_rpow_eq,
  ENNReal.iSup_min_natCast_rpow, MeasureTheory.lintegral_rpow_eq_lintegral_meas_lt_mul_ennreal}
\leanok
For every measure space $(X, \nu)$, every almost everywhere measurable
$F : X \to [0, \infty]$ and every $p > 0$,
\[
  \int F^p \, d\nu = p \int_0^\infty \nu(\{F > t\}) \, t^{p-1} \, dt .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For the truncation $\min(F, N)$, $N \in \N$, this is Mathlib's layer cake formula for real
functions (\texttt{lintegral\_rpow\_eq\_lintegral\_meas\_lt\_mul}), and for $t > 0$,
$\min(F, N) > t$ holds exactly when $F > t$ and $N > t$, so the right side is the integral
over $(0, N)$. As $N \to \infty$ the left side converges by monotone convergence and the right
side by the increasing union $\bigcup_N (0, N) = (0, \infty)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Integrals of powers]
\label{lem:power-integrals}
\lean{MeasureTheory.lintegral_Ioo_rpow, MeasureTheory.lintegral_Ioi_rpow_eq_top,
  MeasureTheory.lintegral_Ioi_rpow_indicator_lt, MeasureTheory.lintegral_Ioi_rpow_indicator_ge}
\leanok
For every $r > -1$ and every $a \in [0, \infty]$,
\[
  \int_{(0, \infty)} \mathbf 1_{\{t < a\}} \, t^r \, dt = \frac{a^{r+1}}{r + 1} .
\]
For every $r < -1$ and every $a \in [0, \infty]$,
\[
  \int_{(0, \infty)} \mathbf 1_{\{t \geq a\}} \, t^r \, dt = \frac{a^{r+1}}{-(r + 1)} .
\]
Here $\infty^{r+1} = \infty$ and $0^{r+1} = 0$ for $r + 1 > 0$, and $\infty^{r+1} = 0$ and
$0^{r+1} = \infty$ for $r + 1 < 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $0 < a < \infty$ these are the integrals of $t^r$ over $(0, a)$ and $[a, \infty)$ (Mathlib's
\texttt{integral\_rpow} and \texttt{integral\_Ioi\_rpow\_of\_lt}), and the first formula also
holds for $a = 0$. The integral of $t^r$ over $(0, \infty)$ is infinite for every $r$, since
$t^r$ is not integrable on $(0, \infty)$ (Mathlib's \texttt{not\_integrableOn\_Ioi\_rpow}); this
is the first integral for $a = \infty$ and the second for $a = 0$. For $a = \infty$ the second
integral vanishes.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Weighted strong type and the Hardy--Littlewood maximal theorem]
\label{thm:maximal-strong-type}
\lean{Rd.maximalConst, Rd.maximalConst_ne_top, Rd.setLIntegral_maximal_gt_le_indicator,
  Rd.withDensity_maximal_gt_mul_le, Rd.measurable_indicator_half_lt,
  MeasureTheory.lintegral_Ioi_indicator_half_lt_mul_rpow,
  Rd.lintegral_Ioi_lintegral_indicator_mul_rpow,
  Rd.lintegral_maximal_rpow_mul_le_of_measurable, Rd.lintegral_maximal_rpow_mul_le,
  Rd.maximalConst_ne_zero, Rd.eLpNorm_maximal_le, Rd.maximal_lt_top_ae}
\leanok
\uses{thm:maximal-weak-type, lem:maximal-function-basic, lem:maximal-function-measurable,
  lem:layer-cake, lem:power-integrals}
Let $1 < p < \infty$ and $C_{n,p} = p \, 2^p \, 5^n / (p - 1)$. For all almost everywhere
measurable $g, w : V \to [0, \infty]$,
\[
  \int (M g)^p \, w \leq C_{n,p} \int g^p \, M w .
\]
For every $g : V \to [0, \infty]$,
\[
  \|M g\|_p \leq C_{n,p}^{1/p} \|g\|_p .
\]
For every $g : V \to [0, \infty]$ with $\|g\|_p < \infty$, $M g$ is finite almost everywhere.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Changing $g$ or $w$ on a null set changes neither side, so let $g$ and $w$ be measurable, and let
$\nu$ be the measure $w(x) \, dx$. By \Cref{lem:layer-cake},
$\int (M g)^p w = p \int_0^\infty \nu(\{M g > t\}) \, t^{p-1} \, dt$. For $t > 0$,
$M g \leq M(\mathbf 1_{\{g > t/2\}} g) + t/2$ by \Cref{lem:maximal-function-basic}, so
$\{M g > t\} \subseteq \{M(\mathbf 1_{\{g > t/2\}} g) > t/2\}$ and \Cref{thm:maximal-weak-type}
gives
\[
  \nu(\{M g > t\}) \leq \frac{2 \cdot 5^n}{t} \int_{\{g > t/2\}} g \, M w .
\]
By Tonelli's theorem and \Cref{lem:power-integrals} with $r = p - 2 > -1$,
\[
  \int_0^\infty t^{p-2} \int_{\{g > t/2\}} g \, M w \, dx \, dt
  = \int g \, M w \, \frac{(2 g)^{p-1}}{p - 1} \, dx
  = \frac{2^{p-1}}{p - 1} \int g^p \, M w ,
\]
which gives the weighted bound. For $w = 1$, $M w = 1$, and taking $p$-th roots gives the bound
in $L_p$ for almost everywhere measurable $g$; for every other $g$, $\|g\|_p = \infty$ in
Mathlib, and $C_{n,p} > 0$ makes the right side infinite. If $\|g\|_p < \infty$, then
$\int (M g)^p < \infty$, so $M g < \infty$ almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

The weighted bound holds for every almost everywhere measurable $g$, with both sides possibly
infinite; this is what the duality argument for the Fefferman--Stein inequality uses, where it is
applied at an exponent other than $p$.

\section{The Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition}

The Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition splits $V$ at a height $t$ into a union $\Omega$ of disjoint
dyadic cubes, on which $G$ has average comparable to $t$, and its complement, on which $G \leq t$
almost everywhere (for $n \geq 1$, $t > 0$ and an almost everywhere measurable $G$ with
$\int G < \infty$). The Fefferman--Stein inequality applies it to the $\ell_q$ norm of a family
of functions. As in \Cref{def:dyadic-cubes}, $Q_{k,m}$ is the dyadic cube of level $k$ and
index $m$, $m^{(l)}$ the index of its ancestor $l$ levels up (\Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}),
and $G_Q$ the average of $G$ over $Q$. Throughout, $G : V \to [0, \infty]$ and
$t \in [0, \infty]$; a cube $Q$ is \emph{big} if $G_Q > t$.

\begin{lemma}[Coarse cubes are not big]
\label{lem:coarse-cubes-not-big}
\lean{Rd.lt_setLAverage_zcell_iff, Rd.exists_forall_setLAverage_zcell_sub_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}
For every $G : V \to [0, \infty]$, every $t \in [0, \infty]$ and every cube $Q$, $G_Q > t$ holds
if and only if $t \, |Q| < \int_Q G$. If $n \geq 1$, then for every $G : V \to [0, \infty]$ with
$\int G < \infty$, every $t \in (0, \infty]$ and every $k \in \Z$ there is $B \in \N$ such that
$G_{Q_{k-l,m'}} \leq t$ for every integer $l > B$ and every $m' \in \Z^n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes}, $G_Q = |Q|^{-1} \int_Q G$ with $0 < |Q| < \infty$. For $t = \infty$
no cube is big. For $t < \infty$ choose $B \in \N$ with $\int G < B \, t \, |Q_{k,0}|$. For
$l > B$, $2^{ln} \geq 2^l > B$ since $n \geq 1$, so by \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}
$t \, |Q_{k-l,m'}| = 2^{ln} \, t \, |Q_{k,0}| > \int G \geq \int_{Q_{k-l,m'}} G$.
\end{proof}

For $n = 0$ the space $V$ is a single point and every cube is $V$, so the last statement needs
$n \geq 1$.

\begin{definition}[Maximal big cubes and the exceptional sets]
\label{def:calderon-zygmund-cubes}
\lean{Rd.czMaximal, Rd.czMaximal_countable, Rd.czSet, Rd.czEnlarged, Rd.measurableSet_czSet,
  Rd.measurableSet_czEnlarged, Rd.zcell_subset_czSet, Rd.czSet_subset_czEnlarged}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}
A pair $(k, m) \in \Z \times \Z^n$ is a maximal big cube of $G$ at height $t$ if
$G_{Q_{k,m}} > t$ and $G_{Q_{k-l,m^{(l)}}} \leq t$ for every integer $l \geq 1$. The exceptional
set $\Omega$ is the union of the maximal big cubes $Q_{k,m}$, and the enlarged exceptional set is
\[
  \Omega^* = \bigcup_{(k, m) \text{ maximal big}} \bar B\bigl(c_{k,m}, 2 n 2^{-k}\bigr) .
\]
There are countably many maximal big cubes, $\Omega$ and $\Omega^*$ are measurable, and every
maximal big cube lies in $\Omega \subseteq \Omega^*$.
\end{definition}

The inclusion $\Omega \subseteq \Omega^*$ holds since $Q_{k,m} \subseteq \bar B(c_{k,m}, n 2^{-k})$
by \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes}; the factor $2$ in the radius keeps every point outside $\Omega^*$ at
a distance greater than $n 2^{-k}$ from each maximal big cube $Q_{k,m}$.

\begin{theorem}[Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition]
\label{thm:calderon-zygmund}
\lean{Rd.exists_maximal_of_big, Rd.czMaximal_disjoint_of_le, Rd.czMaximal_pairwiseDisjoint,
  Rd.cz_avg_gt, Rd.cz_avg_le, Rd.cz_volume_zcell_le, Rd.cz_volume_le, Rd.cz_ae_le_of_notMem}
\leanok
\uses{def:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes, lem:dyadic-cubes-nested, def:calderon-zygmund-cubes,
  lem:coarse-cubes-not-big, thm:lebesgue-differentiation-cubes}
For every $G : V \to [0, \infty]$ and every $t \in [0, \infty]$, with $0^{-1} = \infty$, the
maximal big cubes of $G$ at height $t$ are pairwise disjoint, every maximal big cube $Q$ satisfies
\[
  t < G_Q \leq 2^n t, \qquad |Q| \leq t^{-1} \int_Q G,
\]
and $|\Omega| \leq t^{-1} \int G$. If moreover $n \geq 1$, $\int G < \infty$ and $t > 0$, then
every big cube $Q_{k,m}$ lies in a maximal big cube $Q_{k-l,m^{(l)}}$ for some $l \in \N$, and if
in addition $G$ is almost everywhere measurable, then $G(x) \leq t$ for almost every
$x \in V \setminus \Omega$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Let $Q_{k,m}$ and $Q_{k',m'}$ be distinct maximal big cubes with $k \leq k'$. For $k = k'$ they
are distinct cubes of one level, hence disjoint. For $k' = k + l$ with $l \geq 1$, the cube
$Q_{k',m'}$ lies in its ancestor $Q_{k,m'^{(l)}}$, which is not big by maximality, so
$m'^{(l)} \neq m$ and the two cubes of level $k$ are disjoint. The bound $G_Q > t$ is the
definition. The parent $P$ of $Q$ is not big and $|P| = 2^n |Q|$, so
$\int_Q G \leq \int_P G \leq t \, |P| = 2^n t \, |Q|$. From $t \, |Q| < \int_Q G$ follows
$|Q| \leq t^{-1} \int_Q G$, for $t = 0$ since then $\int_Q G > 0$ and the right side is
$\infty$, and summing over the disjoint maximal cubes gives
$|\Omega| \leq t^{-1} \int_\Omega G \leq t^{-1} \int G$.

Let $Q_{k,m}$ be big. By \Cref{lem:coarse-cubes-not-big} the set of $l \in \N$ with
$Q_{k-l,m^{(l)}}$ big is bounded and contains $0$; for its largest element $l_0$ the cube
$Q_{k-l_0,m^{(l_0)}}$ is a maximal big cube, since its ancestors are the cubes
$Q_{k-l,m^{(l)}}$ with $l > l_0$, and it contains $Q_{k,m}$ by \Cref{lem:dyadic-cubes-nested}.
Hence for $x \notin \Omega$ no cube $Q_k(x)$, $k \in \N$, is big, so $G_{Q_k(x)} \leq t$ for
all $k$, and \Cref{thm:lebesgue-differentiation-cubes} gives $G(x) \leq t$ for almost every
such $x$.
\end{proof}

\chapter{Besov spaces on compact manifolds}
\label{ch:manifolds}

This chapter works on a compact manifold $M$ modelled on a finite-dimensional real inner product
space $E$ with corners $I$, in the sense of Mathlib: charts $e$ map open sets of $M$ to open sets
of a model space $H$, and the extended charts $\kappa = e.\mathrm{extend}\, I$ take values in the
closed set $\Omega = \operatorname{range} I \subseteq E$, the half-space for manifolds with
boundary and $E$ itself for manifolds without boundary.

\section{Smooth measures}

\begin{definition}[Smooth measures]
\label{def:smooth-measure}
\lean{Manifold.IsSmoothMeasure, Manifold.measurableSet_extend_target}
\leanok
A measure $\mu$ on $M$ is smooth if for every chart $e$ of the atlas there is a function $\rho_e$,
continuous on the target $\kappa(e.\mathrm{source})$ with $0 < \rho_e < \infty$ there, such that
$\kappa_* (\mu|_{e.\mathrm{source}}) = \rho_e \, \mathrm{vol}|_{\kappa(e.\mathrm{source})}$. The
target is measurable, the intersection of an open set with the closed range of $I$.
\end{definition}

The Riemannian volume and the Lebesgue measure of a smooth domain are smooth measures.

\begin{lemma}[Integrals in a chart]
\label{lem:chart-integrals}
\lean{Manifold.IsSmoothMeasure.lintegral_source_eq, Manifold.IsSmoothMeasure.exists_density_bounds,
  Manifold.IsSmoothMeasure.exists_lintegral_chart_le}
\leanok
\uses{def:smooth-measure}
For a chart $e$ of the atlas and a measurable $F \geq 0$ on $M$,
\[
  \int_{e.\mathrm{source}} F \, d\mu = \int_{\kappa(e.\mathrm{source})} \rho_e(y) F(\kappa^{-1} y) \, dy .
\]
For a compact $K \subseteq e.\mathrm{source}$ there are $c > 0$ and $C < \infty$ such that every
measurable $F \geq 0$ that vanishes outside $K$ satisfies
$\int_{\kappa(e.\mathrm{source})} F \circ \kappa^{-1} \leq c^{-1} \int_M F \, d\mu$ and
$\int_M F \, d\mu \leq C \int_{\kappa(e.\mathrm{source})} F \circ \kappa^{-1}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The first identity is the definition of the image measure with $F = (F \circ \kappa^{-1}) \circ
\kappa$ on the source. The continuous density is bounded above and below by positive finite
constants on the compact $\kappa(K)$, which carries $F \circ \kappa^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\section{The Besov atlas}

From now on $M$ is compact, smooth, and the range $\Omega$ of $I$ is a Besov domain
(\Cref{def:besov-domain}); $\mu$ is a smooth measure and $1 \leq p < \infty$.

\begin{definition}[Besov atlases]
\label{def:besov-atlas}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas, Manifold.BesovAtlas.nonempty, Manifold.contDiffOn_comp_extend_symm,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.ext, Manifold.BesovAtlas.psi_le_one, Manifold.BesovAtlas.theta_mul_psi}
\leanok
A Besov atlas consists of finitely many charts $e_i$ of the atlas with extended charts
$\kappa_i$, a smooth partition of unity $(\psi_i)$ with $\operatorname{tsupport} \psi_i \subseteq
e_i.\mathrm{source}$, and smooth cut-offs $\theta_i$ with $\theta_i = 1$ on
$\operatorname{tsupport} \psi_i$ and $\operatorname{tsupport} \theta_i \subseteq
e_i.\mathrm{source}$. Every compact smooth manifold has a Besov atlas. A smooth function $g$ on $M$
gives a function $g \circ \kappa^{-1}$ that is smooth within the target of $\kappa$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Finitely many chart sources cover $M$; Mathlib's smooth partitions of unity are subordinate to
them, and a smooth Urysohn function between $\operatorname{tsupport} \psi_i$ and the complement of
an open $V_i$ with $\overline{V_i} \subseteq e_i.\mathrm{source}$ is the cut-off.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Chart pieces]
\label{def:chart-pieces}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece, Manifold.BesovAtlas.aestronglyMeasurable_piece,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.exists_eLpNorm_piece_le, Manifold.BesovAtlas.memLp_piece,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp, Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_ae, Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece_add,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece_smul, Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece_ae_eq, Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_add,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_smul, Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_finsetSum}
\leanok
\uses{def:besov-atlas, lem:chart-integrals}
The $i$-th piece of $g : M \to \C$ is $(\psi_i g) \circ \kappa_i^{-1}$ on the target of
$\kappa_i$ and $0$ elsewhere. It is linear in $g$, depends only on the class of $g$ modulo
$\mu$-null sets, and $\|(\psi_i g) \circ \kappa_i^{-1}\|_{L_p(E)} \leq C \|g\|_{L_p(\mu)}$; so the
pieces of $f \in L_p(\mu)$ lie in $L_p(E)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Changes of charts]
\label{lem:coord-change}
\lean{Manifold.range_inter_coordChange_source, Manifold.range_inter_coordChange_target,
  Manifold.mem_coordChange_source, Manifold.coordChange_target_eq_image,
  Manifold.coordChange_apply}
\leanok
For charts $e, e'$ the change of charts $\kappa' \circ \kappa^{-1}$ (Mathlib's
\texttt{ModelWithCorners.extendCoordChange}) has the source $\kappa(e.\mathrm{source} \cap
e'.\mathrm{source}) = \Omega \cap U$ and the target
$\kappa'(e.\mathrm{source} \cap e'.\mathrm{source}) = \Omega \cap U'$ with open $U, U'$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The image of an open subset $S$ of $H$ under $I$ is $I^{-1}$-preimage of $S$ intersected with
the range of $I$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The changes of charts with cut-offs]
\label{def:chart-transition}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.chartChange, Manifold.BesovAtlas.chartChange_apply,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.tsupport_psi_mul_theta_subset, Manifold.BesovAtlas.transition,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.transition_apply}
\leanok
\uses{def:cutoff-diffeo, lem:coord-change, def:besov-atlas}
For charts $e, e'$ of the atlas and a smooth $c : M \to \R$ with $\operatorname{tsupport} c
\subseteq e.\mathrm{source} \cap e'.\mathrm{source}$, the change of charts
$\Phi = \kappa' \circ \kappa^{-1}$ with the cut-off $\chi = c \circ \kappa'^{-1}$ and
$K_0 = \kappa'(\operatorname{tsupport} c)$ is a smooth change of variables with a cut-off on
$\Omega$. For two Besov atlases $A, A'$ and charts $i$ of $A$ and $j$ of $A'$, the cut-off
$c = \psi_i \theta'_j$ gives the change of variables $T_{ij}$, with
$\Phi_{ij} = \kappa'_j \circ \kappa_i^{-1}$ and $\chi_{ij} = (\psi_i \theta'_j) \circ
\kappa_j'^{-1}$.
\end{definition}

The smoothness of $\Phi_{ij}$ and of its inverse is the compatibility of the charts in the
maximal atlas, Mathlib's
\texttt{ModelWithCorners.\allowbreak contDiffOn\_\allowbreak extendCoordChange}.

\begin{theorem}[The pieces of two atlases]
\label{thm:pieces-two-atlases}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece_eq_sum_apply, Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_eq_sum}
\leanok
\uses{def:chart-pieces, def:chart-transition, lem:cutoff-diffeo-lp}
For every $g : M \to \C$, $(\psi_i g) \circ \kappa_i^{-1} = \sum_j T_{ij}\bigl((\psi'_j g) \circ
\kappa_j'^{-1}\bigr)$ at every point; for $f \in L_p(\mu)$ the same holds in $L_p(E)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
At $y = \kappa_i(x)$, $\psi_i(x) g(x) = \sum_j \psi_i(x) \psi'_j(x) g(x)$, and the $j$-th term is
$\chi_{ij}(\Phi_{ij} y) \cdot (\psi'_j g)(\kappa_j'^{-1} \Phi_{ij} y)$ when $x$ lies in the source
of $e'_j$, since $\theta'_j \psi'_j = \psi'_j$; otherwise $\psi'_j(x) = 0$ and $y$ lies outside the
source of $\Phi_{ij}$.
\end{proof}

\section{Besov spaces on compact manifolds}

\begin{definition}[The Besov quasi-norm of an atlas]
\label{def:manifold-besov-norm}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.besovNorm, Manifold.BesovAtlas.besovNorm_add_le,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.besovNorm_const_smul, Manifold.BesovAtlas.exists_enorm_le_besovNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:chart-pieces, lem:modnorm-on}
For a Besov atlas $A$, $r \in \N$, $s \in \R$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$,
\begin{gather*}
  \|f\|_{A, r, s, q} = \sum_i N_\Omega\bigl((\psi_i f) \circ \kappa_i^{-1}\bigr), \\
  N_\Omega(G) = \|G\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(G, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} .
\end{gather*}
It is subadditive, homogeneous, and $\|f\|_{L_p(\mu)} \leq C \|f\|_{A, r, s, q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Independence of the atlas]
\label{thm:atlas-independence}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.besovNorm_le_besovNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:manifold-besov-norm, thm:pieces-two-atlases, thm:besov-domain-invariance}
For two Besov atlases $A, A'$, $0 < s < r$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ there is $C < \infty$ with
$\|f\|_{A, r, s, q} \leq C \|f\|_{A', r, s, q}$ for all $f \in L_p(\mu)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{thm:pieces-two-atlases} and the subadditivity of $N_\Omega$,
$N_\Omega(\text{piece}_i f) \leq \sum_j N_\Omega(T_{ij}\, \text{piece}'_j f)$, and
$N_\Omega(T_{ij} G) \leq C_{ij} N_\Omega(G)$ on the Besov domain $\Omega$
(\Cref{thm:besov-domain-invariance}).
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Besov spaces on compact manifolds]
\label{def:manifold-besov}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov, Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBesov_iff_besovNorm_lt_top,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBesov_iff_memBesov, Manifold.MemBesov, Manifold.memBesov_iff,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov.add, Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov.const_smul,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov.zero, Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov.finsetSum,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBesov.modNormOn_pieceLp_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{thm:atlas-independence, thm:besov-domain-invariance}
For $s > 0$, $f \in L_p(\mu)$ lies in $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(M)$ if $\|f\|_{A, \lfloor s \rfloor + 1, s,
q} < \infty$ for some Besov atlas $A$. This holds for one atlas if and only if it holds for every
atlas, and with any order $r > s$ in place of $\lfloor s \rfloor + 1$; then every piece has a
finite intrinsic quasi-norm of order $r$. $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(M)$ is a linear subspace of
$L_p(\mu)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{thm:atlas-independence} in both directions; for the order, the comparison of orders on a
Besov domain (\Cref{thm:besov-domain-invariance}) piece by piece.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Functions from a chart]
\label{lem:chart-lift}
\lean{Manifold.chartLift, Manifold.stronglyMeasurable_chartLift, Manifold.memLp_chartLift,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.piece_chartLift, Manifold.BesovAtlas.chartLiftLp,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.pieceLp_chartLiftLp, Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBesov_chartLiftLp}
\leanok
\uses{def:manifold-besov, def:chart-transition, thm:diffeo-invariance, lem:chart-integrals}
For a chart $e$ of the atlas, a continuous $\theta$ on $M$ with $\operatorname{tsupport} \theta
\subseteq e.\mathrm{source}$ and $F \in L_p(E)$, the lift $\theta \cdot F \circ \kappa$ on
$e.\mathrm{source}$, $0$ elsewhere, lies in $L_p(\mu)$. For a Besov atlas, the $i$-th piece of the
lift $\theta_k \cdot F \circ \kappa_k$ is $T_{ik} F$. If
$\|F\|_p + \|(2^{j\sigma} \omega_r(F, 2^{-j})_p)_j\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ with $0 < \sigma < r$,
the lift lies in $\Besov{\sigma}{p}{q}(M)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The chart $\kappa$ is continuous on the open source, so the lift is measurable (Mathlib's
\texttt{ContinuousOn.\allowbreak measurable\_\allowbreak piecewise}). Its integral is comparable to
the integral in the chart on the compact $\operatorname{tsupport} \theta$
(\Cref{lem:chart-integrals}), where $\theta$ is bounded. At $y = \kappa_i(x)$ with
$x \in e_k.\mathrm{source}$, $\psi_i(x) \theta_k(x) F(\kappa_k x) = \chi_{ik}(\Phi_{ik} y)
F(\Phi_{ik} y)$. The changes of charts are bounded into the intrinsic space on $\Omega$
(\Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance}).
\end{proof}

\section{Smooth bounded domains}

\begin{lemma}[Flattening a graph domain]
\label{lem:flattening}
\lean{Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.unflatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.phi_sub_smul,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.unflatten_flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flatten_unflatten,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.inner_flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.flattenEquiv,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.continuous_unflatten,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.contDiff_flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.contDiff_unflatten,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.fderiv_phi_e, Rd.LipschitzGraph.hasFDerivAt_flatten,
  Rd.LipschitzGraph.det_fderiv_flatten, Rd.LipschitzGraph.measurePreserving_flatten}
\leanok
\uses{def:lipschitz-graph}
For a Lipschitz graph domain $\{x : \varphi(x) < \langle x, e \rangle\}$ the flattening
$F(x) = x - \varphi(x) e$ is a bijection of $V$ with inverse $y \mapsto y + \varphi(y) e$ and
$\langle F(x), e \rangle = \langle x, e \rangle - \varphi(x)$. For smooth $\varphi$ both maps are
smooth and $F$ preserves the Lebesgue measure.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\varphi$ is constant along $e$, so $\varphi(x - \varphi(x) e) = \varphi(x)$ and $D\varphi(x) e = 0$.
The derivative $\mathrm{id} - e \otimes D\varphi(x)$ is a transvection with determinant
$1 - D\varphi(x) e = 1$ (Mathlib's \texttt{LinearMap.transvection.det}), and the change of variables
formula gives $F_* \mathrm{vol} = \mathrm{vol}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Smooth bounded domains]
\label{def:smooth-domain}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain, Rd.e0, Rd.norm_e0, Rd.coord_zero_eq_inner, Rd.alignTo,
  Rd.alignTo_apply_self, Rd.LipschitzGraph.closure_domain, Rd.mem_closure_iff_of_inter_eq}
\leanok
\uses{def:bounded-lipschitz}
A smooth bounded domain in $\R^n$ is a bounded Lipschitz domain whose graph functions are smooth.
The reflection $A_e$ in $(e - e_0)^\perp$ maps a unit vector $e$ to $e_0 = (1, 0, \dots, 0)$. The
closure of a graph domain is $\{x : \varphi(x) \leq \langle x, e \rangle\}$, and closures of sets
that agree on an open set $B$ agree on $B$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Straighteners and their charts]
\label{def:straightener}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.tau_mem_closure,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.toMap, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.coe_toMap,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.invMap, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.coe_invMap,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.chart, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.mem_chart_source,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.mem_chart_target, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.chart_apply_coe,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.chart_symm_coe}
\leanok
\uses{def:smooth-domain}
A straightener of the closure of a smooth domain $\Omega$ is a smooth, measure-preserving bijection
