$\sigma$ of $\R^n$ with smooth inverse $\tau$ and an open set $B$ such that for $x \in B$,
$x \in \overline{\Omega}$ if and only if $\sigma(x)_0 \geq 0$. Its chart maps
$\overline{\Omega} \cap B$ by $\sigma$ onto an open subset of the half-space
$\{y : y_0 \geq 0\}$, with inverse $\tau$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Boundary and interior straighteners]
\label{lem:straighteners}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.straighten, Rd.SmoothDomain.unstraighten, Rd.SmoothDomain.straighten_coord,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.boundaryStraightener, Rd.SmoothDomain.interiorRadius,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.interiorRadius_pos, Rd.SmoothDomain.ball_interiorRadius_subset,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.interior_coord_pos, Rd.SmoothDomain.interiorStraightener,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.boundaryIndex,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.mem_ball_boundaryIndex, Rd.SmoothDomain.straightenerAt,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.mem_straightenerAt}
\leanok
\uses{def:straightener, lem:flattening}
On the $j$-th boundary ball, $\sigma_j(x) = A_{e_j}(x - \varphi_j(x) e_j)$ is a straightener, with
$\sigma_j(x)_0 = \langle x, e_j \rangle - \varphi_j(x)$. Around $x_0 \in \Omega$ with
$B(x_0, \rho) \subseteq \Omega$ the translation $x \mapsto x - x_0 + 2\rho e_0$ is a straightener.
Every point of $\overline{\Omega}$ lies in the set of one of them.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\Cref{lem:flattening} and the reflection give the properties of $\sigma_j$; on the ball,
$\overline{\Omega}$ agrees with the closure of the graph domain. For the translation,
$|(x - x_0)_0| \leq \|x - x_0\| < \rho$. A point of $\overline{\Omega} \setminus \Omega$ lies on the
boundary, which the boundary balls cover.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Smooth domains are manifolds with boundary]
\label{thm:smooth-domain-manifold}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.chartedSpace, Rd.SmoothDomain.isManifold, Rd.SmoothDomain.compactSpace,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.volumeOn, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.extend_target,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.isSmoothMeasure_volumeOn}
\leanok
\uses{lem:straighteners, def:smooth-measure}
The closure of a smooth bounded domain, with the charts of all straighteners, is a compact smooth
manifold with boundary modelled on the half-space $\{y \in \R^n : y_0 \geq 0\}$, and the Lebesgue
measure on it is a smooth measure with density $1$ in every chart.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The change of charts from $(\sigma, \tau)$ to $(\sigma', \tau')$ is the restriction of the smooth
map $\sigma' \circ \tau$. The closure is closed and bounded. The image of the Lebesgue measure on
$\overline{\Omega} \cap B$ under the measure-preserving $\sigma$ is the Lebesgue measure on
$\sigma(\overline{\Omega} \cap B)$, the target of the chart.
\end{proof}

\section{Besov spaces of smooth domains by charts}

\begin{definition}[Global atlases]
\label{def:global-atlas}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.nonempty,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.toBesovAtlas, Rd.SmoothDomain.contMDiff_subtype_val,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.hasCompactSupport_psi,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.hasCompactSupport_theta, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.psi_eq_zero,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.theta_eq_zero, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.theta_mul_psi}
\leanok
\uses{thm:smooth-domain-manifold, def:besov-atlas}
A global atlas of a smooth domain $\Omega$ consists of finitely many straighteners $(\sigma_i,
\tau_i, B_i)$ whose sets cover $\overline{\Omega}$, smooth functions $\psi_i \geq 0$ on $\R^n$ with
compact $\operatorname{tsupport} \psi_i \subseteq B_i$ and $\sum_i \psi_i = 1$ on
$\overline{\Omega}$, and smooth cut-offs $\theta_i = 1$ on $\operatorname{tsupport} \psi_i$ with
compact $\operatorname{tsupport} \theta_i \subseteq B_i$. Every smooth domain has one, and their
restrictions to $\overline{\Omega}$ form a Besov atlas, since the inclusion of $\overline{\Omega}$
into $\R^n$ is smooth.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Extension by zero and the pieces]
\label{lem:global-pieces}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.extend, Rd.SmoothDomain.extend_coe, Rd.SmoothDomain.extend_eq_zero,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.stronglyMeasurable_extend, Rd.SmoothDomain.eLpNorm_extend,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.memLp_extend, Rd.SmoothDomain.extLp, Rd.SmoothDomain.extLp_ae,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.volume_coord_zero_eq_zero, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.mem_image_iff,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.homeomorph, Rd.SmoothDomain.Straightener.measurePreserving_tau,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.piece_apply, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.indicator_piece}
\leanok
\uses{def:global-atlas, def:chart-pieces}
The extension by $0$ is an isometry $L_p(\overline{\Omega}) \to L_p(\R^n)$. On the half-space
$\{y_0 \geq 0\}$, the $i$-th piece of $g$ is $(\psi_i\, \mathrm{ext}\, g) \circ \tau_i$. The
hyperplane $\{y_0 = 0\}$ is a null set, and $\tau_i$ preserves the Lebesgue measure.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The image $\sigma_i(\overline{\Omega} \cap B_i)$ is $\{y : y_0 \geq 0,\ \tau_i(y) \in B_i\}$;
outside it on the half-space, $\psi_i \circ \tau_i = 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The changes of variables between $\Omega$ and the charts]
\label{lem:global-changes}
\lean{Rd.CutoffDiffeo.global, Rd.CutoffDiffeo.global_apply, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.toChart,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.toChart_apply, Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.fromChart,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.fromChart_apply, Rd.SmoothDomain.interior_univ_nonempty,
  Rd.modNormOn_congr}
\leanok
\uses{def:cutoff-diffeo, def:global-atlas, lem:modnorm-on}
A smooth bijection $\Phi$ of $V$ with smooth inverse and a smooth cut-off $\chi$ with compact
support form a change of variables with a cut-off on $V$, acting by $(\chi f) \circ \Phi$. In
particular $F \mapsto (\psi_i F) \circ \tau_i$ and $G \mapsto \theta_i (G \circ \sigma_i)$ are
bounded for the Besov quasi-norms of $\R^n$ (\Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance}). The intrinsic
quasi-norm on a set depends only on the restriction to the set.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The charts of the atlas and their inverses are smooth bijections of $V$, and the functions of
the partition of unity are smooth with compact support, so each pair is a change of variables
with a cut-off; \Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance} gives the bounds. The intrinsic quasi-norm on a set
evaluates the function only at points of the set.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The Besov space of a smooth domain by charts]
\label{thm:smooth-domain-besov}
\lean{Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.modNormOn_pieceLp_lt_top,
  Rd.SmoothDomain.GlobalAtlas.modNormOn_extLp_lt_top, Rd.SmoothDomain.memBesov_iff_modNormOn_lt_top}
\leanok
\uses{lem:global-pieces, lem:global-changes, def:manifold-besov, thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov,
  thm:half-space-besov-domain}
For a smooth bounded domain $\Omega \subseteq \R^n$, $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < r$ and
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $f \in L_p(\overline{\Omega})$ lies in the Besov space
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\overline{\Omega})$ of the compact manifold $\overline{\Omega}$ with the Lebesgue
measure if and only if
$\|f\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
If the intrinsic quasi-norm is finite, $f$ agrees on $\Omega$ with some $F \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\R^n)$
(\Cref{thm:bounded-lipschitz-besov}). On the half-space the piece $(\psi_i f) \circ \tau_i$ agrees
almost everywhere with $(\psi_i F) \circ \tau_i$: where $y_0 > 0$ and $\tau_i(y) \in B_i$, the
point $\tau_i(y)$ lies in $\Omega$, and $\tau_i$ preserves null sets. The latter function is in
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\R^n)$ by \Cref{lem:global-changes}. Conversely, the pieces extend from the
half-space (\Cref{thm:half-space-besov-domain}) to $G_i \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\R^n)$, and
$F = \sum_i \theta_i (G_i \circ \sigma_i) \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\R^n)$ agrees with $f$ on $\Omega$,
since $\theta_i \psi_i = \psi_i$ and $\sum_i \psi_i = 1$ on $\overline{\Omega}$.
\end{proof}

\section{Besov spaces on the boundary}

In this section $M$ is a compact smooth manifold with boundary modelled on the half-space
$\{y \in \R^n : y_0 \geq 0\}$. Its boundary hyperplane is $H_0 = e_0^\perp = \{y : y_0 = 0\}$, with
the orthogonal projection $P$ onto $H_0$ and the Lebesgue measure of $H_0$.

\begin{lemma}[Changes of charts preserve the boundary]
\label{lem:coord-change-boundary}
\lean{Manifold.flatDomain, Manifold.bdry, Manifold.mem_bdry_iff, Manifold.bdry_subset_range,
  Manifold.coe_proj_of_mem_bdry, Manifold.isClosed_bdry, Manifold.mem_interior_extend_target_iff,
  Manifold.coordChange_mem_bdry, Manifold.mem_coordChange_source_iff_of_bdry,
  Manifold.mem_coordChange_target_iff_of_bdry, Manifold.coe_proj_coordChange,
  Manifold.coe_proj_coordChange_symm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:coord-change}
$H_0$ is a closed subspace contained in the half-space. A point $y$ of the target of an extended
chart lies in the interior of the target if and only if $y_0 > 0$. For charts $e, e'$ of the
atlas, the change of charts maps every point $y$ of its source with $y_0 = 0$ to a point of $H_0$,
and so does its inverse.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
A point of $M$ is mapped into the interior of the half-space by one chart if and only if by every
chart (Mathlib's \texttt{ModelWithCorners.\allowbreak mem\_interior\_range\_iff\_of\_mem\_atlas}).
The change of charts maps into the half-space, so a point with $y_0 = 0$ goes to a point whose
first coordinate is $\geq 0$ and not $> 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Changes of charts on the boundary]
\label{def:boundary-chart-change}
\lean{Manifold.boundaryChartChange, Manifold.chartChange_apply_coe,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryTransition}
\leanok
\uses{lem:coord-change-boundary, def:chart-transition, def:cutoff-diffeo}
Let $T$ be the change of charts with a cut-off of \Cref{def:chart-transition}, with $\Phi$, $\chi$
and $K_0$. Its restriction $T^\partial$ to $H_0$, with the change of charts $P \circ \Phi$ on
$H_0 \cap U$, its inverse $P \circ \Phi^{-1}$ on $H_0 \cap U'$, the cut-off $\chi|_{H_0}$ and the
compact set $K_0 \cap H_0$, is a smooth change of variables with a cut-off on the whole space
$H_0$, and $(T G)|_{H_0} = T^\partial(G|_{H_0})$ for every $G : \R^n \to \C$. For two Besov
atlases $A, A'$ the restriction of $T_{ij}$ is $T^\partial_{ij}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Besov spaces on the boundary]
\label{def:manifold-boundary-besov}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryPiece, Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBoundaryBesov}
\leanok
\uses{def:chart-pieces}
For a Besov atlas $A$, the $i$-th boundary piece of $g : M \to \C$ is the restriction
$\bigl((\psi_i g) \circ \kappa_i^{-1}\bigr)|_{H_0}$ of its $i$-th piece. For $1 \leq p < \infty$,
$s > 0$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $g$ lies in $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\partial M)$ if every boundary
piece agrees almost everywhere on $H_0$ with some $G_i \in L_p(H_0)$ with
$\|G_i\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(G_i, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, $r = \lfloor s \rfloor + 1$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Independence of the atlas on the boundary]
\label{thm:boundary-atlas-independence}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryPiece_eq_sum, Manifold.BesovAtlas.interior_univ_bdry_nonempty,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.MemBoundaryBesov.of_atlas,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBoundaryBesov_iff_memBoundaryBesov}
\leanok
\uses{def:manifold-boundary-besov, def:boundary-chart-change, thm:pieces-two-atlases,
  thm:diffeo-invariance, lem:modnorm-on}
For two Besov atlases $A, A'$ and $g : M \to \C$, the $i$-th boundary piece of $g$ for $A$ is
$\sum_j T^\partial_{ij}$ applied to the $j$-th boundary piece for $A'$, at every point of $H_0$.
For $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, $g \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\partial M)$
for $A$ if and only if for $A'$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The identity is \Cref{thm:pieces-two-atlases} restricted to $H_0$
(\Cref{def:boundary-chart-change}). The changes of charts $T^\partial_{ij}$ live on the whole
space $H_0$, so \Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance} bounds
$N(T^\partial_{ij} G'_j) \leq C_{ij} N(G'_j)$ for the Besov quasi-norm $N$ of $H_0$, and $N$ is
subadditive (\Cref{lem:modnorm-on}).
\end{proof}

\section{The trace theorem on compact manifolds}

In this section $M$ is a compact manifold with boundary as in the previous section, $\mu$ a smooth
measure, $1 \leq p < \infty$, and $\operatorname{Tr}$ the trace of order $L$ of the open
half-space $\Omega_0 = \{y : y_0 > 0\}$ (\Cref{thm:trace-every-order}).

\begin{lemma}[The closed and the open half-space]
\label{lem:closed-open-half-space}
\lean{Manifold.range_eq_closedHalfSpace, Manifold.range_ae_eq_domain,
  Manifold.cutoff_eq_indicatorLp, Manifold.modNormOn_range_eq}
\leanok
\uses{lem:null-set-domains}
The range of the model is the closed half-space $\{y : y_0 \geq 0\}$, which differs from
$\Omega_0$ by the null set $H_0$. So the cut-offs and the intrinsic quasi-norms on the two sets
agree.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The range of the model is the closed half-space by its definition. The cut-offs and the
intrinsic quasi-norms are built from integrals over the set and over its translates, and these
change only by null sets when $\Omega_0$ is replaced by its closure
(\Cref{lem:null-set-domains}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace commutes with the changes of charts]
\label{thm:trace-chart-change}
\lean{Manifold.trace_chartChange_applyLp_of_continuous,
  Manifold.trace_chartChange_applyLp_of_modNorm, Manifold.trace_chartChange_applyLp}
\leanok
\uses{def:boundary-chart-change, lem:trace-continuous-half-space, lem:partial-sums-approx,
  lem:trace-linear, thm:diffeo-invariance, lem:closed-open-half-space, def:besov-domain}
Let $T$ be a change of charts with a cut-off and $T^\partial$ its restriction to $H_0$
(\Cref{def:boundary-chart-change}), $s > 0$, $L > s + 1/p$, and let $G \in L_p(\R^n)$ satisfy
$\|G\|_{L_p(\Omega)} + \|(2^{k(s+1/p)} \omega_L(G, 2^{-k}, \Omega)_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ on the
closed half-space $\Omega$. Then $\operatorname{Tr}(T G) = T^\partial(\operatorname{Tr} G)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Extend $G$ to $\R^n$, since the half-space is a Besov domain; neither side changes. For the
continuous partial sums $G_J$ of the reproducing formula, $T G_J$ is continuous on the closed
half-space and vanishes outside a compact set (\Cref{def:cutoff-diffeo}), so both sides are the
restriction of $T G_J$ to $H_0$ (\Cref{lem:trace-continuous-half-space}). As $J \to \infty$,
$G_J \to G$ in $\Besov{1/p}{p}{1}$ (\Cref{lem:partial-sums-approx}); $T$ maps this space boundedly
into the intrinsic space on $\Omega$ (\Cref{thm:diffeo-invariance}), where the trace is bounded
(\Cref{lem:trace-linear}), so $\operatorname{Tr}(T G_J) \to \operatorname{Tr}(T G)$; and
$\operatorname{Tr} G_J \to \operatorname{Tr} G$ in $L_p(H_0)$, where $T^\partial$ is bounded.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The trace on the boundary]
\label{def:manifold-boundary-trace}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryTrace, Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryPiece_boundaryTrace_eq_sum}
\leanok
\uses{def:manifold-boundary-besov, def:boundary-chart-change}
For a Besov atlas $A$, an order $L$ and $f \in L_p(\mu)$, the trace on the boundary is
$\operatorname{Tr}_A f = \sum_i \theta_i \cdot \operatorname{Tr}(\text{piece}_i f) \circ P \circ
\kappa_i$ on the chart sources. At every point of $H_0$ its $k$-th boundary piece is
$\sum_i T^\partial_{ki} \bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(\text{piece}_i f)\bigr)$, since $\psi_k \theta_i$
is the cut-off of $T_{ki}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The boundary pieces of the trace]
\label{thm:boundary-trace-pieces}
\lean{Manifold.BesovAtlas.trace_pieceLp_eq_sum, Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryPiece_boundaryTrace,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.boundaryPiece_boundaryTrace_of_atlas}
\leanok
\uses{def:manifold-boundary-trace, thm:trace-chart-change, thm:pieces-two-atlases, lem:trace-sums,
  thm:boundary-atlas-independence}
Let $s > 0$, $L > s + 1/p$ and $f \in \Besov{s+1/p}{p}{q}(M)$. For two Besov atlases $A, A'$,
$\operatorname{Tr}(\text{piece}_i f) = \sum_j T^\partial_{ij}
\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(\text{piece}'_j f)\bigr)$, and the $i$-th boundary piece for $A$ of
$\operatorname{Tr}_{A'} f$ is $\operatorname{Tr}(\text{piece}_i f)$ almost everywhere on $H_0$. In
particular the trace does not depend on the atlas.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Take the traces in $\text{piece}_i f = \sum_j T_{ij}(\text{piece}'_j f)$
(\Cref{thm:pieces-two-atlases}): the trace of the finite sum is the sum of the traces
(\Cref{lem:trace-sums}), and it commutes with the $T_{ij}$ (\Cref{thm:trace-chart-change}). For
$A' = A$ this is the pointwise formula of \Cref{def:manifold-boundary-trace}, almost everywhere; for
general $A'$ combine it with \Cref{thm:boundary-atlas-independence}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The trace theorem on compact manifolds]
\label{thm:manifold-trace}
\lean{Manifold.exists_flatExtension, Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBoundaryBesov_boundaryTrace,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.exists_boundaryTrace_eq,
  Manifold.BesovAtlas.memBoundaryBesov_iff_exists_boundaryTrace}
\leanok
\uses{thm:boundary-trace-pieces, thm:trace-every-order, thm:half-space-trace-space, lem:chart-lift}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $s > 0$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $L \geq 2$ with $s + 1/p < L$. For
$f \in \Besov{s+1/p}{p}{q}(M)$, $\operatorname{Tr}_A f \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\partial M)$. Every
$g \in \Besov{s}{p}{q}(\partial M)$ is the trace of some $f \in \Besov{s+1/p}{p}{q}(M)$: the
boundary pieces of $\operatorname{Tr}_A f$ and of $g$ agree almost everywhere. So
$\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\partial M)$ is the trace space of $\Besov{s+1/p}{p}{q}(M)$ for every $s > 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The boundary pieces of $\operatorname{Tr}_A f$ are the traces of the pieces
(\Cref{thm:boundary-trace-pieces}), which lie in $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(H_0)$
(\Cref{thm:trace-every-order}). Conversely, the extensions $E G_k$ of moment order
$\lfloor s \rfloor + 1$ of the boundary pieces $G_k$ of $g$ have finite norms and traces $G_k$
(\Cref{thm:half-space-trace-space}). The pieces of
$f = \sum_k \theta_k \cdot (E G_k) \circ \kappa_k \in \Besov{s+1/p}{p}{q}(M)$
(\Cref{lem:chart-lift}) are $\sum_k T_{ik}(E G_k)$, with traces $\sum_k T^\partial_{ik} G_k$
(\Cref{thm:trace-chart-change}), the $i$-th boundary piece of $g$
(\Cref{thm:boundary-atlas-independence}).
\end{proof}

The closure of a smooth bounded domain is a compact manifold with boundary
(\Cref{thm:smooth-domain-manifold}), so this is the trace theorem on its boundary for every
$s > 0$, in charts.

\chapter{Dominating mixed smoothness on $\R^d$}

This chapter works on $\R^d$ with Lebesgue measure, for functions of $d$ real variables
$x = (x_1, \dots, x_d)$. The spaces of dominating mixed smoothness measure smoothness in each
coordinate separately: their quasi-norms are $\ell_q$ norms over $k \in \N^d$ with the weights
$2^{s|k|_1}$, $|k|_1 = k_1 + \dots + k_d$. All kernels are tensor products of the one-dimensional
kernels of the previous chapter with $V = \R$, and every convolution is a $d$-dimensional
convolution, so Young's inequality and the approximate identities apply to them. Three
descriptions are shown to agree, mixed differences after T.~Ullrich \cite{UllrichT}, mixed local
means, and tensor Littlewood--Paley blocks after Vyb\'{\i}ral \cite{Vybiral}; the last gives the
rate of approximation on hyperbolic crosses.

\section{Tensor kernels}

\begin{definition}[Tensor kernels]
\label{def:tensor-kernel}
\lean{Rd.tensorKernel, Rd.tensorKernel_apply, Rd.integrable_tensorKernel,
  Rd.continuous_tensorKernel}
\leanok
For one-dimensional kernels $K_1, \dots, K_d$ the tensor product is
$(\otimes K)(x) = \prod_i K_i(x_i)$. Tensor products of integrable (continuous) kernels are
integrable (continuous).
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Integrals and convolutions of tensor kernels]
\label{lem:tensor-kernel-fubini}
\lean{Rd.integral_tensorKernel, Rd.lintegral_enorm_tensorKernel, Rd.eLpNorm_tensorKernel_one,
  Rd.conv_tensorKernel, Rd.dilate_tensorKernel, Rd.sum_tensorKernel_piFinset}
\leanok
\uses{def:tensor-kernel, thm:young}
$\int \otimes K = \prod_i \int K_i$, $\|\otimes K\|_1 = \prod_i \|K_i\|_1$,
$(\otimes K) * (\otimes K') = \otimes (K_i * K'_i)$, $(\otimes K)_c = \otimes (K_i)_c$, and
$\sum_{k \in T_1 \times \dots \times T_d} \otimes (K_i(k_i)) = \otimes \bigl(\sum_{j \in T_i} K_i(j)\bigr)$
for finite sets $T_i$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Fubini's theorem for product measures, and $\prod_i \sum_j = \sum \prod$ for finite sums.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Diagonal changes of variables]
\label{lem:diagonal-substitution}
\lean{Rd.diagMap, Rd.diagMap_apply, Rd.det_diagMap, Rd.diagEquiv, Rd.diagEquiv_apply,
  Rd.integral_comp_diagMap, Rd.integral_tensorKernel_mul_comp_eq_conv, Rd.integrable_comp_diag}
\leanok
\uses{lem:tensor-kernel-fubini}
For $a_1, \dots, a_d > 0$, $\int F((a_i z_i)_i) \, dz = (\prod_i a_i)^{-1} \int F$, the map
$F \mapsto F((a_i \cdot_i)_i)$ keeps integrability, and
\[
  \int (\otimes\varphi)(z) f(x - (a_i z_i)_i) \, dz = ((\otimes \varphi_i^{(1/a_i)}) * f)(x),
  \qquad \varphi^{(c)}(t) = c \, \varphi(ct).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The diagonal map scales Lebesgue measure by its determinant $\prod_i a_i$.
\end{proof}

\section{Sequences on $\N^d$}

\begin{definition}[$\ell_q$ norms on $\N^d$]
\label{def:mixed-lq}
\lean{Rd.mixedWeight, Rd.mixedWeight_ne_top, Rd.mixedWeight_neg_mul_self}
\leanok
\uses{def:lq-norm}
For $a : \N^d \to [0, \infty]$, $\|a\|_{\ell_q} = (\sum_k a_k^q)^{1/q}$ for $q < \infty$ and
$\|a\|_{\ell_\infty} = \sup_k a_k$, the norm of \Cref{def:lq-norm} on the countable set $\N^d$.
The mixed weights are $2^{s|k|_1} = \prod_i 2^{s k_i}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The Schur test]
\label{lem:schur-test}
\lean{Rd.lqNorm_schur_le, Rd.tsum_prod_le_prod_tsum}
\leanok
\uses{def:mixed-lq, lem:weighted-jensen}
Let $w \geq 0$ on $\N^d \times \N^d$ with $\sum_k w(m, k) \leq A$ for every $m$ and
$\sum_m w(m, k) \leq A$ for every $k$. Then for $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and every $b \geq 0$,
$\|(\sum_k w(m, k) b_k)_m\|_{\ell_q} \leq A \|b\|_{\ell_q}$. Moreover
$\sum_{k \in \N^d} \prod_i f_i(k_i) \leq \prod_i \sum_j f_i(j)$ for $f_i \geq 0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By the weighted Jensen inequality (\Cref{lem:weighted-jensen}) for the counting measure,
$(\sum_k w(m, k) b_k)^q \leq A^{q-1} \sum_k w(m, k) b_k^q$; summing over $m$ and exchanging the sums
gives at most $A^q \sum_k b_k^q$. The product bound holds on the finite boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The almost diagonal lemma on $\N^d$]
\label{lem:mixed-almost-diagonal}
\lean{Rd.almostDiagCoeff, Rd.almostDiagProfile, Rd.dyadicWeight_mul_almostDiagCoeff_mul,
  Rd.tsum_almostDiagProfile_le, Rd.almostDiagConst, Rd.almostDiagConst_ne_top,
  Rd.lqNorm_almostDiagonal_pi_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:schur-test, lem:almost-diagonal}
Let $c(i, j) = 2^{-(i - j)L}$ for $j \leq i$ and $c(i, j) = 1$ for $j > i$, let $s \geq 0$ and
$1 \leq q \leq \infty$. Then for every $a \geq 0$
\[
  \bigl\|\bigl(2^{s|m|_1} \textstyle\sum_k \prod_i c(m_i, k_i) \, a_k\bigr)_m\bigr\|_{\ell_q}
  \leq C^d \, \|(2^{s|k|_1} a_k)_k\|_{\ell_q}
\]
with $C = (1 - 2^{-(L-s)})^{-1} + (1 - 2^{-s})^{-1}$, and $C < \infty$ for $0 < s < L$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$2^{s|m|_1} \prod_i c(m_i, k_i) \, 2^{-s|k|_1} = \prod_i u(m_i - k_i)$ with $u(l) = 2^{-(L-s)l}$
for $l \geq 0$ and $u(l) = 2^{sl}$ for $l < 0$, and $\sum_{l \in \Z} u(l) \leq C$. All row and
column sums are therefore at most $C^d$ (\Cref{lem:schur-test}). This replaces the
one-dimensional Hardy inequalities of \Cref{lem:almost-diagonal}, which do not iterate over the
coordinates.
\end{proof}

\section{Mixed differences}

\begin{definition}[Mixed differences]
\label{def:mixed-differences}
\lean{Rd.mixedDiff, Rd.diffAct_coordElem_pow_eq_higherDiff, Rd.mixedDiff_empty, Rd.mixedDiff_insert,
  Rd.mixedDiff_add, Rd.mixedDiff_sub}
\leanok
\uses{def:translation-algebra}
For $e \subseteq \{1, \dots, d\}$ and steps $h \in \R^d$ the mixed difference of order $L$ is
$\Delta^{L,e}_h f = (\prod_{i \in e} \Delta^L_{h_i e_i}) f$, the action of
$\prod_{i \in e} (\delta_{h_i e_i} - 1)^L$ in the commutative algebra of translations; a further
coordinate $i \notin e$ applies $\Delta^L_{h_i e_i}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Properties of mixed differences]
\label{lem:mixed-differences}
\lean{Rd.aestronglyMeasurable_mixedDiff, Rd.integrable_mixedDiff, Rd.mixedDiff_congr_ae,
  Rd.eLpNorm_mixedDiff_le, Rd.mixedDiff_conv_ae, Rd.fwdDiff_single_tensorKernel,
  Rd.higherDiff_single_tensorKernel, Rd.mixedDiff_tensorKernel}
\leanok
\uses{def:mixed-differences, lem:rd-differences, def:tensor-kernel}
Mixed differences keep measurability and integrability, depend only on the class of $f$, satisfy
$\|\Delta^{L,e}_h f\|_p \leq 2^{L|e|} \|f\|_p$ and
$\Delta^{L,e}_h (K * f) = (\Delta^{L,e}_h K) * f$ almost everywhere, and act on tensor kernels
factorwise: $\Delta^{L,e}_h (\otimes K) = \otimes K'$ with $K'_i = \Delta^L_{h_i} K_i$ for
$i \in e$ and $K'_i = K_i$ otherwise.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Induction over $e$ with the one-dimensional properties (\Cref{lem:rd-differences}).
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Mixed moduli and the mixed Besov quasi-norm]
\label{def:mixed-moduli}
\lean{Rd.mixedModulus, Rd.eLpNorm_mixedDiff_le_mixedModulus, Rd.mixedModulus_mono,
  Rd.mixedModulus_empty, Rd.mixedModulus_add_le, Rd.mixedModulus_le, Rd.mixedModulus_congr_ae,
  Rd.mixedSupp, Rd.mixedSupp_zero, Rd.lpMixedModulus, Rd.lpMixedModulus_zero,
  Rd.lpMixedModulus_add_le, Rd.lpMixedModulus_le, Rd.lpMixedModulus_zero_left, Rd.mixedModNorm,
  Rd.enorm_le_mixedModNorm}
\leanok
\uses{lem:mixed-differences, def:mixed-lq}
The mixed modulus of smoothness is
$\Omega^e_L(f, t)_p = \sup \{\|\Delta^{L,e}_h f\|_p : |h_i| \leq t_i \text{ for } i \in e\}$. For
$k \in \N^d$ let $e(k) = \{i : k_i \neq 0\}$. The Besov quasi-norm of dominating mixed smoothness
by differences is $\|(2^{s|k|_1} \Omega^{e(k)}_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)_{k \in \N^d}\|_{\ell_q}$; its term
$k = 0$ is $\|f\|_p$. The mixed modulus increases in $t$, is subadditive, is at most
$2^{L|e|} \|f\|_p$, and depends only on the class of $f$.
\end{definition}

\section{Averaged mixed differences}

\begin{lemma}[Bounded polynomials in translations]
\label{lem:acts-bounded}
\lean{Rd.ActsBounded, Rd.ActsBounded.one, Rd.ActsBounded.single, Rd.ActsBounded.mono,
  Rd.ActsBounded.add, Rd.ActsBounded.mul, Rd.ActsBounded.smul, Rd.ActsBounded.neg,
  Rd.ActsBounded.sub, Rd.ActsBounded.pow, Rd.ActsBounded.prod, Rd.actsBounded_momentPoly,
  Rd.actsBounded_addPoly}
\leanok
\uses{def:mixed-differences}
An element $a$ of the algebra of translations acts boundedly with constant $C$ if $a G$ stays
measurable and $\|a G\|_p \leq C \|G\|_p$. Translations $c \, \delta_v$ act with $|c|$, and sums,
products and powers add and multiply the constants. In particular $1 - (-1)^L (Y - 1)^L$ and
$(-1)^L ((Y + 1)^L - 1)$ act with $2^L + 1$ for every translation $Y$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Translations preserve $L_p$ norms; the triangle inequality and composition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The moment kernel polynomial]
\label{lem:moment-polynomial}
\lean{Rd.sum_jacksonCoeff_smul_pow}
\leanok
\uses{def:moment-kernel}
In every commutative $\C$-algebra,
$\sum_{l=1}^L (-1)^{l+1} \binom{L}{l} Y^l = 1 - (-1)^L (Y - 1)^L$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The binomial theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Tensor products of dilated bumps average translates]
\label{thm:tensor-averaged-differences}
\lean{Rd.diffAct_prod_sum_pow_apply, Rd.conv_tensorKernel_sum_dilate_ae}
\leanok
\uses{lem:diagonal-substitution, lem:tensor-kernel-fubini, lem:acts-bounded}
Let $\kappa_i = \sum_{t \in T} q_i(t) \, g_{c_i/\nu(t)}$ with integers $\nu(t) \geq 1$, scales
$c_i > 0$ and an integrable $g$ on $\R$, and let $Y_i = \delta_{-z_i e_i / c_i}$. For $f \in L_p$
and almost every $x$,
\[
  ((\otimes\kappa) * f)(x) = \int (\otimes g)(z)
    \Bigl(\prod_i \textstyle\sum_t q_i(t) Y_i^{\nu(t)}\Bigr) f(x) \, dz .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
The product expands into the translates $f(x - (\nu(n_i) z_i / c_i)_i)$ with the coefficients
$\prod_i q_i(n_i)$, and each translate integrates to a convolution with an anisotropic dilation
of $\otimes g$ (\Cref{lem:diagonal-substitution}).
\end{proof}

\section{Mixed local means}

\begin{definition}[Mixed local means]
\label{def:mixed-local-means}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedKernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedDual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable_mixedKernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.integrable_mixedDual,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedLocalMean, Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedLocalMeansNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:local-means, def:tensor-kernel}
For a bump $g$ on $\R$ with the kernels $k_j$ and $\eta_j$ of \Cref{def:local-means}, the mixed
kernels are $k_k = \otimes_i k_{k_i}$ and $\eta_k = \otimes_i \eta_{k_i}$, $k \in \N^d$, the mixed
local means of $f$ are $k_k * f$, and the mixed local means quasi-norm is
$\|(2^{s|k|_1} \|k_k * f\|_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Local means kernels as sums of dilated bumps]
\label{lem:kernel-dilation-sum}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.lmExp, Rd.LocalMeansBump.lmCoeff, Rd.LocalMeansBump.one_le_lmExp,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.kernel_eq_sum_dilate, Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_lmCoeff_smul_pow}
\leanok
\uses{def:moment-kernel, lem:moment-polynomial}
With $a_l = (-1)^l \binom{L}{l+1}$, $k_0 = \sum_{l < L} a_l g_{1/(l+1)}$ and
$k_j = \sum_{l < L} a_l (g_{2^j/(l+1)} - g_{2^j/(2(l+1))})$ for $j \geq 1$. The corresponding
polynomials are $P(Y) = 1 - (-1)^L (Y - 1)^L$ and
$P(Y) - P(Y^2) = (Y - 1)^L (-1)^L ((Y + 1)^L - 1)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$m_c = \sum_l a_l g_{c/(l+1)}$ and $g_{2^j/(2(l+1))} = g_{2^{j-1}/(l+1)}$; the factorisation from
$Y^2 - 1 = (Y - 1)(Y + 1)$ and \Cref{lem:moment-polynomial}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Mixed local means are bounded by the mixed modulus]
\label{thm:mixed-local-means-upper}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.lmRemainder, Rd.LocalMeansBump.prod_sum_lmCoeff_eq,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.actsBounded_lmRemainder, Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedLocalMean_ae_eq,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_mixedLocalMean_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedLocalMeansNorm_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:kernel-dilation-sum, thm:tensor-averaged-differences, lem:acts-bounded,
  lem:weighted-jensen, def:mixed-moduli}
If the bump vanishes outside $[-1, 1]$, then
$\|k_k * f\|_p \leq (\|g\|_1 (2^L + 1))^d \, \Omega^{e(k)}_L(f, 2^{-k})_p$. Hence the mixed local
means quasi-norm is at most $(\|g\|_1 (2^L + 1))^d$ times the quasi-norm by differences.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
By \Cref{lem:kernel-dilation-sum} and \Cref{thm:tensor-averaged-differences},
\[
  (k_k * f)(x) = \int (\otimes g)(z) \, B(z) \, \Delta^{L, e(k)}_{h(z)} f(x) \, dz
\]
with $h(z)_i = -z_i 2^{-k_i}$, so $|h(z)_i| \leq 2^{-k_i}$ on the support of $\otimes g$, and with
polynomials $B(z)$ in the translations that act with constant $(2^L + 1)^d$
(\Cref{lem:acts-bounded}). Minkowski's inequality (\Cref{lem:weighted-jensen}) concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The mixed reproducing formula]
\label{lem:mixed-reproducing}
\lean{Rd.conv_dilate_dilate, Rd.convLp_congr, Rd.convLp_finsetSum_kernel, Rd.conv_add_conv_sub,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_conv_dual_kernel, Rd.LocalMeansBump.sum_conv_mixedDual_mixedKernel,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.integral_conv_momentKernel,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.tendsto_sum_mixedDual_mixedLocalMean}
\leanok
\uses{lem:reproducing-formula, lem:reproducing-pointwise, lem:tensor-kernel-fubini,
  thm:approx-identity}
In one variable $\sum_{j \leq J} \eta_j * k_j = m_{2^J} * m_{2^J}$ at every point. Over the boxes
$\{k : k_i \leq J\}$, $\sum_k \eta_k * k_k = (\otimes (m * m))_{2^J}$, and
$\sum_k \eta_k * k_k * f \to f$ in $L_p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$(a + b) * (a - b) = a * a - b * b$ for the commuting continuous kernels with compact support;
tensor products and dilations commute with convolution (\Cref{lem:tensor-kernel-fubini}), and
$\otimes (m * m)$ has integral $1$, so \Cref{thm:approx-identity} applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Mixed moduli of the reproducing terms]
\label{lem:mixed-dual-moduli}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.lpMixedModulus_convLp_mixedDual_le}
\leanok
\uses{lem:dual-kernel-moduli, lem:mixed-differences, lem:tensor-kernel-fubini}
$\Omega^{e(m)}_L(\eta_k * h, 2^{-m})_p \leq C_\eta^d \prod_i c(m_i, k_i) \, \|h\|_p$ with the
coefficients $c$ of \Cref{lem:mixed-almost-diagonal}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\Delta^{L, e(m)}_v (\eta_k * h) = (\Delta^{L, e(m)}_v \eta_k) * h$, a convolution with a tensor
product whose factors have $L_1$ norms at most $C_\eta c(m_i, k_i)$
(\Cref{lem:dual-kernel-moduli}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Mixed local means and mixed differences]
\label{thm:mixed-local-means}
\lean{ENNReal.le_tsum_of_tendsto_finsetSum, Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedModNorm_le_of_tendsto,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedModNorm_le_mixedLocalMeansNorm,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.mixedBesov_iff_mixedLocalMeans}
\leanok
\uses{thm:mixed-local-means-upper, lem:mixed-reproducing, lem:mixed-dual-moduli,
  lem:mixed-almost-diagonal}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < L$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, and let the bump vanish outside
$[-1, 1]$. Then $\|(2^{s|k|_1} \Omega^{e(k)}_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and only if
$\|(2^{s|k|_1} \|k_k * f\|_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$, with equivalent quasi-norms.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
One direction is \Cref{thm:mixed-local-means-upper}. Conversely
$f = \sum_k \eta_k * (k_k * f)$ (\Cref{lem:mixed-reproducing}), and the mixed modulus is
subadditive and bounded on $L_p$, so
$\Omega^{e(m)}_L(f, 2^{-m})_p \leq C_\eta^d \sum_k \prod_i c(m_i, k_i) \|k_k * f\|_p$
(\Cref{lem:mixed-dual-moduli}); \Cref{lem:mixed-almost-diagonal} sums over $k$.
\end{proof}

\section{Tensor Littlewood--Paley blocks}

\begin{definition}[Tensor Littlewood--Paley blocks]
\label{def:mixed-lp}
\lean{Rd.mixedPsi, Rd.mixedTheta, Rd.integrable_mixedPsi, Rd.integrable_mixedTheta,
  Rd.conv_mixedTheta_mixedPsi, Rd.sum_mixedPsi, Rd.mixedBlock, Rd.mixedLPNorm,
  Rd.tendsto_sum_mixedBlock}
\leanok
\uses{def:lp-kernels, thm:kernel-identities, lem:tensor-kernel-fubini, thm:approx-identity}
The tensor blocks are $\delta_k = \otimes_i \psi_{k_i}$, so that
$\delta_k * f = \mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_k \mathcal{F} f)$ for the tensor decomposition of unity
$\varphi_k = \otimes_i \mathcal{F}\psi_{k_i}$. Vyb\'{\i}ral's quasi-norm is
$\|(2^{s|k|_1} \|\delta_k * f\|_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$. The blocks satisfy
$(\otimes_i \Theta_{k_i}) * \delta_k = \delta_k$, sum over the boxes $\{k : k_i \leq J\}$ to
$(\otimes \Phi)_{2^J}$, and $\sum_k \delta_k * f = f$ in $L_p$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[One-dimensional estimates in $L_1$]
\label{lem:mixed-lp-kernels}
\lean{Rd.eLpNorm_conv_eq_enorm_convLp, Rd.one_le_dyadicWeight_mul_almostDiagCoeff,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.eLpNorm_kernel_one_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_eLpNorm_conv_psi_dual_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_eLpNorm_conv_kernel_Theta_le}
\leanok
\uses{thm:band-jackson, thm:kernel-identities, lem:local-means-upper, thm:band-inverse,
  lem:dual-kernel-moduli}
There is $C < \infty$ with $\|\psi_m * \eta_k\|_1 \leq C c(m, k)$ and
$\|k_m * \Theta_k\|_1 \leq C c(m, k)$ for all $m, k \in \N$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
For $k + 3 \leq m$, $\psi_m$ annihilates the band-limited Jackson approximant $J_{m-3} \eta_k$
(\Cref{thm:kernel-identities}), so
$\|\psi_m * \eta_k\|_1 \leq \|\psi_m\|_1 \|\eta_k - J_{m-3} \eta_k\|_1
\leq C \omega_L(\eta_k, 2^{-(m-3)})_1$ (\Cref{thm:band-jackson}), and
\Cref{lem:dual-kernel-moduli} bounds the modulus. For $k < m$,
$\|k_m * \Theta_k\|_1 \leq C \omega_L(\Theta_k, 2^{1-m})_1$ (\Cref{lem:local-means-upper}), and the
differences of $\Theta_k$ give the decay (\Cref{thm:band-inverse}). In the remaining cases Young's
inequality suffices.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Littlewood--Paley blocks, local means and differences]
\label{thm:mixed-lp}
\lean{Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_mixedBlock_convLp_mixedDual_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.enorm_mixedLocalMean_mixedBlock_le, Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_mixedLPNorm_le,
  Rd.LocalMeansBump.exists_mixedLocalMeansNorm_le, Rd.mixedBesov_iff_mixedLP}
\leanok
\uses{def:mixed-lp, lem:mixed-lp-kernels, lem:mixed-reproducing, thm:mixed-local-means,
  lem:mixed-almost-diagonal, lem:bumps-every-radius}
Let $1 \leq p < \infty$, $0 < s < L$ and $1 \leq q \leq \infty$. Then
$\|(2^{s|k|_1} \Omega^{e(k)}_L(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$ if and only if
$\|(2^{s|k|_1} \|\delta_k * f\|_p)_k\|_{\ell_q} < \infty$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\delta_m * f = \sum_k (\delta_m * \eta_k) * (k_k * f)$ and
$k_m * f = \sum_k (k_m * \otimes_i \Theta_{k_i}) * (\delta_k * f)$, and the tensor kernels have
$L_1$ norms at most $C^d \prod_i c(m_i, k_i)$ (\Cref{lem:mixed-lp-kernels}), so
\Cref{lem:mixed-almost-diagonal} compares Vyb\'{\i}ral's quasi-norm with the mixed local means.
\Cref{thm:mixed-local-means} for the centred bump of radius $1$ concludes.
\end{proof}

\section{Hyperbolic crosses}

\begin{definition}[Levels and hyperbolic crosses]
\label{def:hyperbolic-cross}
\lean{Rd.level, Rd.le_level, Rd.levelSet, Rd.mem_levelSet, Rd.mixedWeight_eq_dyadicWeight_level,
  Rd.hyperbolicCross, Rd.mem_hyperbolicCross, Rd.hyperbolicSum}
\leanok
\uses{def:mixed-lp}
The level of $k \in \N^d$ is $|k|_1 = \sum_i k_i$, the hyperbolic cross of level $N$ is
$\{k : |k|_1 \leq N\}$, and $S_N f = \sum_{|k|_1 \leq N} \delta_k * f$ is band-limited to the union
of the dyadic rectangles of these blocks.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Counting and Hölder's inequality on levels]
\label{lem:level-count}
\lean{Rd.card_levelSet_le, Rd.tsum_le_tsum_levelSet, Rd.sum_le_card_rpow_mul_lqNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:hyperbolic-cross, def:mixed-lq}
$\#\{k \in \N^d : |k|_1 = n\} \leq (n + 1)^{d-1}$; sums over $\N^d$ are at most the sums over the
levels of the sums on each level; and $\sum_{k \in S} x_k \leq (\# S)^{1 - 1/q} \|x\|_{\ell_q}$ for
finite $S$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
An index of level $n$ is determined by its first $d - 1$ coordinates; Hölder's inequality on $S$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The rate series]
\label{lem:rate-series}
\lean{Rd.tsum_dyadicWeight_mul_rpow_ne_top, Rd.tsum_tail_le}
\leanok
For $s > 0$ and $a \geq 0$, $C = \sum_l 2^{-sl} (l + 1)^a < \infty$ and
$\sum_{n > N} 2^{-sn} (n + 1)^a \leq C \, 2^{-sN} (N + 1)^a$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$(l + 1)^a \leq (l + 1)^{\lceil a \rceil}$ and $\sum_l l^K r^l < \infty$ for $r < 1$;
$n + 1 \leq (n - N + 1)(N + 1)$ for $n \geq N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Hyperbolic cross approximation]
\label{thm:hyperbolic-cross}
\lean{Rd.enorm_sub_hyperbolicSum_le, Rd.enorm_sub_hyperbolicSum_le_mixedLPNorm}
\leanok
\uses{def:hyperbolic-cross, lem:level-count, lem:rate-series, def:mixed-lp}
For $s > 0$, $1 \leq q \leq \infty$ and $N \in \N$,
\[
  \|f - S_N f\|_p \leq C \, 2^{-sN} (N + 1)^{(d-1)(1 - 1/q)} \,
    \|(2^{s|k|_1} \|\delta_k * f\|_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}
\]
with the constant $C$ of \Cref{lem:rate-series} for $a = (d - 1)(1 - 1/q)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
$\|f - S_N f\|_p \leq \sum_{|k|_1 > N} \|\delta_k * f\|_p$, since $\sum_k \delta_k * f = f$. On the
level $n$, Hölder's inequality costs $(n + 1)^{(d-1)(1 - 1/q)}$ (\Cref{lem:level-count}), and
\Cref{lem:rate-series} sums over $n > N$.
\end{proof}

\chapter{The representations of Besov spaces}
\label{chap:representations}

The earlier chapters describe Besov spaces in many ways and prove that the descriptions agree.
This chapter collects them: what each description measures (\Cref{sec:descriptions}), in which
setting and for which parameters it is proved to give the Besov space (\Cref{sec:where}), and how
the descriptions differ (\Cref{sec:differences}). Throughout $1 \leq q \leq \infty$, and
$1 \leq p < \infty$ unless stated otherwise. Two descriptions agree when the same functions have
a finite quasi-norm; most theorems moreover bound each quasi-norm by a constant times the other.
The dimension of the underlying space is $n$.

\section{The descriptions}
\label{sec:descriptions}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{Approximation} (\Cref{def:approx-space,def:dyadic-norm}).
    $\|f\| + \|(2^{ks} E_{2^k}(f))_k\|_{\ell_q}$, where $E_m(f)$ is the error of best
    approximation of $f$ by the $m$-th set of a scheme: trigonometric polynomials, band-limited
    functions, piecewise polynomials, splines, step functions, or combinations of $m$ elements of a
    dictionary.
  \item \emph{Differences} (\Cref{def:rd-modulus,def:rd-modulus-on};
    \Cref{def:modulus,def:interval-modulus,def:iso-modulus,def:cube-modulus}).
    $\|f\|_p + \|(2^{ks} \omega_r(f, 2^{-k})_p)_k\|_{\ell_q}$ with a modulus of smoothness of
    order $r$ on a group such as $\R^n$ or $\T^n$, or on a set $\Omega$, where only the
    differences with all their points in $\Omega$ count. The moduli on the torus and on an interval
    and the isotropic modulus on the cube are of this form; the moduli along the coordinates of the
    cube take differences in one coordinate at a time.
  \item \emph{Littlewood--Paley blocks} (\Cref{def:lp-blocks}).
    $\|(2^{js} \|\psi_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$, where the Fourier transforms of the kernels
    $\psi_j$ form a smooth dyadic decomposition of unity. For $p = 2$ on the torus the sharp
    dyadic blocks of the Fourier coefficients serve as well:
    \[
      |\hat f(0)| + \Bigl\|\Bigl(2^{ks} \Bigl(\sum_{2^k \leq \deg m < 2^{k+1}} |\hat f(m)|^2
        \Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr)_k\Bigr\|_{\ell_q},
      \qquad \deg m = \max_i |m_i| .
    \]
  \item \emph{Local means} (\Cref{def:local-means}).
    $\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_p)_j\|_{\ell_q}$ with kernels $k_j$ supported in balls of radius
    $C 2^{-j}$, all built from one bump.
  \item \emph{Atoms} (\Cref{def:atomic-system,def:regular-atoms}).
    Decompositions into atoms $a_{j,m}$ of level $j$, supported near a cube of side $2^{-j}$,
    $f = \sum_j \sum_m \lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m}$, measured by
    \[
      \|\lambda\|_{b^s_{p,q}} = \bigl\|\bigl(2^{j(s - n/p)} \|\lambda_{j,\cdot}\|_{\ell_p}\bigr)_j
        \bigr\|_{\ell_q} .
    \]
  \item \emph{Coefficients} (\Cref{def:haar,def:multiwavelets,def:spectral}).
    Weighted $\ell_q(\ell_p)$ norms of the coefficients of $f$ in a basis, level by level: Haar
    functions, multiwavelets, the Fourier basis, a Hilbert basis of a spectral scheme.
  \item \emph{Blocks} (\Cref{def:block-decomposition,def:blocks}).
    Decompositions $f = \sum_k g_k$ with $g_k$ in the $2^k$-th set of the scheme, measured by
    $\|(2^{ks} \|g_k\|)_k\|_{\ell_q}$; for a scheme with uniformly bounded projections $P_m$, the
    dyadic blocks $P_{2^{k+1}} f - P_{2^k} f$, among them martingale differences.
  \item \emph{Interpolation} (\Cref{def:k-functional,def:interpolation}).
    The real interpolation space $(X, Y)_{s/r, q}$ between $X$ and a space $Y$ of smoothness $r$,
    defined through the K-functional.
\end{enumerate}

On a domain $\Omega$ two further quasi-norms enter: the \emph{restriction} quasi-norm, the
infimum of a quasi-norm on $\R^n$ over all extensions of $f$, and the \emph{intrinsic local
means} $\|(2^{js} \|k_j * f\|_{L_p(\Omega)})_j\|_{\ell_q}$ (\Cref{def:intrinsic-local-means}).
Spaces of \emph{dominating mixed smoothness} replace the level $k \in \N$ by $k \in \N^n$, the
weight $2^{ks}$ by $2^{s|k|_1}$, and every kernel by a tensor product
(\Cref{def:mixed-moduli,def:mixed-local-means,def:mixed-lp}).

\section{Where each description is proved}
\label{sec:where}

Each table lists the descriptions that are shown to agree in one setting, the smoothness range of
the statement, and the result. A range $0 \leq s < \theta$ or $0 < s < \theta$ is the range of
the cited statement; outside it the descriptions may differ (\Cref{sec:differences}).

\paragraph{Abstract schemes.} Here $A$ is an approximation scheme in a normed space $X$.

\raggedright Approximation and interpolation, under Jackson's and Bernstein's inequalities of order $r$
  & $0 < s < r$ & \Cref{thm:representation}\newline \Cref{cor:equal-spaces} \\
\raggedright Approximation and moduli, under Jackson's and the inverse inequality of order $\theta$
  & $0 \leq s < \theta$ & \Cref{thm:modulus-coincidence} \\
\raggedright Approximation and blocks & $s > 0$ & \Cref{thm:block-decomposition} \\
\raggedright Approximation and dyadic blocks of projections & $s > 0$ & \Cref{thm:blocks} \\
\raggedright Approximation and martingale differences in $L_p$ & $s > 0$ & \Cref{thm:martingale} \\
\raggedright Approximation and atoms of decay $\sigma$ & $0 < s < \sigma$
  & \Cref{thm:atomic-synthesis}\newline \Cref{thm:atomic-analysis} \\
\raggedright Approximation and coefficients in a Hilbert space & $s > 0$
  & \Cref{thm:spectral-coefficients} \\
\raggedright Intrinsic approximation and restrictions, given an extension & $s \geq 0$
  & \Cref{thm:retract} \\
\hline

\end{center}

\paragraph{The torus $\T^n$.} The scheme consists of the trigonometric polynomials.

\raggedright Trigonometric approximation and smooth Littlewood--Paley blocks, $1 \leq p < \infty$

\section{How the descriptions differ}
\label{sec:differences}

\paragraph{Linear and nonlinear.} The approximation quasi-norm measures best approximation, and a
best approximation need not depend linearly on $f$, although $A^s_q$ is a linear space and $A_m$
may be a nonlinear set, as for combinations of $m$ dictionary elements. Littlewood--Paley blocks,
local means, projections, martingale differences and coefficients in a basis are linear in $f$,
and the coefficients in a basis are unique. Decompositions into blocks or atoms are not unique,
and their quasi-norm is an infimum over all decompositions. The decompositions constructed here
come from near-best approximations in the abstract setting (\Cref{thm:atomic-analysis}) and from
the local means on $\R^n$ (\Cref{thm:atomic-decomposition}), where every term
$\lambda_{j,m} a_{j,m} = \eta_j * (1_{P_{j,m}} (k_j * f))$ is linear in $f$.

\paragraph{The range of smoothness.} Every description detects smoothness only up to a bound.
Differences of order $r$ detect $s < r$, and local means and atoms of order $L$ detect $s < L$.
Littlewood--Paley blocks and Fourier coefficients have no such bound. The approximation space of a scheme is defined for every $s$, and it is the Besov
space below the inverse order $\theta$ of the scheme: $\theta = 1/p$ for piecewise polynomials and
step functions, $\theta = r - 1 + 1/p$ for splines of order $r$, and every $\theta$ for
band-limited functions (\Cref{thm:rd-besov}). A scheme of piecewise polynomials contains
functions with jumps, and these lie in its approximation spaces for every $s$ but in no Besov
space of smoothness $s > 1/p$.

\paragraph{The order.} For $0 < s < r$ the differences of order $r$ give the band-limited
approximation space on $\R^n$ (\Cref{thm:rd-besov}), so the spaces defined by differences of any
order $r > s$ coincide. The same holds for local means of any order $L > s$
(\Cref{thm:local-means}) and their mixed analogues (\Cref{thm:mixed-lp}).

\paragraph{Fourier analysis and $p$.} Fourier coefficients describe the spaces only for $p = 2$,
through Parseval's identity (\Cref{thm:torus-coefficients,thm:spectral-coefficients}). For other
$p$ the sharp dyadic blocks are replaced by the smooth kernels $\psi_j$. The proofs here need
only integrable kernels, Young's inequality and the kernel identities
(\Cref{thm:young,thm:kernel-identities}). On the torus the kernels of $\R^n$ act by convolution
with their periodisations, and their Fourier transforms at the lattice points are Fourier
multipliers; this transfers Jackson's and Bernstein's inequalities to every $L_p(\T^n)$
(\Cref{thm:torus-econv-fourier,thm:torus-lp-moduli}), and the smooth blocks $\psi_j * f$
describe $\Besov{s}{p}{q}(\T^n)$ for every $1 \leq p < \infty$ (\Cref{thm:torus-lp-besov}).

\paragraph{Coordinates, directions and mixed smoothness.} On the cube the coordinate moduli, with
differences along one coordinate at a time, and the isotropic modulus, with differences in every
direction, give the same spaces (\Cref{thm:iso-besov}). Dominating mixed smoothness measures
differences in several coordinates at once, $\Delta^{L,e}_h$, with the weight $2^{s|k|_1}$ for
$k \in \N^n$; its Littlewood--Paley blocks are tensor products, and its natural approximation is by
sums over the hyperbolic crosses $\{|k|_1 \leq N\}$, with the rate
$2^{-sN} (N + 1)^{(n-1)(1-1/q)}$ (\Cref{thm:hyperbolic-cross}).

\paragraph{Domains.} On a domain $\Omega$ the intrinsic differences use only the values of $f$ in
$\Omega$, the restriction quasi-norm uses extensions to $\R^n$, and the intrinsic local means use
the values in $\Omega$ through kernels supported in a cone. On Lipschitz domains they agree, by
Rychkov's extension operator (\Cref{thm:graph-extension}), which is linear and is one operator for
all $0 < s < L$, $p$ and $q$; on bounded Lipschitz domains the graph domains are glued by a
partition of unity and pointwise multipliers (\Cref{thm:multipliers,thm:local-extension}).

\paragraph{Traces.} Restriction to a hypersurface costs the smoothness $1/p$. On the torus every
$s > 0$ is allowed (\Cref{thm:trace-torus}). On the boundary of a Lipschitz domain the Besov space
is defined in charts by differences of order $1$, the order that a Lipschitz change of variables
preserves, and the trace theorem holds for $0 < s < 1$
(\Cref{thm:graph-trace-space,thm:boundary-trace-space}). Smooth changes of charts keep
differences of every order, so on the half-space and on compact manifolds with boundary the trace
theorem holds for every $s > 0$ (\Cref{thm:half-space-trace-space,thm:manifold-trace}).
